GroupTrack — LIVING CHECKLIST — kick-the-can issues, tied to a release
v1 · 2026-11-14 · companion to grouptrack_living_master_2026-11-14_v238.html

⭐⭐⭐ THE TWO-PLATFORM NAV XREF DIFF — A STANDING TOOL, NOT A ONE-OFF (C28-26377)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8b C28-26377 · XREFDRIFT — so the two apps cannot grow apart unnoticed

Fred 11-21 21:27: “the navxref compari[son] is something I think we need in the toolkit going forward just to track any code d[iff]erences so we do not have th[e] apps grow apart unintentionally.”

WHAT IT IS, and it is not a new tool. navxref_2026-11-14_v2.py already parses a COMPOSE CLICK TREE — every clickable { } with its nearest label and the action it runs. The iPad's UI is Compose too. The parser's targets are a three-line list at the top of the file:

ROOTS = ["app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy", ... ]

Point that same parser at grouptrack-shared/src/iosMain + commonMain and it emits an iOS click tree in the identical format. Two files, same shape, and the diff is then mechanical — row by row, label by label, action by action. A ROOTS change and a second output file; a v3 of the proven tool, not a second tool.

⚠ WHY IT MUST BE THE SAME PARSER. Five attempts at a separate iOS xref were made on 11-21 and every one was retired with its defect in its own filename:

attemptwhy it was retired
iosxref_v1_DO_NOT_RUN_window_not_block
iosxref_v2_DO_NOT_RUN_read_comments_as_code
iosxref_v3_DO_NOT_RUN_swallowed_nameerror
iosxref_v4_DO_NOT_RUN_one_platform_only
iosxref_v5_HELD_android_column_is_a_grep — Fred's rule, applied to my own tool

⭐ The lesson in one line: the iOS side was never what failed. The FAKED ANDROID COLUMN was. A comparison is only worth having if BOTH columns come from the same parser reading real code — which is precisely what reusing navxref guarantees and what a second tool cannot.

⚠ CARRY THE TOOL'S KNOWN LIMITS WITH IT (C28-26370 NAVXREFLIMIT). Measured on the radio row alone: one clickable reports “no action found”, and apply() — the point of the whole screen — is reported under the label “Back” (the enclosing-label defect). It also sees 0 dispatch ids and 0 grouptrack-shared mentions. So the diff is a CHANGE DETECTOR, not a verdict: it says “these two rows differ, go look”, and the hand walk still decides what the difference means.

WHEN IT RUNS. With the daily xref regeneration, so a drift that appears today is seen today rather than at the next walk of that letter. Its output belongs beside navigation_xref_2026-11-14.txt, which recommit already rebuilds (gen_xrefs_v3.sh).

⭐⭐⭐ THE iOS SERVICE LAYER HAS NO HOLE — AND THE INDEX SAYS IT DOES (C28-26375)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8b C28-26375 · SVCINDEXBLIND — never judge absence from the index's shape column

Fred 11-21 20:56: “and how are you determining the iOS seam has not been accommodated yet[?] every radio seam existed after you said it did not.”

MEASURED 11-21 20:56, 170 services:

shape, per the indexcountthe truth
PURE SHARED131one body, both platforms — already running on iOS
CONTRACT, implemented in iosMain (+ platform parts, + expect/actual)21the index links these
CONTRACT, reported with NO iOS implementation18⚠ 17 ARE IMPLEMENTED. Only LineSource has no object — and its four atoms ARE GtSpatial.queryRoutes/queryTrails/queryTracks, which the iPad's map already calls every viewport.

THE CAUSE. The index links an iOS implementation by naming convention. Anything written under a different class name reads as a gap: GtArtifactSink → GtIosArtifactSink.kt, GtRecipeEnv → GtIosRecipeEnv.kt, RideFiles/RideNet/RideNetworks/RideSpatial → GtIosRideFiles.kt / GtIosRideNetworks.kt / GtIosRideSpatial.kt, GtHkEnv/GtHkDb → GtIosHkEnv.kt, GtSettingsStore/Editor → GtIosSettings.kt, GtOsmPlatform → GtIosOsmPlatform.kt, GtQueuePlatform → GtIosRecipeEnv.kt, GtQueueRow → GtIosValues.kt, GtImportPlatform → GtIosImportPlatform.kt, GtScreenCapture → GtIosScreenCapture.kt, GtTileStoreEnv / GtImportEnv → GtIosEnv.kt.

⭐ THE STATE, IN THREE NUMBERS: 131 pure shared · 37 contracts with iOS implementations · 0 genuine absences. Porting is a LOOKUP. The service exists, under a name that is not Android's.

⚠ THE RULE. svcref --find is a QUERY TOOL, not a coverage report. It may be used to FIND a service; it may never be used to conclude one is absent. To establish absence: query by capability and synonym, then grep iosMain directly, then read the comm API, then check what Android calls at the same hop. This is the same failure as trusting a stale code comment, one layer up — and it produced eighteen phantom gaps in a single reading.

⚠ AND J IS NOT THE EXCEPTION IT WAS EXPECTED TO BE. Every row of the J map menu maps to a shared service with an iOS implementation already written — GtMapRecipe, GtMBTiles, GtVectorQueue, GtOsm*, GtStateDataRun, GtGpxImport, GtImportConverter, GtArtifactFiles, GtWrite, GtRead — including the internet fetch (GtHttp → IosHttp, GtIosHttp.kt:139). Full row-by-row table in GroupTrack_2.8b_launch_surface_model_and_schedule_2026-11-21_v2.html.

⭐⭐⭐ THE AI BUILD ROUTE IS THE CROSS-PLATFORM PROOF (C28-26376)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8b C28-26376 · AIROUTECERT — one test that exercises the math, the data and the port at once

Fred 11-21 21:05: “ai build route is an incredible test for the accuracy of our math, our trail network being discovered and route be[ing] created identically across the three platforms.”

WHY IT IS THE STRONGEST SINGLE TEST WE HAVE. Most comparisons check one value. A route build exercises a whole chain and produces an artifact that can be compared exactly:

  • the math — distance, bearing, scoring, the RideCalc calibration;
  • the trail network discovery — which trails are found, in what order, and how they connect;
  • the route itself — a geometry that can be compared byte for byte across the three devices.

Run under C28-26374's conditions: the same database on 2.7f (Droid 2), 2.8b (Droid 1) and 2.8b (iPad/sim), the same start and end, the same settings. Identical input, identical geometry, or the difference is ours. And it needs no radio, so unlike the radio surfaces it certifies 2↔3 on the simulator — which is exactly why it belongs in the sim-model milestone.

⚠ It is also the natural check on the DB-contained functions Fred named in the same message: the sim model can exercise database access and everything that lives inside the database completely, because none of it needs Bluetooth.

⭐⭐⭐ UNIT TESTING IS A THREE-WAY SIDE-BY-SIDE, FROM ONE DATABASE (C28-26374)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8b C28-26374 · UNITTEST3WAY — what certifies a function, after it builds

Fred 11-21 20:29: “same standard as today, iOS build and compiles using service layer repairs. unit testing of each function is the follow on. unit testing is the side by side comparison of function on 2.7f (droid2) 2.8b (droid1) and sim/Ipad running 2.8b iPad version. that should be the unit testing exercise on living doc[.] each device has the same db as a starting point.”

THE STANDARD, IN TWO STAGES. Today's standard is stage one and does not change: walk the call stack, repair the seam with the service layer, and get a green compile on BOTH targets plus the iOS build. That is not certification. Stage two is the unit test, and it is a three-way side-by-side comparison of the same function:

#devicebuildwhat it is
1Droid 22.7fthe BASELINE — the behaviour before the service layer moved under it
2Droid 12.8bthe same platform, now running on the shared services
3iPad / sim2.8b iPadthe port

⭐⭐ WHY THREE AND NOT TWO — IT IS TWO INDEPENDENT COMPARISONS IN ONE EXERCISE, and that is what makes a failure diagnosable:

pairagreement provesa difference means
1 ↔ 2  (2.7f Droid 2 ↔ 2.8b Droid 1)the service-layer move did not change Android's behavioura REGRESSION we introduced in the shared layer — fix the service, not the port
2 ↔ 3  (2.8b Droid 1 ↔ 2.8b iPad)both platforms run the same process on the same servicesa PORT GAP on iOS — a missing seam, a missing branch, or an invented behaviour

With only pair 2↔3 a shared-layer regression and a port gap look identical, and the wrong half gets repaired. 1↔2 is what tells them apart.

⚠ THE PRECONDITION, AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL: ONE DATABASE UNDER ALL THREE. Fred: “each device has the same db as a starting point.” Without it a difference in output can be a difference in DATA, and the comparison proves nothing. The baseline already exists and is already the sim's seed: _data/ref/droid2_full_2026-11-19_1509 — Droid 2's whole GroupTrack directory, pulled 11-19 and verified clean (routes 23, tracks 136, waypoints 599, trails 229,363; no raw or shape duplicates). It carries more than the two databases on purpose — tiles, map_keys.json, map_slots.json, state json — because a map filtered differently on one device is the same class of false difference.

⭐ This also means the DB is the control, not a variable. Measured 11-21 while chasing the no-trails regression: that same database answers queryTrails with a full 1000-row page and 199,494 of 229,363 rows pass the rider's real filter. So when a function differs across the three devices, the database has already been eliminated — which is exactly what made the 11-21 diagnosis land on the display gate rather than the data.

⭐⭐ THE ORDER OF THE EXERCISE — WALK THE MENUS, A TO J. Fred 11-21 20:31: “we will walk the menus when testing just like when building[,] starting at a and ending at j.” The test is not a list of functions picked by risk; it is the SAME walk as the build, in the SAME order, on three devices at once. A launch point is reached the way a rider reaches it, and each function is compared across the three as it comes up. Build order and test order are one order, so a function certified in the test is the function that was just repaired, and nothing is reached by a route no rider takes.

WHAT THE EXERCISE PRODUCES. One row per function, three columns, the difference NAMED — the ROWDIFF-2026-11-20 output format, extended by one column. A function is certified when columns 1, 2 and 3 agree, or when a difference is explained by a platform capability that is written down. A green compile on three targets is not a row in this table.

⚠ THE ONE THING THIS EXERCISE CANNOT DO, said here so it is not discovered later: the radio. Droid 1 and Droid 2 have radios; the simulator has none, and GtIosCommConfig returns NotSupported by design. So every radio function certifies 1↔2 only, and 2↔3 waits for the sideloaded iPad with a radio (NOSIMRADIO-2026-11-18). Do not read a NotSupported on the sim as a port gap.

⭐⭐ A STUB'S OWN TEXT IS NOT EVIDENCE ABOUT THE SERVICE (C28-26373)

⭐⭐ 2.8b C28-26373 · STUBLIES — the recorder was built on both platforms and the stub said it wasn't

Fred 11-21 14:41: “so this is exactly the case I am worried about[:] a stubbed service will function never called but [the] function was corrected and built for both platforms already[.] great catch.”

B2·REC's iPad call site (GtIosPanels.kt:1266-1269) sets RecordingState.RECORDING and reports “recording (no iOS recorder yet -- nothing is written)”. That text was written 11-19 and was true then. The recorder is now shared and built: MyCartProcess (track/TrackRules.kt:578), TrackWriter (:303), ScreenWriter (:453), LeadCartProcess (:481), GtTracks (track/GtTracks.kt:65), and RecordingState itself — all commonMain. The iPad's position feed is built too (GtIosLocation, already driving the map and HUD). Nothing in iosMain calls the recorder, so a finished cross-platform function reads as a missing feature.

⏻ THE RULE. A stub's message describes the STUB, not the world. It is a claim made on the day it was written and nothing ever updates it. Never take a stub's text, or a TODO, or a “not built yet” comment, as evidence that a service is absent — query the service layer (svcref --find) and read the function. This is SVCSTALE in its most dangerous form: earlier the stale claim sat in a card, here it sits in the code's own output where it looks authoritative.

⏻ AND THE INSTRUMENT HAS THE SAME LIMIT. iosxref's WORK / REPORT verdicts grade the CALL SITE. REPORT means “this site does not act”; it never means “the function does not exist.” Both halves must be read together: the site from the xref, the capability from the service index.

CONSEQUENCE FOR B2, measured: the row is wiring, not building — GtIosLocation → MyCartProcess → TrackWriter → GtTracks, every piece already certified. GtBackgroundLocation is NOT B2's blocker; it is needed only for recording while the iPad is locked or backgrounded, which is separable.

⏻ THE AUDIT THIS IMPLIES, and it is bounded: there are 19 distinct onMessage names (C28-26371), and several carry text asserting something “is not built on the iPad yet.” Each such claim is dated and may now be false exactly as B2's was. Every one must be checked against the service layer before it is believed or priced.

⏻⏻⏻ THE iPAD ANNOUNCES 19 JOBS IT DOES NOT PERFORM (C28-26371)

⏻⏻⏻ 2.8b C28-26371 · ONMESSAGE-2026-11-21 — walked the FILTER process cradle to grave; it ends in a log line

Fred 11-21 13:38: “Your attacking a list, not a process. We are handwalking every process that executes... we are reviewing every process from cradle to grave.” He was right, and walking one process properly found what the list never would.

H·FILTER, THE WHOLE PROCESS, ON THE iPAD:

  1. Rider taps a filter tick → GtFilterBody (FilterModule.kt:72) — shared, byte-for-byte the same code Android runs, reached from GroupTrack.kt:318 via openModule.id == "filter". Both hosts call GroupTrackLaunchLayer (Android GroupTrackScreen.kt:3055, iPad GtIosPanels.kt:1322). This part is genuinely MATCHED.
  2. → GtFilterBridge.setState, the iPad's own construction (GtIosPanels.kt:951): sets slot(t).intValue. Local state is kept, so the tick moves and stays moved.
  3. → js(viewportJs). Measured, not assumed: viewportJs (:692) is map.getBounds() + getZoom() reported back through Android.onViewportChanged. It carries no display state whatever.
  4. → onMessage("setDisplay", "Trails=1") → GroupTrackPanels.swift:48-50 → log.add(name, body) → a line in the HUD log. THE MAP IS NEVER TOLD.

The Swift host says so itself (GroupTrackPanels.swift:14-18): “The status strip is the CERTIFICATION INSTRUMENT here, not decoration. Every launch point whose function is a map-side job it does not have yet reports through onMessage... the strip shows that a control FIRED even when the map cannot yet answer it.” So this is deliberate scaffolding, honestly labelled — but it is scaffolding, and the record called it done.

⏻ MEASURED SCOPE: GtIosPanels.kt makes 43 onMessage sends under 19 distinct names. Both Swift hosts (GroupTrackPanels.swift:48, VecMapProbe.swift:36) route onMessage to log.add and nothing else. Not one of the 19 has a consumer. (Apparent hits in grouptrack_help_links.json, the manual, LaunchModules.kt and GtMapState.kt are false positives — help ids, row declarations and an unrelated field, not handlers.)

THE 19, AND THE LETTER EACH BELONGS TO: detail ×15 (readout) · record ×4 (B) · editDisplay ×3, setDisplay, hideEverything, offGridAvailable, offGridDownloading (H) · mode ×2 (C) · fit ×2, mapType (F) · centre (I) · group (D) · searchOpen, searchPlace (M) · gps, offline (E) · toggleView, profile, settings.

⏻⏻ WHAT THIS DOES TO THE DIFFERENCES DOC. C, D, F, I and H were recorded at “0 differences / MATCHED”. That verdict was panel-level: the panel is shared, the row is reached, the first hop is real. Cradle to grave they are not complete — each terminates in a report whose only consumer is a log line. MATCHED was never wrong about what it measured; it measured the wrong thing. The unit of certification is the process, not the row.

⚠ THE NEGATIVE WITNESS, which is why this survived five weeks: it compiles on both targets; the panel draws; the tick moves and stays moved; the state persists; the HUD shows the control fired. Everything an observer would check says working. The only thing that would reveal it is the map not changing — and the map-side job was known-absent, so nobody looked. This is CONDVAL exactly: the observable outcome is consistent with both working and broken.

NEXT, and it is Fred's frame not mine: walk each of the 19 cradle to grave and separate the two cases — (a) the Kotlin side already does the real work and the message is pure telemetry (likely for mode and group, which persist through GtSettings), versus (b) the message IS the work, so the process stops at the log line. Only (b) is remaining build. Do not assume either way: read the call site.

⏻⏻⏻ WHAT NAV XREF CAN AND CANNOT CERTIFY, MEASURED (C28-26370)

⏻⏻⏻ 2.8b C28-26370 · NAVXREFLIMIT — one row certified, five letters downgraded

Fred 11-21 13:18: “If you ran a query over code it may not be in the call stack and represent old code. Make su[r]e everything you identified is called from a launch point. I hate automated task searches. Tell us nothing without nav xref usage.”

He is right, and it invalidated three things I had just said. I grepped app/src/main/java for dispatch ids and reported verdicts off the hits. A text hit proves a string exists in a file; it proves nothing about reachability, and .bak files sit in the same tree. Worse, I then built that same grep into iosxref v5 as an “Android column” — automating the unsound step instead of fixing it.

MEASURED LIMITS OF NAV XREF. Authority: docs/navigation_xref.txt, generated 2026-11-20 22:20 at 06135bd8b — 273 files, 216 composables, 376 clickables.

  • Dispatch-id strings in nav xref: 0. It indexes panels, clickables and state flags, NOT the "module.process" to { } launch dispatch map. Nav xref therefore cannot answer “does Android dispatch this launch row” at all.
  • grouptrack-shared mentions in nav xref: 0. Gap 1, re-measured. Any row whose panel now lives in commonMain is invisible to it — which is why no Map Key panel appears in Android's click tree even though MapKeysPanel.kt is real.
  • The iPad's LaunchModules labels are descriptions (“CHECK IN (Group) — ride, role and radio on one screen, then records”), not Android's button text, so joining the two tools by label fails too. Ids never matched across platforms either. There is at present no reliable automatic join between the Android and iPad halves of a launch row.

⭐ WHAT IS CERTIFIED FROM AN INSTRUMENT TODAY — ONE ROW, AND ONE CORRECTION:

  • B1 · record.checkin — REAL DIFFERENCE. Nav xref section 2: CheckInSheet at ConvoyCheckIn.kt:52, 8 clickables. Section 3: the opener showCheckIn → CheckInSheet. Android reaches it; the iPad has no dispatch entry. Witness: a named panel, a named opener flag and a clickable count, all from the instrument.
  • map.sources — MOVED, NOT REMOVED. My earlier statement was wrong. Nav xref section 4: ConvoySettingsScreen.kt:286 | ConvoySettingsScreen | onNavigateToMapSources(). Android reaches Map source credits from Settings — which is A's “Map source credits” row. I had said it was “removed by decision, surviving only in .bak”; the grep missed it because it searched the map menu's dispatch map.

⏻ WHAT IS THEREFORE NOT CERTIFIED, AND MUST NOT BE TREATED AS IF IT WERE: the five key.* rows, three filter.* rows, four readout.* rows, record.rec and record.end. Each is unattached on the iPad — iosxref section 1 is sound, because it reads the shared module the iPad actually runs. What ANDROID does at the same hop is unknown to the instruments. G·MAP KEY and H·FILTER at “zero differences” are a hand assertion, not a certification.

THE FIX, for when Fred is back — this is the “insert the tool into the nav ref process” work: nav xref needs Android's launch dispatch indexed, "module.process" → its action and the function it calls, so the two tools join on the id each platform actually uses. Until that exists, every launch-row verdict is iPad-half-only and must say so.

Retired: iosxref_2026-11-21_v4.py → _DO_NOT_RUN_one_platform_only. iosxref_2026-11-21_v5.py is HELD, NOT TRUSTED — its Android column is the grep this card invalidates and must be rebuilt off nav xref before anyone reads a verdict from it.

⭐⭐⭐ THE B–M DIFFERENCE WORK LIST — FOUR ITEMS, TWO NEW SERVICES (C28-26369)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8b C28-26369 · DIFFS1121 — walked 11-21 08:06-08:20, differences only

THE DOC: docs/GroupTrack_2.8b_launch_point_differences_2026-11-21_v1.html — per-letter detail, every Android line cited, the services named. This card is the summary so the finding is not lost if the doc is not open.

⭐ THE HEADLINE: most of B–M is already built. Four items need completion, two of them already carded. C, D, G, H and I are MATCHED — and G·MAP KEY and H·FILTER turned out to be ALREADY SHARED (MapKeysPanel.kt and GtFilterBridge are both in commonMain, drawn by GroupTrack.kt:318-319, with the iPad's filter wiring doing real work). I had them down as absent until I stopped searching for "key." / "filter." dispatch ids, which exist on NEITHER platform — those rows are panel sections. Third instance today of the same error: searching by the wrong key reports built work as missing.

⛔ B·RECORD IS THE REAL WORK — three rows, all HOLLOW. The iPad's RECORD button (GtIosPanels.kt:1234-1252) advances a local recState and does nothing else: no check-in is written, no recorder starts, no lead is assigned, no permission is checked. Android does all of it at GroupTrackScreen.kt:1762-1800. ⚠ CONDVAL exactly: the button changes colour and label and pause even reports “paused”, so a tester concludes recording works and files the failure against storage or the GPS — neither of which was called.

⛔ TWO NEW SERVICES, both with their six negatives shown in the doc:

  • GtReachability.online(): Boolean — E·Status needs it (Android refuses the toggle when offline with no offline mode; the iOS comment at :1033 admits the gap). Every J download and import needs it too. The only near-hit in shared code is GtSettings:86's hasInternet parameter, whose KDoc says it is unused.
  • GtBackgroundLocation — authorised(), start(), stop(). B2 needs it: Android gates recording on ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION. GtIosLocation has requestWhenInUseAuthorization only — no authorisation query, no always-authorisation, no background updates. ⚠ Built BESIDE it per C28-26366 SVCEXT, not bolted in.

⭐ AND THE ONE THAT WAS ALREADY THERE: GtRides.checkIn(ride, callsign, role) at commonMain/ride/GtRides.kt:663 — B1's service is SHARED, and Android's ConvoyRideStore.checkIn:96 is just a delegate to it. ⚠ It returns NULL on failure rather than throwing, so the caller must handle null or a failed check-in passes silently.

⛔ DECISION OWED: F's map-type list and M's two carded gaps all read from the MAP layer. They are listed under their own letters, but they may belong with J·Map. Fred's call.

⚠ TWO LIMITS ON THIS PASS, stated in the doc: navxref could not document the iOS side at all (it does not scan grouptrack-shared), so the iOS half was read from source; and the letter-to-module mapping was derived from the RIGHT side's seven slots rather than re-read from the manual's diagram, which is the authority. Also: this counts rows REACHABLE FROM THE LAUNCH LAYER — it does not certify what each row's stack does below its first hop. That is still the per-row walk, and it is what found A4's missing renderer and A3's missing feed.

⏸ FLAGGED FOR AFTER THE LOOP — FOUR SYNCHRONISATION MECHANISMS IN iosMain (C28-26368)

⏸ 2.8b C28-26368 · SYNCFOUR — audit result 11-21 07:52. ⛔ DO NOT ACT DURING THE LOOP

Fred 11-21 07:51: “no you have created services that are dupe and need to be replaced by the existing services which were already tested and [certified]. the last thing we wan[t is] two different std services.” 07:52: “you need to follow my rules today or we will be [chasing] one service and missing half the code in a week.”

THE AUDIT HE ASKED FOR, run by capability against every service created on 11-20/11-21:

CreatedPre-existing equivalent?Verdict
GtBuildInfo / GtIosBuildInfonone — the only appVersion / device hits in shared code are its own callersNOT a dupe
GtScreenCapturenone in commonMain (the “capture” hits are track capture and config capturedDate)NOT a dupe
GtAssetVersionnone — six negatives run before buildingNOT a dupe
GtIssueModemirrors GtGroupMode's shape over the generic GtSettingsStore. A second INSTANCE of an existing pattern, not a second serviceNOT a dupe — ⚠ but see the note below
GtLocknone in commonMain. Three files had already documented the gap: TrailFilterState:29 (“Kotlin common code has no @Synchronized”), GtMBTiles:17, GtStartupHousekeeping:21NOT a dupe — ⛔ but it is the FOURTH mechanism, see below

⛔ THE REAL FINDING: iosMain now has FOUR ways to serialise access.

  1. Mutex() — IosSearchDb, GtIosPanels:296. SUSPENDING, so it cannot be used from a non-suspend function at all.
  2. kotlin.concurrent.AtomicInt — GtIosFlight:36-37, with its own written rationale (the non-experimental native surface on Kotlin 2.3.10). A compare-and-set FLAG, not a lock.
  3. A hand-rolled re-entrancy latch — GtIosDownloadQueue's picking/pendingPick. DRAIN-2026-11-17 says outright WHY it exists: it stands in for @Synchronized “in the only form available here”, a bare boolean latch is NOT reentrant, it refused a legitimate re-entry, and at 14 jobs with maxConcurrent 4 it left ten jobs QUEUED FOREVER until the loop was added.
  4. GtLock — NSRecursiveLock / ReentrantLock, reentrant, blocking. GTLOCK-2026-11-20.

⇒ (3) is the one that matters. It is a documented workaround for the absence of exactly what (4) now provides — and it cost a real defect to get right. When the loop is done, evaluate replacing the latch with GtLock, by Fred's method (C28-26367): count the consumers, weigh the benefit against the volume of changes. ⚠ Its re-entrancy behaviour is CERTIFIED by simulation (gt_dlqsim_2026-11-17_v2 cases 8, 16, 17, 18), so any replacement must keep those cases passing — which is itself the argument for not touching it mid-loop.

⚠ And the note on GtIssueMode: it is the SECOND per-flag object over the same store (GtGroupMode was the first, and set the precedent before this port). A third would be the point to stop and ask whether one generic “flag in a store” helper replaces them — that is the “expand services dramatically” risk Fred named at 07:27, arriving by instalments rather than all at once. Count them at the same evaluation.

⛔ NOTHING IN THIS CARD IS TO BE ACTED ON BEFORE THE LOOP IS COMPLETE AND CERTIFIED. Fred 07:31: “multiple changes on multiple features is quicksand.”

⏸ FLAGGED FOR AFTER THE LOOP — THREE EMAIL METHODS, ONE INTERACTION? (C28-26367)

⏸ 2.8 C28-26367 · EMAILTRIO — evaluate ONLY after this loop is tested and certified

Fred 11-21 07:31: “flag the services to be evaluated when we have completed this loop and tested [a]nd certified code. not before.” ⛔ DO NOT ACT ON THIS CARD DURING THE CERTIFICATION LOOP. It exists so the question is not lost, not so it gets answered early.

The question: GtArtifactSink now carries three methods for what may be one platform interaction — hand items to the rider's share path:

MethodFilesBorn
email(handle, fileName, subject, body)exactly onethe original
emailAll(handles, subject, body)N, and an empty list is REFUSED as a caller errorEMAILALL-2026-11-20, built beside email rather than reshaping it
emailText(subject, body)none — the body aloneEMAILTEXT-2026-11-21, built beside both for the same reason

What to weigh when the loop is done: whether these are one interaction with a list of length 0, 1 or N; whether emailAll's empty-list refusal should survive once a body-only path exists beside it (the two now answer the same situation differently, which is the honest cost of the add-beside rule); and whether email's fileName parameter still earns its place. Consolidating would retouch every consumer, which is exactly why it waits for a certified baseline.

⭐ HOW TO DECIDE IT, Fred 11-21 07:33: “when we are finished we will count the consumers of each service and weigh the consolidation benefits again[st] the volume of changes.”

⇒ So the evaluation is ARITHMETIC, not taste, and it needs a baseline that is not moving. For each of the three: count its call sites, then set the benefit of one interaction against the number of consumers a merge would have to touch. That count is only trustworthy once the loop is done — every launch point we certify adds consumers, so counting now would measure a half-built app and under-price the merge. ⚠ The count is of CALL SITES, not of files, and it includes test stubs and cert fakes, because every implementor of the interface is a consumer too (today: one — IosArtifactSink).

⚠ Do not read this card as a defect. Three methods that each do one thing correctly are a sound state to certify from. Sprawl is a cost to settle deliberately, with the consumers counted and the tests passing — not mid-loop, on judgment.

⭐⭐⭐ HOW TO EXTEND A SERVICE — A SECOND SERVICE, NOT A RETOUCH (C28-26366)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8 C28-26366 · SVCEXT — Fred's rule, and the one test that tells the two cases apart

Fred 11-21 07:29: “my gu[ide] [f]o[r] extending the services (like w[e] did for multiple attachment emails) is to build [a] second service for that use case rather the[n] retouching all existing service consumers.”

THE RULE: extend by ADDING a service beside the old one. Do not reshape a service that already has consumers. The precedent is EMAILALL-2026-11-20: email(handle, …) could have grown into email(handles, …) — and every existing caller would have had to be found and changed. Instead emailAll(handles, …) was built beside it and the consumers were left alone, to be moved over one function at a time. Fred 11-20: “no. just the build the service. we will find the consumers one function at a time.”

⭐ AND THE TEST THAT DECIDES WHICH CASE YOU ARE IN: CAN ANY EXISTING CONSUMER OBSERVE THE CHANGE?

ChangeObservable by a current caller?Verdict
email(handle) → email(handles)YES — every call site's signature changesSECOND SERVICE (this is EMAILALL)
emailAll stops REFUSING an empty listNO — every current caller passes a non-empty list and behaves identically⛔ OVERRULED 07:31 — STILL A SECOND SERVICE. I argued “relax in place” because no consumer could observe it. Correctness was never the question. See the change-control rule below
adding emailText(subject, body)no, but it encodes a USE CASE as a capability⛔ NEITHER — do not build it. Zero files is a list length, not a service

⛔⛔ THE RULE, CORRECTED BY FRED AT 07:31, AND IT IS CHANGE CONTROL, NOT CORRECTNESS: “listen, we have a list that we are working to get a stable starting point[.] multiple changes on multiple features is quicksand. build the second service[,] flag the services to be evaluated when we have completed this loop and tested [a]nd certified code. not before.”

⇒ So my “can a consumer observe it” test is NOT the deciding question, and reaching for it was the error. A relaxation that no caller can observe is STILL a change to a service other features use, made while the loop is certifying a different feature. What it costs is not correctness — it is the stable baseline the whole loop exists to reach. Two features moving at once means a later defect has two candidate causes, which is CONDVAL at the level of the day's work instead of the row's.

⇒ THE PROCEDURE, both halves: (1) BUILD THE SECOND SERVICE — always, even when editing the first one looks safe and smaller. (2) FLAG the consolidation for evaluation AFTER this loop is complete and the code is tested and certified — not before. The flag is what makes (1) acceptable rather than permanent sprawl: see C28-26367.

⛔ AND THE LINE UNDER BOTH OF THEM (Fred 07:27): “we are not building processes into services. service should be [a] label object interaction not application manipulation [o]f that data. that is use case specific. and will expand services dramatically.” ⇒ A service names a PLATFORM INTERACTION — hand these items to the share path, write this file, read this row. What the application then does with the data is not a service, however convenient it would be to put it there. The giveaway is a method whose name contains the caller's situation rather than the interaction.

⛔ A3 · RIDE STATISTICS — THE WALK, AND THE TWO THINGS THAT BLOCK A CLEAN FINISH (C28-26365)

⛔⛔ 2.8b C28-26365 · RIDESTATSWALK — walked 11-21 07:20-07:30, one row, to its end level

The row: A·MENU > settings > Ride statistics (ConvoySettingsScreen.kt:406) → dialog, monospace, scrollable, three controls: REFRESH :419, RESET :422, CLOSE :423. Android's entry is two one-liners — ConvoyViewModel:788 RideStats.report(nowMs, preset, intervalSecs, avgChannel) and :789 RideStats.reset(). The engine is app/.../convoy/RideStats.kt, 167 lines, ZERO import lines (every platform type is fully qualified at its use site, so nothing can be missed).

⭐ WHAT THE WALK CONFIRMED PRESENT — SVCFIRST was right twice:

  • GpsPresets IS ALREADY SHARED — commonMain/.../convoy/RecordingState.kt:12, moved there by ONEMAPHOST-2026-10-25 precisely because RideStats and the view model both use it. A find -name 'GpsPresets*.kt' returns NOTHING, because it lives inside another file: search by SYMBOL, never by filename.
  • com.grouptrack.comm.Member reachable from commonMain — api(projects.grouptrackComm), build.gradle.kts:42. Fields used: id, callsign, latitude, longitude, snr, rssi, hopsAway, channelUtilPct, airUtilPct.
  • 6 × @Synchronized → GtLock (built 11-20). 5 × org.json → GtOrgJson (shared/json/). LeadEstimate.Outcome is the six primitives as claimed, so the 204-line estimate engine does NOT come with the port.
  • The work order's %,d substitution was imaginary — there is no %,d anywhere in the file. The real formats are %.0f, %.1f, %.6f and %.0f%%, all covered by GtFmt.f(x, n).

⛔ LANDMINE 1 — TrackGeo.metres IS NOT THE SAME FORMULA. DO NOT SUBSTITUTE IT. TrackGeo.metres (TrackRules.kt:100-103) is EQUIRECTANGULAR: cos(midLat) × 111_320 by 110_574, via GtMath.hypot. RideStats.metres (:62-68) is TRUE HAVERSINE with r = 6_371_000.0. Reaching for the shared helper — exactly what a tidy port does — silently changes EVERY distance in the report: farthest heard, all five distance bands, every loss distance, the closest loss. It would surface days later as “the iPad's distances are wrong”, with a math-parity cert suite that passes. ⇒ The port keeps RideStats' own haversine, rebuilt on GtMath.sin/cos/asin + GtJava.toRadians.

⛔ LANDMINE 2 — json() BELONGS TO RIDES, AND GtOrgJson HAS NO num(Float). RideStats.json() is consumed by ConvoyViewModel:786 → GroupTrackScreen:3091 → GtRideStatsSend.send(...) — the “Email last ride statistics” row, which LaunchModules.kt:138 places last in RIDES, on the bottom bar. So its output is ANOTHER launch point's certified behaviour. GtOrgJson reproduces org.json by construction (STEP15A-2026-11-08, golden run) — but it offers num(Double), num(Long), num(Int) and no num(Float), while snr, ch and air are Float. Android's numberToString falls through to Float.toString, so 0.1f writes "0.1"; widening to Double writes "0.10000000149011612". A byte change in the Rides email payload. ⚠ And Float.toString is not guaranteed identical between Kotlin/Native and the JVM — which is why GtFmt and GtMath exist at all.

⛔ LANDMINE 3 — THE FEED IS NOT WIRED, AND IT IS D's. The three producers (onHeard, onMine, onEstimate) are called from ConvoyViewModel:615, :1191 and :1308, each gated by GroupMode.on. There is NO shared member-heard path, and iosMain's comm link exposes no heard-member surface. ⇒ The ported report would read “Nothing recorded yet” on a real iPad WITH a radio — indistinguishable from correct-but-empty, which is CONDVAL exactly. The feed belongs with D·GRP AWARENESS, beside C28-26364: RideStats' own header says it is “recorded ONLY while group awareness is on”. ⚠ This corrects AWORK-2026-11-20's witness for A3, which said an empty report on the SIMULATOR is correct. True, and insufficient: it is empty on the DEVICE too, until D wires the feed.

⇒ DECISION OWED BEFORE A3 CAN BE FINISHED (Fred's call, two options):

  1. Add num(Float) to GtOrgJson mirroring Android's Float.toString, then move the whole object. Cleanest end state, ONE copy — but it edits a CERTIFIED shared component for RIDES' benefit while we are certifying A, and it wants the parity suite run.
  2. Move report/reset/the producers and leave json() on Android for now. Keeps A's work inside A — but json() reads the same private state, so the object cannot actually be split without duplicating it, which breaks the DE-DUPLICATION rule.

⇒ Because (2) is not really available, A3 cannot be finished without touching a certified component. RECOMMENDATION: do A2 next — it also builds JSON, but its output is NEW, so nothing downstream depends on byte-identity — and settle the num(Float) question deliberately rather than inside a port.

⛔ PARKED FOR D·GRP AWARENESS — THE LEAD TRACK HAS NO RENDERER ON THE iPAD (C28-26364)

⛔⛔ 2.8b C28-26364 · LEADTRACKD — ABSENT at both ends, and it is NOT an A item

Fred 11-21 07:12: “a4 is[] contingent on the[] [g]r[p a]warenes[s] setting[;] some items do not appear unless those setting are on.” ⇒ the lead track is a GRP AWARENESS item, so its renderer belongs to D, walked at D and not inside A.

⭐ A's SIDE IS MATCHED — nothing is owed in A. The panel already gates the row behind group awareness exactly as Android does (ConvoySettingsScreen:338): with group off, the iPad shows “The alert thresholds and the lead track apply to a GROUP ride” in place of the row. The flag is session-only on BOTH platforms — Android's ConvoyViewModel:521 is a plain MutableStateFlow(true), the iPad's is remember { mutableStateOf(true) }. ⛔ Do not re-open this as a persistence defect; it was wrongly called one on 11-20.

⛔ WHAT IS ACTUALLY ABSENT, measured 11-21 07:13 — BOTH ENDS, which is why the AWORK order was wrong:

  • ⛔⛔ CORRECTED 07:18 — THE PRODUCER EXISTS, AND MY FIRST MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG (LEADCORR-2026-11-21). I searched for Android's SYMBOL NAMES — leadTrackSegments, leadDots — found nothing in grouptrack-shared, and wrote “no producer”. Literally true of those names, and MISLEADING: the producer is there under different names, because the seam was DESIGNED not to carry Android's display type. TrackRules.kt:569-571 says so outright: “ConvoyEngine.LeadTrackSegment is an ANDROID DISPLAY type, so it does not cross the seam: this hands over the DRAWN (trimmed) lead line and the host builds its own segments from it.” ⭐ What shared code already gives any host: LeadCartProcess.drawnLine() (:572, the trimmed line), leadId() (:574) and dots() (:530, the gap pins that fit no line). Both halves, already across the seam, since SEAMRULE-2026-11-19. ⇒ THE LESSON, and it is CRAFT rule 2 verbatim: search by the dated marker and by CAPABILITY, never by the other platform's symbol name. A port's counterpart is renamed precisely when the seam was done properly, so searching for Android's name finds nothing exactly where the work HAS been done. This is also SVCFIRST from the other direction: I inferred absence instead of showing it.
  • What is genuinely absent is the HOST ADAPTER and the DRAW API — two small things, not a subsystem. (a) Android's adapter is LeadCartSegments.kt, FIFTEEN LINES (843 bytes): an extension LeadCartProcess.segments() that reads drawnLine() and leadId() and builds Android's display type. The iPad needs its own counterpart of that size. (b) GtVecMap.ios.kt's whole public surface is read(z, x, y) for tiles, request(args), the composable and gtVecMapController(onMessage) — no draw API, so there is nothing yet to hand a polyline or a dot array to. That is the real work at D.
  • Android has THREE gated consumption points (the handoff claimed two): :535 lead-for-pan, :578 the drawn segments, :611 a leadDots JS array pushed into the page.
  • Android has THREE consumption points, not the two the handoff claimed: :535 lead-for-pan (the auto-pan fit), :578 the drawn segments, and :611 a leadDots JS array pushed into the page. All three are gated by the same flag; all three are missing.

⚠ WHAT WOULD HAVE HAPPENED WITHOUT THE WALK. AWORK-2026-11-20 said to “make the lead segments the vecmap page receives honour it”. The iPad receives NO lead segments, so that patch would have produced a control gating an empty path — compiling green, reading as done, and surviving a stack walk as “MATCHED” because the value IS consumed. This is the admission-window class of defect, and the only thing that caught it was asking whether the next function EXISTS rather than whether it is referenced.

⚠ THE WITNESS, with Fred's contingency built in: group awareness must be ON before a missing lead track means anything. With it off, the absence is CORRECT behaviour on both platforms. So the negative witness is: group on, a lead cart with a track, and still nothing drawn. Any check run with group off passes either way and is worthless.

Scope when D is reached, REPRICED after the 07:18 correction: (1) an iOS host adapter mirroring LeadCartSegments.kt — about fifteen lines, reading drawnLine(), leadId() and dots(); (2) a DRAW API on GtVecMap for a polyline and a dot array, which is the one genuinely new piece; (3) the three gated consumption points. The shared producer is NOT in scope — it is built. Still map-layer work and still priced at D, but it is an adapter plus a draw call, not a subsystem. Do not carry it as a loose end in A.

⚠ THE FOUR EMPTY-LIST RETURNS ARE THE DANGEROUS ONES, and they are why this card exists. The file's own header at :32 states that NotSupported and an empty list are “both legitimate values in this contract” — true for the CONTRACT, and not the same thing for the RIDER. A NotSupported can be surfaced as “not available on iPad”. An empty list cannot be told apart from a real empty result: savedConfigs() says “you have no saved configs” and driftSinceLastSave() says “nothing has drifted”. That is LASTSTALE (C28-26356) in another costume — substituting a benign-looking value for data we do not have — and it is exactly the class the four questions exist to catch: the result IS consumed, so a stack walk alone says MATCHED.

WHAT THE WALK AT N MUST PRODUCE — a verdict per member, not a verdict for the file: each of the twelve is either a GENUINE iOS LIMIT (named, with the platform reason, and surfaced to the rider as such) or UNPORTED (with the Android function named). Known already: the saved-setups half needs GroupTrack's config-doc format and RadioBackups' store, neither of which is in shared code yet — so some of these are unported, not limits. Known genuine limits elsewhere in the comm container (do not re-litigate): forget() (no unpair API — the rider uses iOS Settings), onBoxWifi (LoRa only), radioName() (Android's RadioNameStore lives in core/data), RadioEntry.paired (iOS does not expose the bonded list).

AND THE SECOND CLAIM TO RE-TEST AT N: CommLink was reported as “all 16 members implemented”. Implemented is not verified — that is the same word that covered “attached” on 11-20. It gets the same per-member walk.

⛔ NOTHING HERE IS A DEFECT IN LAST NIGHT'S WORK. The comm container was built to the contract and is honest about what it does not do. This card records that the contract being satisfied is not the same as the screen being certified, and that the distinction is owed at N.

⭐⭐⭐ CHECK THE SERVICES THAT EXIST BEFORE BUILDING A NEW ONE — FRED 11-20 22:03 (C28-26361)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8 C28-26361 · SVCFIRST — read the inventory, not a card, before building a service

Fred, 2026-11-20 22:03: “remember to check on created services before creating any new service.....”

THE RULE: before writing any new service, expect/actual, or platform interface, read the inventory first — from the directories and the contract doc. Not from a card, not from a memory of a card, not from a note written on another day.

WHY IT IS A RULE. Earlier tonight I told Fred the service layer had nine holes, sourced from 11-16 cards. He answered “LOOKS MORE LIKE AN INPRUNED ITEM FORM THE 16TH” and “TRUSTE ME IF IT WAS IDENTFIED AS A SEAM WE HAVE SERVICED IT.” Measurement proved him right on every one — GtIosRecipeEnv, GtIosHkEnv, GtIosSettings, GtMapSpecEnv, GtTileStoreEnv all present and working, and GtVecMap.ios.kt:262 emits the very store OPEN · from store 4 line quoted on 11-18 as proof the tile store works. A stale card cost that detour; the directory listing would have cost ten seconds.

⛔⛔ THE SERVICE LAYER IS ~95% COMPLETE, AND THE COMMENTS IN THE CODE ARE OUT OF DATE (SVCSTALE-2026-11-20). Fred, 2026-11-20 22:08: “also notes the comments and cards in the code are outdated and the services played is 95% complete. there may ba straggler but it will be unusal and the services need to checked before creating new.”

This flips the default and it flips the burden of proof.

  • Assume the service EXISTS and go find it. At 95%, “missing” is the unusual case, not the likely one. A straggler is possible — and when one turns up it is a real find, logged as such — but it is not the first hypothesis. Fred 11-20: “IF WE MISSED ANY WE WILL STEP RIGHT ON TJEM AS WE COMPLETE PROCESSES.” The processes find the stragglers; guessing at them does not.
  • An in-code comment claiming a service is missing, stubbed, pending or “not yet” is STALE BY DEFAULT. So is a card. They were written while the layer was being built and were not revised as it was finished. A comment is evidence of what someone believed on the day they typed it — never evidence of today's code. This is CRAFT rule 4 (the comment is not the code — verify the claim) with the direction named: the stale claim is almost always “absent”, and the code is almost always present.
  • BEFORE building, absence must be SHOWN, not inferred. The bar is: not in commonMain/platform/, not in commonMain/concurrent/, not in iosMain/platform/, not among the 9 implemented seams, not in artifact/ or settings/, and not in §20. Six negatives. A TODO, a log line saying “no iOS <thing> yet”, or a card from another day is none of those six.
  • Corollary for the A2/A3 ports: walk in expecting every dependency to be present. On 11-20 that is exactly how it measured — TrailCheck's ten substitutions all resolved to existing shared code, and RideStats' only true blocker (@Synchronized) was one small primitive, not a service.

The failure this prevents is not wasted work — it is a SECOND implementation of something that already works, built because a comment said it did not. That compiles, looks done, and splits every future consumer.

WHERE TO LOOK — the five places, measured 2026-11-20 22:05:

PlaceHoldsAs of 11-20
commonMain/.../shared/platform/the toolkit's platform interfaces GtBuildInfo (appVersion/device), GtScreenCapture (capture/clear)
commonMain/.../shared/concurrent/concurrency primitives expect class GtLock — the toolkit's FIRST lock (ReentrantLock / NSRecursiveLock)
iosMain/.../shared/platform/the iOS actuals and Env classes 25 GtIos* files: Storage, Settings, Http, Location, Places, Assets, Flight, Startup, Values, StateRun, DownloadQueue, QueuePlatform, ImportPlatform, RideFiles, RideImport, RideNetworks, RideSpatial, OsmPlatform, RecipeEnv, HkEnv, Env, ArtifactSink, BuildInfo, ScreenCapture, VecMap
the serviced SEAMScommonMain interfaces an Ios* class implements 9: GtArtifactSink, GtHelpAssets, GtHkDb, GtHkEnv, GtImportEnv, GtMapSpecEnv, GtRecipeEnv, GtSettingsStore, GtTileStoreEnv
artifact/ and settings/the shared services proper GtArtifactFiles (email, emailAll); GtSettings, GtRideSettings, GtGroupMode, GtIssueMode

AND THE WRITTEN CONTRACT: grouptrack_service_layer_design_*.html §20 Platform services contract — the doc a new row goes INTO when a service is genuinely new. If a service is missing from §20 but present in the directories, §20 is what is stale, not the code.

THE TWO COMMANDS — either beats a card:

ls grouptrack-shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/grouptrack/shared/platform/ \
   grouptrack-shared/src/iosMain/kotlin/com/grouptrack/shared/platform/

grep -rhoE ': *Gt[A-Za-z0-9_]+(Env|Sink|Db|Settings|Assets|Store)\b' \
   grouptrack-shared/src/iosMain --include=*.kt | sort -u

THE SAME FAILURE, THIRD TIME. It is the 10-01 rule (fix the existing tool, do not invent one beside it) and it is hand-grepping A·MENU for two hours while navxref already held the whole click tree. The answer was already in the repo all three times. A second service built beside a working one is worse than a missing one: it compiles, it looks done, and it splits every future consumer between two implementations.

THE GOOD CASE, for contrast — EMAILALL-2026-11-20. emailAll WAS genuinely new: GtArtifactFiles had email for one handle and nothing for a list. Fred: “no. just the build the service. we will find the consumers one function at a time. this is a generic service that takes list.” That is the shape of a correct new service — inventory checked first, gap named, built once generically, consumers found afterwards one at a time.

⭐⭐⭐ METHOD — HOW A LAUNCH POINT IS CERTIFIED COMPLETE (LAUNCHCERT-2026-11-20) (C28-26360)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8 C28-26360 · LAUNCHCERT — three instruments, four questions, one function at a time

Fred 11-20 21:30: “document this process so we repeat for all completed launch points to certify all functioning with the call stacks.” And 21:35: “screen contents must be compared. we were missing many values under setting[s]. the cleaner we get this pass the better the sta[r]ting point for unit testing.”

WHY A METHOD AND NOT A CHECKLIST. On 2026-11-20, A·MENU — one launch point of thirteen, already counted as done — produced four functions to build, two silent defects, one row that did not exist, and two dead chrome methods. Nothing found that by inspection or by memory. Each class of defect needed a different instrument, and using the wrong one returns a clean answer that is wrong.

THE THREE INSTRUMENTS — each finds a class the others CANNOT

#InstrumentWhat only it findsWhat it proves about completeness
1MARKER SCAN. Grep the surface for notBuilt(, NotSupported, “not attached yet”, “not built”, “no iOS”, “not on the iPad yet”, TODO(). Stubs that announce themselves. 42 sites on 11-20, 27 in one file. NOTHING. It is a to-do list, not evidence. Every defect below is invisible to it.
2SCREEN CONTENT COMPARISON (Fred 21:35). Enumerate EVERY section label, row, control and bottom matter of the Android screen. Enumerate the shared/iOS equivalent. Diff them. Rows that were never written. No marker, no stub, no string — nothing to search for. On 11-20 this found Ride statistics absent from the iPad settings panel entirely, and the build stamp and Map HTML lines absent — the last two existing on Android for a measured reason (HTMLVER-2026-08-13B: a stale asset date cost an evening of bisecting because it could not be seen). That the two screens hold the same THINGS. Says nothing about whether any of them work.
3CALL-STACK WALK, one function at a time, from the control the rider touches to the thing that does the work. Rows that are present and hollow. On 11-20: the ISSUE-email switch that moved and forgot; HELP dead on every panel because one chrome method was empty; applyRide returning NotSupported inside a layer reported as built. This is the only instrument that certifies a function. Its output IS the evidence.

THE FOUR QUESTIONS — asked out loud at EVERY hop

  1. Does the next function EXIST? Not “is it referenced” — cartFromMember was referenced at :1328 for five weeks and never written, and the group silently never admitted a cart that had not sent a position packet.
  2. Does it do the work, or does it REPORT? A message, a log line, a default, an empty list and a NotSupported are all reports. “Attached”, “wired”, “plumbed”, “the contract exists” and “compile-verified” are all steps, and none of them is done.
  3. Is the result CONSUMED? Follow the value to its point of USE, not to its assignment. The admission window was read from settings, stored in a field and never compared against anything — slider working, UI text correct, function doing nothing, no marker anywhere.
  4. What does ANDROID do at this hop? Android is the specification. A difference is either a port gap or a deliberate platform decision, and it is named as one or the other, never left unexplained. The platform answer must be the honest one: on iOS CommLink.forget is a real limit (no unpair API), and the subject UIActivityViewController drops is a real delta — both are recorded, not hidden.

THE OUTPUT — what “certified” means on paper

Per function: the walked stack itself, hop by hop with file and line, and one verdict — COMPLETE, in which case the stack is the evidence and it goes to the completed doc, or BROKEN AT HOP n, naming what is missing. A verdict with no stack behind it is not a verdict. “I reviewed six and here is a summary” is how 35 functions came to be called complete with stubs inside them.

THE RULES THAT MAKE IT HOLD

  • ONE function at a time, reviewed before the next. No batching.
  • A patch carries ONE function's remedy. Not a bundle across functions, not a sweep, and never code that is not broken — on 11-20 a patch was written that “fixed” a function the walk had just certified COMPLETE.
  • Serve the standard; do not invent one beside it. The five panels that hand-roll a title row instead of drawing in GtPanel are why HELP went missing five times. The remedy is to consume the standard, not to add a second header (SHELL-2026-10-22 standards 1, 4, 5).
  • No assertion about what does or does not exist without reading the code. Broken twice on 11-20, in opposite directions: stubs counted as complete in the afternoon, built services counted as missing in the evening. Both came from reading a summary instead of the thing.
  • A comment that states a CONDITION must be re-read when the condition changes. Four stale comments on 11-20, every one claiming a blocker that had already been built — “that is D work”, “needs GtHelp's link table”, “until the help table is shared”, “the middle 80%”.
  • A stale OPEN card is not a passive gap. Fred 11-20 20:40: “not only are they done and not marked as completed, they plot a different course than the one we are on.” Nine service cards from 11-16 nearly redirected a week into rebuilding a tile store that was already serving tiles.
  • A stub is a decision Fred has not made yet (C28-26359 · STUBRULE). The sanctioned set is empty.

WHY THIS PASS DECIDES WHAT UNIT TESTING IS WORTH

Fred 11-20 21:35: “the cleaner we get this pass the better the sta[r]ting point for unit testing.” A unit test written against a hollow row certifies the hollow row. It will pass, and it will pass for years. Today's value-parity run already shows the shape of that failure one layer up: three suites in the green 22 run in 0.0, 0.001 and 0.002 seconds and may assert nothing at all, while reading as certified. Every function this pass leaves hollow becomes a test that lies.

TIED TO: 2.8a and 2.8b, and every release after. GRADUATES TO: the living master — this is a method rule. APPLIES TO: all thirteen launch points; A·MENU is the worked example, and the one it was derived from.

⛔⛔ DURABLE RULE — A STUB IS A DECISION FRED HAS NOT MADE YET (STUBRULE-2026-11-20)

⛔⛔ 2.8 C28-26359 · STUBRULE — never stub an undiscussed item, and never count a stub as complete

Fred 11-20 16:26: “WHEN YOU STUB ITEMS WE HAVE NOT DISCUSSED AND CALL THEM COMPLETE[,] THAT DOES NOT FALL ON ME.” With 11-20 15:49: “I HOPE WE DO NOT MISS STUB[S] AND COUNT THEM AS COMPLETED ITEMS.”

⛔ FIRST, THE DISTINCTION I COLLAPSED. Fred 11-20 16:30: “[D]OCUMENT STUBS ARE A NORMAL PART OF THE PROCESS. SOF[T]WARE STUBS ARE NOT. WE OFFERED TO STUB AROUND RADIO FUNCTIONS ONLY.”

KindStatusWhy
DOCUMENT stub — a manual section, release-note entry or QuickStart step marked 🚧 TO FILL NORMAL. Part of the process. It is a visible placeholder in a document a human reads, it names what it is missing, and it is scheduled (MANUALSTUB-2026-11-18: “on Monday we need to complete these stubs, screen captures, section removals”). A reader cannot mistake it for finished text.
SOFTWARE stub — a function that returns, logs or shows a message instead of doing its job ⛔ NOT a normal part of the process. It compiles, it runs, the screen opens, and nothing says a decision is owed. It is indistinguishable from working code from the outside — which is the whole defect.
The ONE sanctioned exception: RADIO functions Agreed, and only this. Offered and accepted because mesh BLE cannot execute on the iPad simulator at all. The exception was negotiated. It was never a licence that generalised to anything else.

⛔⛔⛔ AND AS OF TODAY THE EXCEPTION SET IS EMPTY. Fred 11-20 16:32: “WE STARTED WITH RADIO AND EMAIL FUNCTIONS. WE THEN DESIGNED AN EMAIL SERVICE SOLUTION BECAUSE OF THE VOLUME OF UNTESTABLE EMAIL USES. AND TODAY WE CREATED OUR RADIO LAYER SO WHEN WE GET TO MAPS THERE SHOULD BE NO SERVICE[S] LAYER MISSING. THAT WAS THE PLAN.”

There were ever only two sanctioned stub cases, and both were sanctioned for the same reason — a volume of uses that could not be executed on the simulator. Both have since been closed by building the thing, which was always the plan:

Sanctioned caseWhy it was grantedHow it was closedStill a licence?
EMAIL functionsThe volume of email uses that cannot be exercised on the simulator An email service solution was designed rather than stubbing each use site NO — closed by design
RADIO functionsMesh BLE cannot execute on the iPad simulator at all The radio layer was built TODAY — IOSCOMM / IOSCONFIG / IOSCOMMWIRE, commit 22a3475feNO — closed by construction, 2026-11-20

THE CONSEQUENCE, and it reaches one of my own entries below: :813 (“Archived radio setups are not stored on the iPad yet”) was called “the one sanctioned stub” in v15 of this checklist. That was true under the condition that no radio layer existed. The condition expired today. With the layer built, the saved-setups half — which returns NotSupported / emptyList() pending GroupTrack's config-doc format and RadioBackups — is owed work like everything else. Seventeen of seventeen are now live, and there is no remaining category of software stub that anyone has agreed to.

⛓ MEASURED AT THIS EOD — 17 software stub sites in GtIosPanels.kt, and ONE is radio. The other sixteen were written outside the agreement, by me, without being raised:

LineWhat it stubsInside the radio exception?
:813“Archived radio setups are not stored on the iPad yet” WAS — sanctioned only while no radio layer existed; that condition expired 11-20 when the layer was built
:972“checked in (the ride env is not built on the iPad yet)”no

⛔⛔ THREE OF THEM ARE WORSE THAN STUBS — they report success for work that did not happen. At :972 the app says “checked in”, at :976 it says “recording” while writing nothing, and at :995 it says “ended”. A rider reading those three has been told their track is being recorded when no recorder exists. That is not an incomplete function, it is a false statement to the user, and it is the same class as C28-26356 · LASTSTALE — never substitute a plausible answer for one we do not have. These three are the first items of the cleanup pass, ahead of the rest.

THE RULE, in two parts.

  1. A stub is not a building decision I get to make. When a function's behaviour has not been discussed, the answer is to ASK, not to write a placeholder that satisfies the compiler and moves the cursor forward. A stub written in place of a conversation hides the fact that a decision is owed, and it hides it inside working code where nothing will surface it again.
  2. A stub is never reported as complete, and never closes a launch point. Not “attached”, not “wired”, not “plumbed” — those words described finished work today and were not true. A launch point is finished when every function reachable from its screen is built.

WHY IT HAS TO BE A RULE AND NOT AN INTENTION. It already failed twice in one day, in both directions. My first count of the launch points said “7 DONE” and included menu.settings — which has four stubs inside it — and menu.help, which has no document viewer at all. And when Fred asked what was actually built behind RADIO SETUP, the honest answer was that a status message had been attached and the configurator had not been written; he had to ask “did we do plumbing all day[?]” to get it. Both were caught by Fred reading what I wrote, not by anything in the process.

THE CONTROL. The launch-point register (C28-26357 · COMPLETIONREG, in the 11-20 EOD handoff) is regenerated mechanically at each start of day, with the demotion rule intact: any process whose body contains a stub marker, a notBuilt( call or a “not attached yet” string is demoted out of DONE regardless of whether its screen opens. A count produced by hand is not acceptable evidence of completion — mine was wrong by two on the first attempt. And any stub that must exist for a build to proceed is raised with Fred the same day, named, before it is written.

TIED TO: 2.8a and 2.8b, and every release after. GRADUATES TO: the living master — this is a method rule, not a release item.

⭐⭐ THE HORIZON AFTER THE CLEANUP — FRED 11-20 16:22 (C28-26358)

⭐⭐ 2.8b C28-26358 · SEQHORIZON — cleanup, then ADD ROUTE, then at least three days on map functions

Fred 11-20 16:22: “TOMORROW WILL BE A PAINFUL DAY UNTIL WE GET TO ADD ROUTE. I DO NOT KNOW IF WE WILL GET THROUGH ADD ROUTES TOMORROW. THE[N] AT LEAST 3 DAYS ON MAP FUNCTIONS.”

PROBLEM DEFINITION. The order rule means the next three to five days are not feature days. Tomorrow is spent closing functions that were already counted as finished — 5 stubs, 17 shells, 30 unbuilt of 57 launch processes, plus 16 stubs sitting inside screens that look ported. ADD ROUTE may not be reached tomorrow at all, and the map functions behind it are at least three days on their own.

⛓ WHERE THIS CAME FROM, STATED CORRECTLY. Fred 11-20 16:26: “I DON'T CALL IT IMPATIENCE, I CALL IT BEING EXPEDITIOUS[.] WHEN YOU STUB ITEMS WE HAVE NOT DISCUSSED AND CALL THEM COMPLETE[,] THAT DOES NOT FALL ON ME.” An earlier version of this card (v13) named the risk as impatience on Fred's part. That was wrong and it is corrected here. The three-to-five day window exists because I wrote stubs for functions that were never discussed with him and then reported the launch points as complete. Moving fast was never the defect; the defect was substituting a stub for a decision and then counting it. Fred's pace did not create this backlog — it is the only reason the backlog is small enough to clear in a week.

ANTICIPATED SOLUTION. Hold the order rule for the whole window and let the register be the judge of “finished” rather than the screen opening. Concretely: the launch-point register (C28-26357 · COMPLETIONREG, now in the 11-20 EOD handoff) is regenerated mechanically at each start of day, with the demotion rule intact, so progress is counted the same way every morning and a stub can never drift back into the DONE column. ADD ROUTE is started when the register says the buttons before it have no unbuilt function — not on a day when it feels overdue. Expect the DONE count to move slowly and the STUB and SHELL counts to move fast; that is the shape of a cleanup pass going correctly.

TIED TO: 2.8b, and 2.8a for the Android side of each shared function. HORIZON: 11-21 cleanup → ADD ROUTE (11-21 or 11-22) → map functions, 3+ days. Aspirational by design: no per-function detail here, because the detail belongs in the EOD doc on the morning each function is actually taken up.

⭐ THE GOVERNING PRINCIPLE, Fred 11-19 15:45, verbatim — this is the one to keep: "We cannot stop the process at a level because the service doesn't exist. We need to address the missing service and continue the current process function." A missing service is not a stopping condition. It is the next thing to build, and then you finish the function you were in. Stopping at the level where the service is absent is what produced every half-depth port found today.

⭐ OPENED 2026-11-19 EOD — THE LEVEL-2 DEPTH CHECK (C28-26347)

⭐ 2.8b C28-26347 · DEPTHCHECK — how to find a port that stops two layers down, and what is still unchecked

Fred 11-19 15:37, the diagnosis in one sentence: "you went [two] layers down and said t[h]at['s] enough." 16:49: "we need to detail the check for items below the level 2 invocation."

⛔ THE FAILURE SHAPE. A panel is called with the right name and the right arguments, and the layer under those arguments is missing. It compiles, it draws, it passes a symbol sweep, and it is wrong on glass. Every defect found on 11-19 had this shape:

Symptom Fred reportedWhat was actually missing, one layer down
"search returns me to the same point I exited from" (13:52)openArtifact was Android's FALLBACK branch; gtFitOne (the DB bbox, the 10% pad, the display set) was never ported
"no options display under fit" (15:33)ArtifactDetailPanel takes 15 parameters; TEN action callbacks defaulted to null and the iPad passed none. Android passes 14 of 15.
"the filter has no impact on the map display" (16:10)GtSpatial.viewport took no display states at all — its own comment said "with every type ON"
"fit... not executing from route detail panel" (16:37)the panel's FIT is onDismiss(type, id) and the host ignored both arguments, making FIT a second CLOSE button
settings panel showed one sentence (11-19 midday)seven rows omitted inside a transcribed file — no seam involved at all

⭐ THE CHECK, three passes. The first two are mechanical and take seconds.

1. SEAM DIFF — catches a substituted argument. Parse the SAME call on both platforms (GroupTrackLaunchLayer, GtSearchBridge, GtFilterBridge, each panel) and compare argument by argument: where Android's expression names a function and the iPad's is inline JS, a message or {}, that is a stub. This found the autoPan question, routePrecheck = { false }, mapsDownloading = false, onToggleView, viewLabel, mapType/mapTypes, onHelp, openSelect, toggleHide and setOffGrid*.

2. SCOPED LABEL DIFF — catches content omitted INSIDE a port. A seam diff cannot see this, because nothing crosses a seam. Extract the user-visible string literals from the port and from the Android ORIGIN RANGE and diff them: missing labels are missing rows. This is what would have caught the settings panel.

3. THE DISCIPLINE THAT MAKES PASS 2 POSSIBLE: every ported file cites its Android origin by line range in its header. Measured 11-19 15:2x: GtRecordButton, GtGpsHudRow and GtDocsChooser all cite one and check clean; GtAlertSettingsPanel cites MapKeysPanel.kt, which does not exist — so it cannot be checked at all, and it is the one panel that failed. A port with no resolvable origin range is unverifiable by construction.

⚠ NEITHER PASS catches logic that is present but wrong. That still needs glass. The two passes cover omission, which is most of what 11-19 produced.

⭐ CODE RULE 1 EARNED ITS KEEP TWICE TODAY, both worth remembering: (a) ArtifactDetailPanel's ten nullable action callbacks are the rule's exact failure mode — "optional defers the 'is every caller wiring this correctly?' check to silent field discovery", and the discovery was Fred on the glass. (b) Making displayState REQUIRED on GtVecMap found a second call site (the bare Map tab) in four seconds. Had it defaulted, that tab would have compiled and the GroupTrack tab's filter would have kept doing nothing, with two candidate paths and no way to tell them apart.

⛔ PROVEN ON GLASS 11-19 vs COMPILED BUT NEVER EXERCISED. This is tomorrow's cleanup scope — it is a definite list, not "re-test everything":

Proven on glassCompiled and wired, NEVER exercised

⭐ FIRST RESULT OF THE DEPTH CHECK, already found (16:45): the baseline has no my_tracks directory and no recorded .gpx files. A track's export IS the file written at record time (MYTRACKSDB-2026-09-12) — it is never rebuilt, because rebuilding re-rounds every coordinate. Android's tracksDir() = root / "my_tracks", so the iPad path is correct and the data is simply absent; it is not in the 13 app-private files either. So export and share work for routes, trails and waypoints (built from the database) and FAIL for tracks — on the iPad and on Droid 2 alike. Droid 2 holds 136 track ROWS and zero track FILES. A baseline fact, not a code defect, and it must be settled before export is certified.
