⭐⭐⭐ PRINCIPLE ADOPTED 2026-11-20 — "LAST SAVED" IS A LIE ABOUT THE PRESENT (C28-26356)

⭐⭐⭐ 2.8 C28-26356 · LASTSTALE — stop substituting a default or a last-known value for data we do not have

Fred 11-20 10:59: "this last save st[uff] is always wrong and leads t[o] these results." And 10:58, pointing at the alternative: "w[hy are] we not using bearing from the current record as long as it is not standing still[?]"

THE PRINCIPLE. A value we do not currently have must be reported as absent, never as a default and never as the last one we held. "Last known" is not a fact about now; it is a claim about the present that the data does not support, and the consumer cannot tell the difference. This is CODE RULE 1's rationale extended from parameters to values: the shortcut defers the "is this real?" question to whoever draws the screen, and they have no way to ask it.

FOUR INSTANCES FOUND TODAY, all in one defect chain (C28-26354):

  • ConvoyViewModel.kt:1335 — heading_deg = msg.track?.courseDeg ?: 0f. Absent course becomes due north.
  • :1039 — val hdg = loc?.bearing ?: 0f. Same substitution, tablet path.
  • :1043 — heading_deg = prevMe?.heading_deg ?: 0f. The last-saved value, then a default when even that is missing.
  • vec_carts.js gtLastHdg — held a stale 0 indefinitely once set, observed on device as (held 0) across the whole 12-minute capture.

And a fifth, in the same log: windowMph repeating one value for 45 seconds, which reads as a live speed and is not.

⭐⭐ FRED'S ALTERNATIVE IS BETTER THAN A GATE, AND THE MACHINERY ALREADY EXISTS. Take the heading from the recorded track, not from the GPS chip's bearing field. The reason this is not merely a different source: TrackRules already defines what counts as movement, and it defines it for the track the rider actually sees drawn. TrackRules.kt (commonMain, 599 lines) — R1: "every real point is posted"; R2: "a GAP = more than 4 s AND more than 5 m since the last written point". So a newly written point means the cart genuinely moved; the bearing between consecutive written points exists only when there is real displacement, and is undefined when there is not. The condition "as long as it is not standing still" is therefore not a new threshold to invent — it is already the rule that governs the track.

the design that follows. (1) Compute the course from the last two written track points, in TrackRules/GtMath so both platforms share it — no bearing-between-points helper exists yet; haversineM does (GtRiderScan.kt:174). (2) When no new point has been written, send nothing — do not call gtMyHeading, do not hold, do not default. (3) The page already handles that correctly: isFinite(hdg) and gtLastHdg != null mean absence is understood. (4) Heading then agrees with the track being drawn, from one source, which the current design cannot guarantee.

⚠ ONE OPEN QUESTION FOR FRED: heading-up while NOT recording. The track-derived course exists only during a recording. Options: fall back to the chip's bearing but only when hasBearing() is true (never substituted), or hold north-up until a recording starts. A decision, not a task.

Principle stated by Fred 2026-11-20 10:59 (LASTSTALE-2026-11-20); the four instances and the TrackRules reading are measured against the 2.8 branch the same day.

⭐⭐ OPENED 2026-11-20 — THE SPIN, TRACED END TO END (C28-26354) — CORRECTS C28-26351

⭐⭐ 2.8a C28-26354 · HEADINGTRACE — the page is right; the app hands it "due north" when the course is unknown

Fred 11-20 10:49: "no it spins as the map refreshe[s] m[y] position[.] simulate the code." Simulated below, end to end, per the standing rule.

⛔ FIRST, THE CORRECTION I OWE. C28-26351 said the feature was never implemented, on the strength of two comments: vecmap_main.html:89 listing gtSetHeadingUp among "placeholders", and vec_view.js saying "gtSetHeadingUp stays a placeholder (VECPORT todo) until the heading work." Both are stale. The work was done and landed in a different file, and those notes were never updated. My grep also missed it because bearing does not match getBearing. Two avoidable errors: trusting a comment over the code, and a case-sensitive search.

THE PAGE IMPLEMENTATION IS CORRECT AND CAREFUL — vec_carts.js:68-85, with both guards I claimed were missing:

function gtTurnTo(b) {
  var cur = map.getBearing(), d = ((b - cur) % 360 + 540) % 360 - 180;
  if (Math.abs(d) < 3) return;            // no jitter -- a 3 degree deadband
  map.rotateTo(b, { duration: 800 });
}
window.gtMyHeading = function (hdg, mph) {
  if (mph >= 2 && isFinite(hdg)) gtLastHdg = hdg;   // stopped -> hold the last good direction
  if (gtHeadUp && gtLastHdg != null) gtTurnTo(gtLastHdg);
};

Its own header states the design: "the app pushes my cart's course every tick (gtMyHeading). Below 2 mph the course is noise -- the map holds the last good direction."

THE CALL SITE — GroupTrackScreen.kt:486, once per tick for my cart only: gtMyHeading(${node.heading_deg}, ${node.speed_mph}).

⛔ AND HERE IS THE DEFECT, in the app's data. ConvoyViewModel.kt:1334-1335:

speed_mph   = radioMph ?: windowMph,
heading_deg = msg.track?.courseDeg?.toFloat() ?: 0f,

Two independent faults meet there.

  • A missing course becomes due north. ?: 0f substitutes a real, confident bearing for "unknown". A parked radio that sends no ground_track reports 0°. This is exactly the shape CODE RULE 1 exists to prevent — a default standing in for an absent value, with no documented justification. Zero is not "no heading"; it is north.
  • The speed that opens the gate is not the radio's. radioMph is a correct m/s→mph conversion (:1324, ×2.23694) but is null when the radio sends no ground_speed, and the fallback is windowMph — a 60-second window speed derived from position deltas (:1310). Parked, with GPS wander over a full minute, that can sit above 2 mph.

SO THE SIMULATION RUNS LIKE THIS, STATIONARY: windowMph reads, say, 3 — above the page's 2 mph gate. heading_deg alternates between a real jittering course and the ?: 0f fallback whenever a report arrives without a track. Each tick, gtMyHeading(0, 3) then gtMyHeading(187, 3) passes the gate, updates gtLastHdg, clears the 3° deadband easily, and calls map.rotateTo(b, {duration: 800}). The map swings on every position refresh — which is precisely what Fred reported. The page's guards cannot help: it was handed a confident heading and a speed above the gate.

⭐ CORROBORATION ALREADY IN THE CODEBASE. ConvoyEngine.kt:75-76 computes group heading only from carts with speed_mph > 3f before reading heading_deg. So the codebase already treats heading at low speed as untrustworthy — in one place, and not in this one.

fix shape — in the app, not the page. (1) Carry the course as nullable and do not call gtMyHeading at all when it is absent; CODE RULE 1's justification is explicit here, because "no course" is a real state the rider's map must respect. (2) Decide whether the gate should read the radio's speed only, rather than falling back to a 60-second window speed that noise can inflate. (3) Consider aligning the threshold with ConvoyEngine's existing > 3f. The page needs no change.

⚠ ONE THING STILL UNMEASURED: whether a parked radio here actually sends ground_speed/ground_track at all, and what windowMph really reads in the driveway. Both are observable from one recording with the VECHEADING diagnostic, which already logs every 15 s: "VECHEADING my course <hdg> at <mph> mph (held <gtLastHdg>)". That log settles it without a single code change.

⭐⭐ CONFIRMED ON DEVICE 2026-11-20 10:43-10:55 — Droid 1 logcat, VECHEADING (HEADINGPROOF-2026-11-20). 40 lines captured indoors. The fallback path is now observed, not inferred.

1. The course alternates between a real value and exactly 0. 116.0, 116.1 → 0, 0, 0 → 33.3, 33.0, 33.0 → 0, 0, 0 → 3.00, 3.02 → 0, 0 → 207.0 → 0 → 210.4 → 0. Zero is not a drifting value among those; it is the ?: 0f at ConvoyViewModel.kt:1335 firing whenever the radio sends no ground_track.

2. The two populations are separable by their SPEED signature, which proves which branch produced them. Rows carrying a course have all-distinct speeds — 0.4591317, 0.5538075, 0.46920556, 0.534087, 0.6387119 — i.e. fresh radioMph readings. Rows carrying course 0 repeat the same speed across consecutive ticks: 0.027085396 ×3, 0.007882598 ×4, 0.04825069 ×3, 0.04807689 ×3. A speed that does not change for 45 seconds is not a live reading; it is the cached 60-second windowMph of :1310. So no radio track → heading 0 AND windowMph, together, as traced.

3. ⛔ AND gtLastHdg IS STUCK AT 0. Every line reads (held 0), yet vec_carts.js:68 initialises it to null. It can only become 0 if a tick once passed mph >= 2 while carrying course 0 — the fallback firing while the cart was actually moving. Consequence today: tapping "My dir" turns the map due north and holds it there. That is a second user-visible symptom of the same one line.

4. Indoors the page's gate works correctly. Every speed in the capture is 0.0–0.64 mph, below the 2 mph threshold, so gtLastHdg is never updated and nothing rotates. The spin requires motion, which is why it was seen riding and not at the desk: above 2 mph BOTH populations clear the gate, so the sequence becomes (116°, 14 mph) → (0°, 14 mph) → (33°, 13 mph) and the map swings to north and back on alternating refreshes. The 3° deadband cannot damp a 116° alternation.

⭐⭐ CORRECTED BY FRED 11-20 10:56 — THE SOURCE IS THE DROID'S GPS, NOT THE RADIO (HEADINGSOURCE-2026-11-20). "we are recording the track and my tracks a[re] from droid[']s gps. n[o]t radio." Verified, and it makes the defect clearer rather than changing it — both paths read android.location.Location and both discard an honest "unknown":

  • Radio known / group aware — :1030 packs the DROID's fix into the outgoing message: track = loc?.takeIf { it.hasSpeed() && it.hasBearing() }?.let { CotTrack(it.speed, it.bearing) }, sent via onBoardMessage(msg, fromTablet = true). When the droid's GPS has no bearing, track is null — and the round trip then turns null into north at :1335.
  • No radio / Android-only — :1038-1039: val spd = (loc?.speed ?: 0f) * 2.23694f and val hdg = loc?.bearing ?: 0f. Same substitution, second site.
  • No fix at all — :1043: speed_mph = prevMe?.speed_mph ?: 0f, heading_deg = prevMe?.heading_deg ?: 0f. Third site.

⛔ WHY THIS IS THE WHOLE BUG, in one sentence: Android's Location.hasBearing() is false precisely when the course is unknown — at rest or near it — so it is a reliable signal that the app throws away and replaces with a confident 0°. The log's two populations are exactly this: hasBearing() true gives fresh distinct speeds and a real course; false gives windowMph and 0°.

⭐ AND THE PAGE WAS WRITTEN EXPECTING THE HONEST ANSWER. gtMyHeading already guards with isFinite(hdg), and gtTurnTo is only called when gtLastHdg != null. So if the app passed NaN — or simply did not call gtMyHeading when the bearing is absent — the page would hold the last good direction, which is the designed behaviour. The fix is to stop lying to a function that already handles the truth.

⛔⛔⛔ THE "RESOLVED" PARAGRAPH BELOW IS WRONG — CORRECTED 11-20 11:15 (HEADINGTRUE-2026-11-20). It claims the radio drove the cart indoors. It cannot have. ConvoyViewModel.kt:220: private val tabletHasGps: Boolean by lazy { TabletPosition.hasGps(appContext) }, and TabletPosition.kt:55-58 is allProviders.contains(GPS_PROVIDER) — a hardware capability check, computed once and cached by lazy. Droid 1 has a GPS chip, so tabletHasGps is permanently true, skipMine (:1266) is therefore always true for my cart, and the radio's CoT never moves it. :1261's radio-driven branch is unreachable on any device with a GPS chip.

⭐ THE ACTUAL SOURCE: the tablet's FUSED feed. ONEFEED-2026-10-19, Google fused location, HIGH_ACCURACY, every 3 s. TabletPosition.kt:12-13 records why fused was chosen and also explains the indoor data: "Droid 1's chip reported 'no fix' for minutes with 13 satellites in view; fused kept giving 10-25 m positions." So indoors the tablet HAD positions (which is why the track recorded and the speeds were non-zero) but frequently no bearing at a crawl. The 0s are loc.hasBearing() false on the TABLET — the original reading — and the clustered 236.9× / 309.9× / 116× courses are fused drift smoothed into a stable course for minutes, not a carried radio.

⭐ AND FRED'S RULE IS ALREADY IMPLEMENTED (11-20 11:15: "radio is not used f[or] gps recording [or] map writes. droid device is used. nothing should be radio unless [the droid] has no gps."). The droid wins everywhere: skipMine for the cart, and onRadioPosition double-guarded by state == RECORDING && useRadioGps (ConvoyGpsService:282) plus gpsService?.useRadioGps == true at the call site (:1277).

⚠ ONE GENUINE FINDING FALLS OUT: hasGps answers "is there a GPS chip", but it is used where the question is "is there a usable fix". They are different, and the difference makes the !tabletHasGps fallback dead code on every tablet with a chip. Whether that matters depends on whether a radio fallback is ever wanted on such a device — a decision for Fred, not a defect on its own, given his rule above says the droid should win.

⚠ METHOD: four readings of this defect, three wrong. Each time a consistent story was accepted before being tested against the code that gates it — the stale comment, the throttled log, then the source rule. The gate is what must be read first, every time.

⭐⭐⭐ RESOLVED 11-20 11:11 BY ONE FACT FROM FRED (HEADINGCLOSED-2026-11-20): "I walked [the] radio around but n[o]t the device." That separates the two sources in the log and the chain closes.

INDOORS THE TABLET HAS NO FIX, so tabletHasGps is false, and ConvoyViewModel.kt:1261 then DROPS the tablet feed — if (fromTablet && myNum != null && !tabletHasGps && commLink.health.value.talking && nowMine - myRadioFixMs < 45_000L) return, commented "the radio is driving my cart". So every row of the 10:26-11:04 capture came from the radio's CoT:

  • Radio moving → it sends ground_track → a real course, clustering because it was carried one way then set down: 236.9× for five minutes, then 309.9×, 115.9×, 33.0×, 3.0×, 45.9×.
  • Radio still → no ground_track → msg.track null → ?: 0f at :1335 → exactly 0.

That explains the overlapping speeds that defeated the earlier readings (0 at 0.4596 mph beside 116° at 0.4591): speed and course arrive in the SAME radio report, and only the course goes missing. It was never hasBearing() on the tablet, and never the centring logic — which the log also rules out directly: zero onBearing lines in 38 minutes, so the map never rotated at all during the capture (My dir was off).

⛔ THE DEFECT, STATED FINALLY: a stopped radio reports no course, and the app calls that north. With My dir on, every refresh then swings the map between north and the last real bearing. Fred's driveway test had exactly these conditions — weak tablet fix, radio moving intermittently — which is why it spun there. Compounding it, gtLastHdg latches on brief speed spikes above 2 mph: the capture shows it jump from 0 to 15.032137 between two logged lines at 11:00:07, in a call the 15-second log throttle never printed.

⭐⭐ FRED'S OWN OBSERVATION, 11-20 11:14 (HEADINGFRED-2026-11-20) — which answers the question in the paragraph below and matches the diagnosis: "it spun every time[,] settled on my device while driving. it never settled and spun as soon as it stopped[,] it started again."

So: driving → settles. Stopped → spins, every time. Exactly what the two sources predict — moving, the tablet holds a fix, skipMine (:1266) ignores the radio and a real bearing drives the map; stopped, the tablet loses its fix, :1261 hands the cart to the radio, and a stationary radio sends no course which ?: 0f turns into north. The symptom is therefore NOT a road-speed defect, and the fix in C28-26356 addresses it directly.

STATUS: LEFT OPEN at Fred's instruction — "leave the item open[,] I will have to drive to rep[ort]. but n[ot] now." A road capture with My dir on is the confirmation; it is not blocking the fix.

⚠ STILL UNCONFIRMED, and it changes the priority: whether this also occurs at 40-60 mph. With a solid tablet fix, skipMine at :1266 ignores the radio entirely and the tablet's own bearing drives the cart, which should be clean. So this may be specifically a no-tablet-fix defect. One road capture with My dir on answers it.

⭐ METHOD NOTE: the log throttle hid the mechanism. gtMyHeading is called every tick; the diagnostic prints at most once per 15 s. Three readings of this defect were built on one sample in five. A diagnostic that samples must say so where it is read, or it will be mistaken for the signal.

⛔⛔ REOPENED BY FRED 11-20 10:59 (HEADINGOPEN-2026-11-20) — the paragraph below overstates what the capture proves, and the in-motion cause is NOT established. Fred: "I am driving at 40-60 mph[;] no gps point should [be without] a heading." Correct, and decisive: Android reports a bearing reliably at road speed, so hasBearing() is true throughout a real ride. Neither mechanism traced here produces a 0 at 40-60 mph, so the 10:43-10:55 indoor capture cannot be the defect seen while riding.

AND INDOORS THERE ARE TWO CANDIDATE SOURCES THIS LOG CANNOT SEPARATE. ConvoyViewModel.kt:1266, ONEFEED-2026-10-19: val skipMine = !fromTablet && num == myNum && tabletHasGps.

  • tabletHasGps true → the radio's own CoT is skipped and only the tablet feed moves my cart, so the 0s are tablet fixes with hasBearing() false — normal at 0 mph.
  • tabletHasGps false → the radio path drives my cart, and the radio sent no track, so the 0s are radio-sourced via ?: 0f.

Both fit the capture exactly; both vanish at road speed. The log does not say which, and the earlier paragraphs here read as though it did.

WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT: the same logcat | grep VECHEADING after a REAL drive with "My dir" on. At speed, whether any 0 appears at all is the answer; the paired speed values then name the source (distinct = fresh fix, repeated = cached windowMph).

⚠ WHAT DOES NOT DEPEND ON THAT ANSWER: the three ?: 0f sites are wrong either way — see C28-26356. A point with no GPS must not display a heading. Fix that first; it is correct independently, and it may remove the symptom as a side effect.

⚠ METHOD NOTE, third over-confidence on this one defect in one morning: C28-26351 claimed the feature was unbuilt (wrong), then C28-26354 claimed the indoor capture explained the in-motion spin (wrong). Both times the error was the same shape — a consistent story accepted before it was tested against the condition that actually mattered. Fred supplied that condition both times.

⭐ THE UNMEASURED ITEM IS NOW CLOSED. The radio does send a track, intermittently; when it does not, the app substitutes north and a stale window speed. No further observation is needed before the fix.

Traced 2026-11-20 against the 2.8 branch (HEADINGTRACE-2026-11-20) at Fred's instruction to simulate rather than patch. Supersedes C28-26351's diagnosis and the REFINES card's claim that the feature needed building; both are flagged in place.

⛔ 2.8a C28-26352 · PAUSEUI — the paused state is cloudy on screen, and the service already specifies it correctly

Fred 11-20 10:47: "when paused the recover sequence is cloudy. [The button] should read paused with two options end or resume[,] no back and fo[r]th with buttons."

⭐ THE SPECIFICATION ALREADY EXISTS, IN THE SERVICE'S OWN HEADER. ConvoyGpsService.kt:30-34, verbatim:

States: IDLE → RECORDING → PAUSED → RECORDING → STOPPED

Notification actions mirror the on-screen REC button:
  RECORDING: PAUSE | END
  PAUSED:    RESUME | END

AND THE NOTIFICATION IMPLEMENTS IT EXACTLY (ConvoyGpsService.kt:520-531): in State.PAUSED the title reads "GPS Recording Paused", the text reads "Tap RESUME to continue recording", and it offers precisely two actions, RESUME and END. That is what Fred is asking for, already built — in the notification.

⛔ SO THE DEFECT IS A DIVERGENCE FROM A STATED CONTRACT. The header says the notification "mirrors the on-screen REC button." The notification is right; the on-screen control is not. The screen drifted from its own specification, which is why this reads as cloudy rather than broken: both halves exist, they just disagree. The on-screen side runs through RecordingState (convoy/RecordingState.kt, moved there by ONEMAPHOST-2026-10-25) and is switched in GroupTrackScreen.kt around :1763-1777.

fix shape — make the on-screen control mirror the notification, as the header already requires: when PAUSED, the panel reads PAUSED and offers exactly two choices, RESUME and END. No toggling button that changes meaning between taps. The notification needs no change; it is the reference.

⛔ 2.8a C28-26353 · AILOCATION — adding a location in the AI create must select it, and the user must see the selection

Fred 11-20 10:47: "adding a location in the ai automated create should select the location and requires a reflection by the user."

WHERE THE LOCATION LIVES. The AI search takes its location as the anchor on RouteExplorer.Request — anchorLat / anchorLon (RouteExplorer.kt:42-43), passed through to the shared engine at :56, and everything downstream keys off it: selectionTable (:111), selectionBreakdown (:118), connectedMiles (:130), connectedLines (:135), assess (:138), explore (:151). The aim ring's radius and the trailhead check distance are at :34 (RELEASEF-2026-10-08 / AIFAILCHECK-2026-11-03).

TWO REQUIREMENTS, and they are separate. (1) Adding a location must actually select it — set the anchor the search runs against. (2) The selection must be reflected back to the user before the search runs, so a rider can see which location the AI is about to search from. This is the same principle Fred set for the ride time zone on 11-08: "awareness is the key" — the system may decide, but the rider is told what was decided.

⚠ NOT YET LOCATED: the panel that sets the anchor. ConvoyExploreScreen.kt is a thin wrapper and RouteExplorer.kt is the 159-line engine, so the location-entry UI is elsewhere — the search panel in the Route flow. That is the first thing to find before this is scoped, and it must be found by where-used rather than by guessing at a filename.

Measured 2026-11-20 against the 2.8 branch (RIDEDEFECTS2-2026-11-20). Reported by Fred 10:47. The anchor plumbing is measured; the entry UI is not yet located.

⭐ REFINES C28-26351 · HEADINGSPIN — the required behaviour, from Fred 11-20 10:48

⛔⛔ CORRECTED 2026-11-20 — THE CENTRAL CLAIM IN THIS CARD IS WRONG. The heading-up feature IS implemented. See C28-26354 · HEADINGTRACE below for the traced mechanism; read that card instead of this one.

Fred: "will it move[] panel when it is clicked so bearing is up? needs t[o] be fixed."

REQUIRED BEHAVIOUR: tapping My dir rotates the map so the rider's heading is at the top of the view, and holds it there. Tapping the compass (view.north) returns to north-up. So this is a build, not a repair — gtSetHeadingUp is still the placeholder declared at vecmap_main.html:89 and confirmed unimplemented in vec_view.js.

⛔ AND THE SPIN IS PROBABLY EXPLAINED BY THE SAME THING. A heading-up map driven by raw GPS course-over-ground will spin when the vehicle is stopped, because course is meaningless at zero speed and jitters freely. Fred tested in his driveway. So the implementation needs, from the first line: a speed gate below which the bearing holds its last value, and smoothing above it. Without both, the feature reproduces the reported defect by design. The only speed test anywhere near this today is ConvoyViewModel.kt:1029 — loc?.takeIf { it.hasSpeed() && it.hasBearing() } — and that gates the CoT report payload, not the view.

Written 2026-11-20 (RIDEDEFECTS2-2026-11-20). Supersedes C28-26351's open question about the spin's source: the mechanism is named, though the threshold values are a decision Fred has not made yet.

⛔ 2.8a C28-26351 · HEADINGSPIN — "My dir" spins the view continuously, and the button it is on was never implemented

⛔⛔ CORRECTED 2026-11-20 — THE CENTRAL CLAIM IN THIS CARD IS WRONG. The heading-up feature IS implemented. See C28-26354 · HEADINGTRACE below for the traced mechanism; read that card instead of this one.

Fred 11-20 10:44: "me my direction as top of the view spins the view continuously."

⚠ THE MEASUREMENT CONTRADICTS THE OBVIOUS READING. The button is wired on the Kotlin side: GroupTrack.kt:230 renders "My dir" / "N up" and fires actions["view.heading"]; GroupTrackScreen.kt:3144 flips GtMapView.headingUp and calls gtSetHeadingUp(<bool>) in the page. But that page function does not exist. vecmap_main.html:89 lists 'gtSet3D', 'gtSetHeadingUp' with the comment "the 3D and orientation buttons -- placeholders"; gtSet3D was later implemented in vec_view.js, and gtSetHeadingUp was not — that file says so in terms: "gtSetHeadingUp stays a placeholder (VECPORT todo) until the heading work." There is no setBearing, rotateTo or bearing assignment anywhere in vec_view.js, vec_carts.js or vecmap_main.html, and GtMapView.bearing (MapEngine.kt:55) is documented as read from the page for the compass needle, not written to it.

SO THE SPIN IS NOT THE HEADING FEATURE BEHAVING BADLY — the heading feature is not built. The likeliest source is MapLibre's own rotation, which is enabled by default and carries inertia: a two-finger gesture rotates the map and keeps rotating. Fred confirmed on 11-19 that pinch zoom and two-finger tilt both work, so rotate gestures are live. That is a hypothesis, not a measurement.

⚠ ONE OBSERVATION SEPARATES THEM, and it costs nothing: does the view spin without pressing "My dir"? If yes, it is gesture rotation with inertia and the button is a red herring (and still a stub). If it only spins after pressing it, something is reaching the map that this measurement has not found, and the next place to look is the shim — the iPad's bridge is a Proxy that forwards any name, and the Android WebView call is wrapped in try{}catch{}, so a missing function fails silently on both platforms.

⛔ EITHER WAY, A SECOND FINDING STANDS ON ITS OWN: "My dir" is a live button on the right-hand panel that does nothing, and it has been shipping since VIEWHOUSE-2026-10-30. That is the category the 11-19 level-2 pass exists to catch, and it was not on the list of seven.

Measured 2026-11-20 against the 2.8 branch (RIDEDEFECTS-2026-11-20). Reported by Fred from the driveway record test on 2.8a. Root cause of the spin NOT established — see the observation above.

The viewer is 23 files.

GESTURES — nothing of ours to build. vec_ports.js gtGestures(on) enables/disables dragPan, dragRotate, touchZoomRotate, touchPitch, doubleClickZoom and boxZoom, and is called with false ONLY while drawing an area box. All six are MapLibre's and on by default. gtEvLL returns null when touches.length > 1, so a two-finger gesture never registers as a feature tap. Pan is proven; pinch and two-finger tilt are enabled but untested, and tilt needs testing with 3D ON because gtSet3D(false) means "flat, tilt 0". On the simulator: pinch is Option-drag, tilt is Option-Shift-drag.

    ⭐ IT PERSISTS FOR REAL: GtTracks.insertWaypoint is ALREADY SHARED (GtTracks.kt:287) and Android's SpatialDbManager.insertWaypoint is only a front door to it (STEP15CDE-SWITCH-2026-11-10).   3. ⛔⛔ GESTURES — CORRECTED 11-19 13:05, AND I HAD IT BACKWARDS TWICE.
    Fred: "on taps route build in a function not launched fro[m] a map touch ... I think y[o]u may be confusing the route record state and it[s] imp[a]ct on touches but that is handled b[y] the existing processes." He is right, and the VIEWER'S OWN CODE says so — vec_spatial.js:252-266:
    if (!window.gtOneMapRoute) window.__routeMode = false;
    if (window.__routeMode && window.Android) { ... Android.onMapTap(...); return; }
    — then, only after that block returns, the feature search (routes, tracks, trails).
    So onMapTap and onProximityTap are sent ONLY while __routeMode is on, and that flag is forced false unless gtOneMapRoute is set. An ORDINARY MAP TOUCH NEVER REACHES THEM; it falls through to the feature taps. Route building is a MODE entered deliberately (setRouteMode), and the state's effect on touches is already handled in the page. The two route gestures therefore belong to the Route+ process, NOT to B.
    ⛔ MY TWO ERRORS, both from reading the Kotlin and not the page. First I called these gestures "not features" and left them out. Then, told to include them, I read onMapTap's Kotlin body — whose second branch really does snap, add a vertex and draw a build line — and announced it "the HEAVIEST item in B", building the day's estimate on it. The branch is real; it is simply unreachable from a plain touch. The lesson is the one already written down: the Kotlin says what WOULD happen, the page says WHETHER it happens. I grepped vecmap_main.html alone and got zeros for every method, including onViewportChanged, which demonstrably works — the viewer is a FOLDER of 23 files (vec_spatial.js, vec_ports.js, vec_carts.js, vec_compass.js), and one more grep would have said so.
    WHAT SURVIVES AS B GESTURES:
    · onMapLongPress — waypoint creation (item 2 above).
    · onAreaSelected (vec_ports.js, gated by gtDrawOn) — the AREA service; downloads are E/F.
    · onRouteModeOffRequested — the PAGE→HOST callback is DEAD on the vector viewer (zero references in all 23 files; it was the Leaflet badge tap). ⚠ BUT ROUTE-MODE-OFF ITSELF IS VERY MUCH ALIVE, which Fred asked to be checked (13:07: "on route mode off has impact in the tap functionality. lets be sure it still exists maybe under a different name. tap adds point to a route [i]n planning and it opens map[] features when route planning is off."). He is right that it matters and right that it has another name:
      · setRouteMode(on) lives in vec_spatial.js:252 and in vecmap_main.html's stub list, and is turned OFF from EIGHT host call sites — GroupTrackScreen 940, 1030, 1351 and RouteModule 146, 275, 860, 866.
      · the viewer also resets it on EVERY click: if (!window.gtOneMapRoute) window.__routeMode = false; — so a click outside ONE MAP's route context clears it even if the host forgot. window.gtOneMapRoute = true (GroupTrackScreen:1034) is what lets ONE MAP have route mode at all.
      · ⚠ AND IT HAS BITTEN BEFORE. vec_ports.js:5 records it: "renderPins / clearPins (the missing clearPins left the map in route mode)". The map got STUCK in route mode once, and the fix was adding clearPins. Fred's worry is a failure with history, not a hypothetical — which is exactly why the per-click reset is there.
      · ON THE iPAD TODAY: the viewer has setRouteMode, but nothing in iosMain calls it and gtOneMapRoute is never set, so __routeMode is permanently false and every tap reaches the feature search. The iPad CANNOT get stuck. When Route+ comes to the iPad, that per-click reset must come with it, or the iPad inherits the 10-31 bug.
      · ⭐⭐ A THIRD MECHANISM, and Fred named it from memory (13:09: "the route on/off mode is recorded in the m[a]p persi[st]ance json and may be read from the[re] when maps refresh"). CONFIRMED in the shared code: GtMapState.kt:87 — data class GtRouteState(val open: Boolean, val status: String, val draftName: String), whose own comment reads "open = route-add mode active". It hangs off GtMapSnapshot.routeState, written at :175 and read back at :227. So route-add mode PERSISTS IN THE MAP JSON and is restored on refresh — in the SHARED GtMapState, which the iPad already uses.
      · ⚠ AND ONE MAP DID NOT REMOVE IT — ONE MAP IS WHAT STARTED WRITING IT. Fred 13:10: "I believe we removed that w[hen] we went to one map. everything got simpler." Measured, and it went the other way. GroupTrackScreen.kt:346-347 passes GtRoute.routeState() into the snapshot, and the comment on that very line reads "GTROUTE1-2026-10-23: ONE MAP's RouteState (was null)" — so the argument WAS null before ONE MAP and ONE MAP populated it. It is also load-bearing rather than vestigial: RouteModule.kt:836 is recoveryDetected.value = MapStateStore.readMap(rideKey()).routeState?.open == true — ROUTE-BUILD RECOVERY, which is what makes an interrupted route reopenable. The ONE MAP simplification was real but landed elsewhere; this field gained use. Recorded because a wrong belief here would have had the iPad safely ignoring a field that is actually live on Android.
      · ⚠ A LATENT DIVERGENCE IS SITTING IN THE SEEDED DATA. GtVecMap.ios.kt:166 reads that same JSON and takes only the bbox: GtMapState(IosFiles(), IosImportPaths()).readMap(GtMapState.rideKey()).bbox. It never looks at routeState. The iPad's dataset came from Droid 1's snapshot (11-16), so if that snapshot holds routeState.open = true, ANDROID RESTORES ROUTE MODE FROM IT AND THE iPAD IGNORES IT — one file, two behaviours. Harmless today (no Route+ on the iPad) and exactly the kind of thing that reads as a port defect weeks later. Worth checking the seed's actual value before it is anyone's mystery.
      · ⛔ SO ROUTE+ ON THE iPAD NEEDS ALL THREE TOGETHER: honour routeState.open on restore, accept setRouteMode from the host, and carry the per-click reset. Honouring the persisted flag WITHOUT the reset is the 10-31 stuck-in-route-mode bug pre-loaded from disk at startup — worse than the original, because the user never did anything to cause it.
      · So: do not port the dead callback; DO carry the three mechanisms that replaced it.
  4. THE CART TAP — in B (Fred 12:58). onMarkerTapped(nodeId) finds the node in convoyState.value.nodes and opens its panel. I had filed this under level G because the state behind it is radio-fed; Fred's point stands — it is a map feature interaction and belongs with the others. What that means concretely: the TAP and the PANEL are B; the CARTS in it come from the mesh, so on the iPad the panel opens on an empty or seeded node list until G. Build it to Fred's 12:26 standard (look and feel, machinery backfilled) rather than deferring the whole interaction.
  5. PLUMBING, not B. onBearing, onMapReady, onMapBoundsReady, onViewportChanged (the only one wired today).

⭐⭐ THE ORGANISING PRINCIPLE OF LEVEL B — Fred 11-19 13:00: "agreed 100% area selected becomes a service. here we are defining that service."

B DEFINES THE SERVICES. D THROUGH G IMPLEMENT THEM. That is the rule, and it resolves what looked like a scoping problem a minute earlier. An interaction does not wait for the thing it launches: the interaction is certified at B by defining the SERVICE it calls, and the later level fills that service in. So:

  • onAreaSelected → an AREA service. B defines it — what an area selection hands over (the four bounds, and what it means to take one). The map DOWNLOADS behind it are E/F.
  • onMarkerTapped → a CART service. B defines it and opens the panel; the carts come from the mesh at G.
  • onShare / onExport on ArtifactDetailPanel → already lambdas. B defines what they promise; the iOS EMAIL half is D ("including ios email solution").
  • onMapTap's route building → the ROUTE service. GtRoute (2,674 lines) and RouteManager (296, one platform touch) are the implementation and they largely exist already; B defines how the map hands a tap to them.

⭐ WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT CUT, not just a convenient one. It is the same line the whole port has been drawn on: SEAMRULE-2026-11-19 says a panel that READS or TOGGLES host state takes a lambda, and anything that ACQUIRES or ADMITS a platform resource stays with the host. A service definition IS that lambda, named and given a shape. It also means B is finishable: nine interactions, each ending at a defined service, with no level-B item blocked on a radio, the internet or a file. And it is why the INGRESS taxonomy put A to C on the simulator — defining a service needs no ingress; honouring one does.

⚠ THE DISCIPLINE THIS REQUIRES. A defined-but-unimplemented service must SAY SO when called, as every level-A stub does (the status strip reports "not built on the iPad yet"). A service that silently does nothing is indistinguishable from a broken interaction — which is the whole reason TrackGrounds got a LOUD no-op rather than a quiet one this morning (TRACKGROUNDS-2026-11-19).

SEVEN MESSAGES, NOT NINE. The four feature taps, the long press, the cart tap and the area select — all already delivered to the iPad by the SHIM, all doing nothing. The two route gestures belong to the Route+ mode, and onRouteModeOffRequested is dead on this viewer. So B is answering SEVEN messages that already arrive.

⭐⭐ ArtifactDetailPanel IS THE BEST-SEAMED PANEL IN THE PROJECT, and this is the measurement that says so. 665 lines, and its signature is already ELEVEN nullable lambdas — onLoadDetail, onLoadAliases, onRename, onDelete, onShare, onExport, onShowNotes, onAddRide, onCreateRideFromTrack, onBuildFromRecipe, onDownloadMaps, onDownloadCorridor, onChangeType, onDeleteAlias — each with a CODE RULE 1 justification in place ("Nullable like its siblings: a screen that does not offer it passes nothing"). The panel already knows how to be given its world. Its buttons: DETAILS, ALIASES, RENAME, DELETE, SHARE, EXPORT, SAVE MAPS, CREATE A RIDE, CONTINUE, CANCEL.

THE COUPLINGS, measured — there are only THREE:

  1. fitWebView: android.webkit.WebView? — a PARAMETER, used ONCE (line 188) to call triggerViewportUpdate(). Becomes onRefreshMap: () -> Unit; the iPad already has that exact call in its js().
  2. SpatialDbManager ×3 (lines 163, 185, 196: trailheadsNear, insertWaypoint, routeNameExists) — ALL inside the CREATE A RIDE path, which is ALREADY gated behind the nullable onCreateRideFromTrack. So a host that does not offer ride creation never reaches them.
  3. android.graphics.Color.parseColor(hex) (line 628) — needs a shared hex→Color. Six lines.

⭐ AND LocalContext IS NOT A COUPLING AT ALL. val ctx = LocalContext.current at line 86 is DEAD — declared once, used nowhere in 665 lines. Deleting that one line removes the import and the dependency. Found by grepping for \bctx\b rather than assuming the declaration meant a use.

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE WORK. B is not a 665-line port with a dozen seams; it is one WebView parameter turned into a lambda, one colour parser, one dead line deleted, and a ride-creation sub-flow that is already optional. The iPad side then needs the four tap messages routed (the SHIM already delivers them) and onLoadDetail / onLoadAliases answered from the spatial database — which IosSearchDb already opens and reads through the shared GtRead.

⚠ WHAT IS STILL FRED'S DECISION, and it gates part of B. EXPORTPICK (C28-26321) is marked PROVISIONAL on Fred's own words of 11-17 15:17: "let me finish my testing and I will return with a better overview of functions." What IS settled there: EXPORT writes and stops, SHARE is REDUNDANT and comes off the panel, EMAIL lives in the picker. So the panel's button SET is not final, and the SHARE button measured above is one Fred has already said should go. Do not port the button set as it stands without raising this. The EMAIL half is level D anyway ("including ios email solution").

comment 11-19 12:55: scoped, not built. Level A is complete and signed off; this is the next build once the button-set question is answered or deliberately deferred.

⭐ 2.8b — THE RIDE IS FRIDAY 2026-11-20, MORNING (Fred 11-19 12:53) C28-26343 · SIDEBYSIDE — 2.8a on Droid 1, two GPX files against Droid 2

Release: 2.8a build / 2.8b certification. Fred 10:57, a DATED COMMITMENT, not a backlog item.

⭐ STATUS 11-19 12:53 — THE BUILD HALF IS DONE; THE RIDE IS TOMORROW MORNING. Fred 12:53: "ride takes place tomorrow morning." So the 10:57 wording "by end of day" applied to the BUILD and the DEPLOY, both of which landed: 2.8a = 55704d1a5 was built at 11:27 and installed on Droid 1 at 11:28:54 (versionCode 29321690), with Droid 2 untouched on 2.7f. That commit carries BOTH the TrackRules move and MYCARTSNAP, and nothing committed since touches the Android recorder, so the APK on Droid 1 is the right one to ride.

⚠ THE TWO THINGS STILL OWED BEFORE THE RIDE, neither of them a build:

  • A two-minute recording in the driveway. Three things in that APK have never run on hardware: TrackGrounds.assertSet at record-start, MyCartProcess with fill = true for the first time ever (MYCARTSNAP), and the MY DEVICE GPS n lamp at 16 characters where 12 fitted. One short recording plus grep -i "TRACKGROUNDS\|TRACKRULES" ~/Downloads/gt_logs/droid1_1119_1128.log answers all three. source = DbLineSource (set at startup) is the good answer; IS UNSET means the ride would certify nothing.
  • "Create trails from tracks" on Droid 1 BETWEEN THE LEGS. Measured 11-19: ConvoyGpsService contains NO rider-trail call of any kind — the only triggers are that dialog (MapScreens.kt:392) and the track-import screen. Without that step the return leg has nothing within SNAP_M (50 m), Droid 1 writes raw exactly like Droid 2, and the ride proves nothing.

Fred's words: "make a note to regen 2.8a model and deploy to droid 1 by end of day. I wan[t] to record track with dr[oi]d 1 and droid 2 together. I want to use 2.8a on d[r]oid1[,] 2.7f on dorid 2 and record two sep[a]rate gpx files [we] can evaluate. trail databases are duplicated."

⭐⭐ WHY THIS IS THE CERTIFICATION, NOT JUST A TEST. Today's TrackRules move substitutes Math.cos / Math.hypot for GtMath.cos / GtMath.hypot on the code path that decides what every recorded track looks like (TRACKRULES-2026-10-19: the snap rules, interpolated road points, the hold-at-intersection rule). GtMath exists precisely for this (GTMATH2-2026-11-10, "Android's math on every platform") and MATHPROBE certifies its functions on seeded and real inputs — but two devices riding the same trail with duplicated databases and producing two GPX files is stronger evidence than any synthetic probe: it exercises the rules, the snapping, the timing and the writer together, on real GPS noise.

THE SETUP, as Fred specified it:
· Droid 1 — 2.8a (today's build, with TrackRules moved to shared + GtMath)
· Droid 2 — 2.7f (the comparison baseline, with Android's own Math)
· trail databases duplicated across both, so the snap targets are identical
· recorded together, one ride, two GPX files
· then evaluate the two files against each other

⛔ CORRECTED 11-19 11:05 — THIS IS NOT A GtMath TEST. I had it backwards this morning. GtMath.android.kt delegates every function straight to java.lang.Math, and GTMATH-2026-11-10 says so outright: "Android: GtMath IS java.lang.Math — exactly what 2.7e and every certified step already call. Android does not change by a bit." The Math.cos / Math.hypot substitutions are therefore bit-for-bit identical on Droid, provably, before anyone drives anywhere. Math.toRadians went to GtJava.toRadians (deg * 0.017453292519943295, proven on Droid 1 to equal java.lang.Math.toRadians) and NOT to deg * PI / 180.0, which is a third, differently-rounded variant. So a divergence between the two GPX files is a REFACTOR defect — something moved wrong — never a math defect. GtMath against Apple's libm is the iPad's test, and that comes later (Fred 11:00: iPad certs follow when a test instance is deployed there).

⛔⛔ THREE-WAY CERTIFICATION — DROID 2 MUST NOT BE TOUCHED. Fred 11-19 11:22: "droid 2 ha[s] [to] stay on 2.7f for certification[:] old code droid, new code droid, and 2.8b Mac." Droid 2 = 2.7f = THE ORACLE. It is the only record of what the old code does, and it cannot be regenerated once overwritten.

THE INSTALL HAZARD, measured 11-19 11:21. Since 10-13 BOTH tablets plug into the Mac, and deploy_v3.sh:45 reads DEVS=$(adb devices ...) — it installs on EVERY connected device unless given a serial. So:

  • ⛔ NEVER gt_build_v2.sh --deploy during this certification — it calls deploy_v3.sh with no serial and would put 2.8a on Droid 2, destroying the oracle.
  • ⛔ NEVER deploy_v3.sh latest without a serial, for the same reason.
  • ✅ Build plain (bash "$GT_CMDS/gt_build_v2.sh", no flag — it does not install), then bash "$GT_CMDS/deploy_v3.sh" <build> <DROID-1-SERIAL>.
  • Unplugging Droid 2 for the duration is the belt-and-braces version, and costs nothing.

⭐ WHAT THE RIDE ACTUALLY MEASURES, after MYCARTSNAP-2026-11-19. Fred reversed MYCARTRAW-2026-10-19 himself at 11:06 ("my return track should not stay raw it should snap to the outbound trail created by my track recording"), so MyCartProcess now takes the route / trail rules. With the flip on Droid 1 only, the two devices stop being a before/after pair and become truth and rules side by side for the whole 98 miles:

  • Droid 2 (2.7f, raw) — ground truth. Where he actually drove, both legs.
  • Droid 1 (2.8a, snapped) — what the rules did with it. Snaps to published trail wherever the duplicated databases have it, and to his own rider trail on the return where they do not.

This is a STRONGER certification of the snap rules than two raw tracks, and it costs nothing extra on the drive. Droid 1 is only raw on virgin ground — which is exactly where RiderTrailWriter creates the trail (source 'rider', "the ground you rode where no trail is published") that the return leg then snaps to. Fred 11:03: "tracks write creates a trail if the track is overground with no trails."

THE ROUTE TIER — BY DESIGN, AND NOT IN PLAY HERE. Fred 11:09: "a route is a preplanned item and has n[o] bearing on my trails. there will be no rout[e] in this instance. all that means is if a route was planned it[s] course takes precedence over known trails." A route is the rider's DECLARED INTENT, so it outranks known trails on purpose. There is no route in this test, so the gate never fires. Recorded here only so the tier order is not mistaken for a defect when someone reads the rules later. Measured at the tier gate: if (shown != null) return Fill(listOf(a, b), "STRAIGHT", emptyList()) — GAPFILL-2026-10-20, "off the displayed route = straight (no trails)". With a route on the map the rules use that route ALONE and never reach the trail lookup, so the outbound trail is ignored and the return track is written as straight joins. With no route shown, the tier is tracksNear (which finds the outbound TRACK) falling through to trail() (which finds the rider TRAIL) — either path is the outbound geometry, which is what Fred asked for at 11:08.

HOW TO READ THE TWO FILES. The devices have different GPS chips, so nothing will be byte-identical and should not be expected to be. On the OUTBOUND leg both are near-raw over virgin ground, so that leg is the apples-to-apples check. On the RETURN leg the comparison is deliberately asymmetric: Droid 2 shows where he drove, Droid 1 shows where the rules put him. A Droid 1 return point that sits ON the outbound line where Droid 2 wandered off it is the rules WORKING. A Droid 1 return point that snapped to the wrong line, or that stayed off the outbound line entirely, is the defect.

OWED TO MAKE IT POSSIBLE: regen the 2.8a build and install it on Droid 1. Confirm what "2.7f" is on Droid 2 — the newest tag measured on 11-18 was 2.7e-2026-11-11f, so the name needs pinning before the comparison is recorded, or the evaluation will not say which baseline it beat.

ACTIVE 2.8b — FIXED SAME DAY C28-26341 · FILTERTICK — the ticks looked identical on and off

Release: 2.8b. Fred found it on the iPad within minutes of the first launch.

Fred 09:25: "checks for sel[e]ction on filter type for motorized a[nd] trail types lo[ok] the same for on or off. check box. the same color as box background?" — and he was right about the cause as well as the symptom.

MEASURED: Gt.accent and Gt.dim are BOTH GtScheme.panelText (GtPanel.kt:82 and :84), and boxColors() = CheckboxDefaults.colors(checkedColor = Gt.accent, uncheckedColor = Gt.dim) — so checked and unchecked were the same value. checkmarkColor was never set, so it fell back to Material's onPrimary, which GtTheme sets to the PANEL background. "The same color as box background" was literally true.

⭐ accent == dim IS DELIBERATE (STD1C-2026-10-24: dim/accent/good/warn/bad all read the text colour "until a use is given its own colour for a reason"). This was that reason, so the ROLES were left alone and the control stopped asking two identical roles to carry a distinction. The fix is STD1H-2026-10-24's own rule — "a SELECTION shows a check mark, never a coloured box": OFF is an outlined empty box, ON is a check mark in the text colour.

⚠ STILL OPEN, AND IT IS THE SAME ROOT: Gt.good, Gt.warn and Gt.bad are ALSO all panelText. Anywhere a screen uses those three to mean green / amber / red they render identically. E·Status is the obvious next one to look at. And the condition is not iPad-specific: GroupTrackScreen.kt:3055 passes mapType = if (hudOnDark) "SAT" else "VEC", and forMap sends anything that is not SAT/HYB to the topo scheme — so accent == dim on the Droid too. Whether it READS wrong on Android is Fred's call from Droid 1; it is not asserted here as an Android defect.

OPENED 2026-11-19 — FOUND WHILE PORTING THE LAUNCH LAYER (C28-26333)

ACTIVE 2.8b — SILENT ON IPAD C28-26333 · STARTJOBS — three startup jobs run from a composable, not from the startup road

Release: 2.8b, and it is a latent defect on Android too.

MEASURED 11-19 while sizing the A–I + M port. GroupTrackLaunchLayer (GroupTrack.kt) carries a LaunchedEffect(Unit) that runs three startup jobs: GtDbAudit.repair(ctx.applicationContext), GtFileHome.resetOnce(ctx.applicationContext) and GtBackup.autoCheck(ctx.applicationContext).

NONE of the three is in housekeeping on either platform. The shared GtStartupHousekeeping.run does jobPalette, jobSchemaConverge and jobManifestSweep; Android's convoy/StartupHousekeeping.kt only delegates to it with GtHkAndroid(ctx). Measured: housekeeping=0 for all three. GtDbAudit already exists in shared (6 files) and is simply never called from the road.

WHY IT MATTERS FOR 2.8b: the iPad reaches the map through the startup road (GtIosStartup → GtStartupHousekeeping → map → saved frame, IOSROAD-2026-11-18). Housekeeping does not call these three, so on the iPad they would never run and nothing would say so — a database audit that never audits, a file-home reset that never resets, a DR backup that never checks.

WHY IT IS ALSO AN ANDROID SMELL: startup work attached to a screen's composition runs when that screen composes, not when the app starts — so it is coupled to navigation rather than to launch, and anything that bypasses or re-enters the map screen changes when it runs.

THE WORK: 1 · GtDbAudit is already shared — add it to GtStartupHousekeeping as a job and delete the call from the composable. 2 · Decide where GtFileHome.resetOnce and GtBackup.autoCheck belong: a shared housekeeping job with a platform env, or an Android-host-only step. Both take applicationContext today. 3 · Confirm nothing depends on them running at map-composition time rather than at launch.

DOES NOT BLOCK the A–I + M port — panels do not need these to draw. Carded so the port does not quietly drop three jobs (PANELMOVE1-2026-11-19).

WHAT THIS DOCUMENT IS — and what it is not (Fred, 2026-11-14 20:46)

Fred's words, verbatim, because they define the whole thing:
"the living checklist is our kick the can issues by release. they are the sideboards that wreak havoc once we start down the rabbit hole. our goal is a good problem definition and anticipated solution so we can address without distracting from the task at hand. these are the little tasks that are close enough or a distraction and we never get back to them. they should be tie[d] to a release and commented on the living checklist, there will be a lot of them once we start certifying."

So the job of an entry is to let the item be PUT DOWN safely. Two fields carry that weight: a problem definition good enough that nobody re-derives it, and an anticipated solution so picking it up is work rather than thinking. An entry without those is a reminder, not a checklist item.

⚠ IT IS NOT A STATUS TRACKER, AND IT IS NOT A HISTORY. Closed items are REMOVED, not struck through — completed work is archived in a per-release completed-activity doc (Fred 09-09), and the living master carries open design and the durable rules. Keeping closed items here is what made a half-finished item read as open for six weeks on 08-29.

Why it is its own file as of 11-14: it was a 39 KB section inside the living master, which had grown to 803 KB — 54% of which was eleven superseded daily START HERE sections that were appended and never pruned (11-11 alone was 240 KB). Fred 20:43: "we are supposed to be pruning activities from eod when they are finished.... how can it be so large." Same finding as 09-09 at 1.1 MB: "it is becoming all docs."

THE THREE DOCUMENTS, after 11-14:
· LIVING CHECKLIST (this file) — kick-the-can issues, tied to a release, problem + anticipated solution. Pruned as items close.
· LIVING MASTER — open design, the durable rules that outlive their release, one pointer line per extracted release.
· EOD / HANDOFF — the SHORT-RANGE PLAN only: the next 2–3 days of activity, derived from this checklist and the open items. Pages, not hundreds of KB.

THE ITEM KEY — a Julian date plus the description (JULIANKEY-2026-11-15)

Fred, 2026-11-15 07:47–07:48: "I would prefer a Julian method so we know how old the item is. the older, the less detailed and relevant the item is ... approaching to[o] months need to be reapproved and deemed still relevant" and "we could ma[p] entries on a Julian date so the description and the Julian date represent the item".

THE KEY IS C28-YYDDD PLUS THE DESCRIPTION. No sequence number — the description carries the rest of the identity, so nothing is ever renumbered and the tie to a commit cannot be broken by renumbering. C28-26319 is release 2.8, 2026 day 319 = 2026-11-15. Age is one subtraction, visible wherever the key appears. A commit closing an item reads DONE: 26319 <description>.

⚠ UNIQUENESS WITHIN THE DAY IS REQUIRED. Because the description carries the identity, two items created the same day must not be describable by the same words — otherwise nothing can tie a commit to one of them. When a match is ambiguous the tooling reports the ambiguity and refuses to guess, exactly as it refuses to infer anything else.

⚠ THE DECAY IS LOSS OF CORRECTNESS, NOT LOSS OF INTEREST. An item's problem definition and anticipated solution are written against the code AS IT WAS. After weeks of refactoring the file:line references may no longer resolve and the anticipated solution may name something that no longer exists. Measured examples from this week alone: the start-of-day script's "Droid 2 is on Utah time" note went stale in a day, and MBTilesStore's header still documents five tile types that have not existed since the vector move.

⛔ THE 60-DAY GATE. At 60 days an item is REAPPROVED OR DROPPED, and the review asks three specific things: (1) does the defect still exist, (2) do the file:line references still resolve, (3) is the anticipated solution still the right shape. Reapproval re-dates the item with a note; it does not silently extend it.

Grouping falls out of the key: the checklist is ordered by Julian day, newest first, so current work is at the top and items approaching the gate sink to the bottom where the review catches them. The document sorts itself by relevance.

The 127 KB imported verbatim from the master on 11-15 has NO keys yet. Items get a key as they are re-authored into this format — which gives that job a unit of progress: items WITH a key, versus prose still to read down.

THE ENTRY FORMAT (Fred set it 11-14 08:40–08:41: "i just want to get it in the list with its process so we do not lose it")

fieldwhat goes in it
ties toThe release. Not "later". If it has no release it will not be done.
problemWhat is wrong, in terms that survive a month away from it. A rider-visible symptom where there is one.
anticipated solutionThe field that makes this document work. What we think the fix is, so picking it up is work and not thinking. Often DELETE, not repair.
classThe CATEGORY, not the instance — fix a class once and several entries close together. This is what keeps a hundred entries tractable.
wherefile:line, measured not guessed.
evidenceHow we know. A measurement, a commit, a device observation.
commentStatus, blockers, and any decision Fred owes. Dated.

TIED TO 2.8a — the Android refactor release

DESIGN SET C28-26321 · EXPORTPICK — share becomes export-then-pick, and EXPORT/SHARE collapse

problem Three attempts on 11-17 to make the artifact panel's SHARE "become an email" all failed in the same way, because they were fighting the platform rather than the design. setPackage("com.google.android.gm") cannot resolve (the manifest's <queries> declares only the geo: intent, so on API 30+ a named-package lookup returns null even with Gmail installed — and Backup.send() has carried that same dead branch in production). application/octet-stream on the intent made the chooser list Drive, Files and Bluetooth, so it read as a generic share sheet. Fred 11-17 14:51: "everything with an attachment open via share" — on Android a chooser IS the mechanism, so no amount of intent tuning turns SHARE into mail.

anticipated solution — REFINED 11-17 15:17, and this is the version that stands Fred: "so export should not open selector. it open share and that is redundant. file is already saved." So there is no auto-launch and no preselect:

  • EXPORT writes the artifact to its owning directory and stops. That is what it already does today.
  • SHARE is REDUNDANT and comes off the panel. The file is already saved, so a second hand-off mechanism beside EXPORT is duplicate function.
  • EMAIL lives in the picker, where the files already are, alongside IMPORT and DELETE.

The earlier 15:13 form had EXPORT open the picker with the new file ticked. That is superseded: it needed a preselect parameter and a launch path, and both exist only to re-find a file the rider just wrote on purpose. Nothing transient either way — no cache copy, no FileProvider URI, no share sheet.

⭐ why this is the right shape, not just a workaround It is identical on both platforms: write a file, list the directory, act on the selection. The iPad does that natively, so Android stops being the special case. The three failed attempts were all trying to make Android's share sheet BE email; this makes the picker the one place a file leaves the app, on either platform.

⚠ PROVISIONAL — Fred 11-17 15:17: "let me finish my testing and I will return with a better overview of functions." So the panel's button set is not settled here. What IS settled: EXPORT writes and stops, SHARE is redundant, EMAIL is the picker's. The fuller function overview supersedes the rest of this entry when it arrives.

class Supersedes the 11-17 SHAREMAIL / EMAILHOUSE intent work for the detail panel (that code stays correct and shipping until this replaces it). Also removes the open question of whether to add an intent selector or message/rfc822 — neither is needed once EMAIL comes from the picker.

comment 11-17: the archive half already ships — EXPORT and SHARE both write to GroupTrack Map Features through GtFileHome, and the picker already has EMAIL. What is missing is only the preselect and the launch.

⭐ METHOD RULE 3 — ADOPTED 11-17 C28-26321 · MANUALBASE — the manual’s version of record is INSIDE the file, not its name

This is METHOD RULE 1 applied to documents. Rule 1 says verify code by DECLARED NAME, never by filename. The same trap exists in the doc set and it cost most of an hour tonight.

WHAT HAPPENED. Asked to update the manual, I searched docs/, docs_mirror/ and the Drive inbox/, found inbox/grouptrack_manual_APP_v137.html as the highest number, and built APP_v138 from it. The real head was app/src/main/assets/grouptrack_manual.html, whose own comments run to MANUAL v139. I never looked in assets — the one place the live manual actually is. Two versions, and 8,757 characters of real content, would have been destroyed by the deploy: the 2.7f title and the 22-entry stub index, which is the backfill register the whole stub method depends on.

THE RULE, three parts. 1 The manual of record is app/src/main/assets/grouptrack_manual.html — the file the app opens, named in GtHelpViewer.kt:59-61 and GroupTrackScreen.kt:2130-2132. Same for grouptrack_quickstart.html and grouptrack_release_notes.html. 2 Its VERSION is the highest <!-- MANUAL vNNN --> comment inside it, never the filename and never the inbox’s highest number. 3 Any rebuild asserts its expected baseline and ABORTS if it does not match — gt_manual28stub_2026-11-17_v3.py refuses to run unless the asset reports exactly v139.

WHY THE INBOX LOOKED AUTHORITATIVE AND WAS NOT: APP_v134–v137 reached the inbox and stopped there, because recommit_v26.sh:121 leaves anything over 10 MB in place. Four versions, correctly delivered, never collected. The inbox is a WAITING ROOM, and a waiting room full of files looks exactly like an archive.

⭐ DURABLE RULE — 11-17 C28-26321 · SKIPLOUD — a skip nobody acts on is a loss, not a warning

Two instances now, the same shape. recommit_v26.sh prints LEFT in inbox (over 10 MB) and moves on — four manual versions sat unarchived for ten days. And the morning report lists 38 superseded/void docs that have never been acted on. Both are faithfully reported and functionally invisible.

THE RULE: a report with no actor is indistinguishable from no report. A skip must be made LOUD (it fails, or it blocks the step that depends on it) or IMPOSSIBLE (the condition cannot arise). Adding a line to a log is neither.

✅ FIXED TONIGHT BY THE SECOND METHOD, NOT THE FIRST. The threshold stays at 10 MB. The manual simply no longer ARRIVES anywhere — it lives in docs/ and is deployed FROM there to assets, so there is no transfer left to skip. I had drafted recommit_v27.sh with a bigger number; that would have moved the cliff instead of stepping away from it. Prefer removing the hop to raising the limit.

TIED TO POST-2.8 — found by using the app, fix after the refactor ships

QUEUED POST-2.8b — MEASURED 11-18 C28-26322 · TRAILRECLASS — the unrideable classification EXISTS; three setters are missing, and one of the three is not safe yet

Fred's words: "we do not want to delete trail as it would just be recreated. you want to save it as a non rideable trail[] so route planning does not use it and it does not display with trails... scary that we lose this info." (08:33) · "we even created a classification for this item... it is[in] map keys already. I looked for the function to use it to reclassify but it does not exist." (08:34) · "unrideable trails are never selected in the filter so trail building skips the category. what is missing [is] the function to reclassify [an] exist[ing] trail to unrideable and the ability to change rider to unrideable and the ability to change unridable back to rider." (08:38)

⭐ THE CLASSIFICATION AND BOTH EFFECTS ARE ALREADY BUILT. Measured 11-18:

  • "Unofficial / uncertain" — TrailFilterState.ROW_UNOFFICIAL = "__unofficial" (shared, TrailFilterState.kt:40); legend row MapKeysPanel.kt:99. Fred's own 09-10 help text: "Not available for riding." 501 rows today.
  • It acts on the trails table's status column, not carto_code (MapKeysPanel.kt:97: "a trail is unofficial AND a track") — obeying ⛔ OWNERSHIP IS A SEPARATE FIELD, NOT A CATEGORY (TrailClassifier.kt:24, Fred 08-31), the rule that exists because folding an axis into the category once left 201 rows unable to say what they used to be.
  • Display: map_keys_defaults.json ships __unofficial in off; TrailFilterState.kt:155 renders that as AND (status IS NULL OR status NOT IN ('UNOFFICIAL','UNCERTAIN')). Off by default, still switchable on, so the geometry stays reachable when it is the only way through.
  • Routing: GtRoute.kt:417 applies the same predicate; GtRoute.kt:383 counts what it excludes; cert mode off-unofficial exists (GtCert.kt:42).

⭐⭐ WHY rider ↔ unrideable IS A ROUND TRIP AT ALL. rider is a carto_code (RiderTrailWriter.CATEGORY, MapKeysPanel.kt:96); unrideable is a status. So "change rider to unrideable" does not overwrite the category — it sets a flag beside it, and the trail stays carto_code='rider'. Reversing is clearing that flag. Nothing has to remember what the trail used to be, which is exactly the loss the 08-31 rule was written to prevent. Fred's three functions are therefore ONE toggle, not three conversions.

THE GAP. Nothing writes status from the UI. It arrives only from import (TrailImporterCertOld.kt:582, extra["status"]). RiderTrailWriter.kt:262 updates carto_code, carto_code_source, use_type, land_status and deliberately not status. There is no path from "the rider is looking at the trail and knows it is a road" to "marked not for riding."

⚠ ONE CORRECTION TO THE PREMISE, and it is in our favour. Trail BUILDING does not skip the category. RiderTrailWriter.candidatesFor (:148) selects candidates by bounding box plus an optional carto_code <> 'rider', and carries no status predicate at all. That is load-bearing: because the matcher still sees an unrideable trail, a track ridden down it MATCHES rather than being treated as new ground. If the candidate query were ever "tidied" to exclude unofficial rows, the track would match nothing and a fresh rider trail would be written over the road — recreating precisely what the rider marked, by a different door than overwriting.

⭐ DURABLE RULE — THE MATCHER MUST KEEP SEEING UNOFFICIAL TRAILS. Do not add a status filter to RiderTrailWriter.candidatesFor. Excluding unrideable trails from the candidate set does not hide them; it regenerates them.

The three functions, by whether they are safe today:

  1. Imported trail → unrideable: SAFE. Matcher still sees it (no status predicate), and the writer's UPDATE never touches status. One-column UPDATE and done.
  2. Unrideable → rider: SAFE. Clearing the flag; the carto_code was never disturbed.
  3. ⚠ Rider trail → unrideable: NOT ESTABLISHED. The reconcile pass calls candidatesFor(excludeRider = true) (RIDERIDS-2026-10-09) — rider trails are deliberately OUT of the candidate set, matched against the real trails only. So marking a RIDER trail unrideable may leave its ground looking unmatched, and the next reconcile could re-derive it as a fresh, unmarked rider trail: the information lost by duplication rather than by overwrite. Whether the dedup session's geom_hash / stable IDs already prevent that has not been determined — this entry does not assume either answer. Check this before writing function 2.

Anticipated solution. One toggle on the trail's artifact detail panel writing status, plus the refresh any trail edit already triggers. Legend row, default-off, filter SQL, route predicate and cert mode need no work.

⚠ Two guards:

  1. A state RE-IMPORT can overwrite it. TrailImporter writes status from the source. Follows an existing precedent: a status_source column mirroring carto_code_source, or an import rule that will not downgrade a rider-set value.
  2. status is multi-valued — it also carries 'CLOSED' (TrailCheck.kt:226). Writing UNOFFICIAL must not silently discard a CLOSED.

Open for Fred: should a rider-set mark carry its own value ('RIDER_UNOFFICIAL') to stay distinguishable from a surveyed UNOFFICIAL? Everything filters on status IN ('UNOFFICIAL','UNCERTAIN'), so a third value means one edit to the shared predicate, the route side and the cert — cheap decided now, archaeology decided later.

QUEUED POST-2.8b — MEASURED 11-17 C28-26321 · MANUALIMG — pull the 183 images out, so the daily manual update is free

MEASURED on v140: the manual is 10.29 MB, of which 10.10 MB (98%) is 183 embedded base64 images. The actual text is 0.19 MB. Compressed, each version costs ~7 MB; 154 manual versions are already in git history and .git is 474 MB, so the manual is most of the repository.

FRED 11-17 17:01: “if we save 10 gig a day that is … duplication of 90% of the data.” Correct, and the real figure is 98%.

THE CHANGE. The 183 images become files the HTML references. git then stores each image ONCE, forever, and the tracked manual becomes 0.19 MB of text that deltas to almost nothing: ~3 MB per version → ~20 KB. The single-file base64 build stays, as a GENERATED app asset produced at build time.

⚠ WHAT CONSTRAINS IT: grouptrack_manual.html is the name the app opens (GtHelpViewer.kt:59-61) and the target of every grouptrack_manual.html#… link in the Quick Start. The asset must stay ONE self-contained file that works offline with no network and no unpacking. So this is a build step, not a restructure of what ships.

⭐ WHY THIS IS NOT AN OPTIMISATION. The daily manual update is the process that FIXED the accuracy problem (see DOCDAILY). The image weight is what makes that daily discipline look expensive, and what would eventually argue for updating less often — which is the old failure mode. Doing this protects the method.

⭐ RATIONALE — FRED 11-17 17:04 / 17:06 C28-26321 · DOCDAILY — why the manual is updated daily, and why stubs

Recorded because the change that undoes it will look sensible.

THE OLD WAY AND ITS COST. “updating the manual every 2 or three weeks and then loosing that version of the manual was painful. it lived in [my] downloads if I was lucky but we los[t] whole releases of manual info.” Updates lagged the code by weeks, the current version lived in a Downloads folder, and whole releases’ worth of manual information was lost.

THE ROOT CAUSE, Fred 17:06: “you having access to Google Drive is the real key. I was always the go between and docs got lost in downloads.” Every document used to make a trip through a human courier. That hop does not fail loudly; it just quietly does not happen on a day spent chasing a build.

WHY STUBS WORK — the decoupling. Stubbing the screen captures separates the TEXT from the SCREENSHOTS. Text is corrected the day behaviour changes (start_of_day v7: “we have one moment of clarity on changes. it is as soon as they are completed”); captures backfill whenever a build is on a tablet. The old method made accuracy WAIT on a screenshot session, which is why updates slipped to two or three weeks, which is why versions were lost. Tonight is the proof: map.backup was marked text is wrong the same afternoon the decision was made, not three weeks later when a rider noticed it still said “pick your email”.

⛔ DO NOT “FIX” THIS BY COMMITTING AT RELEASE BOUNDARIES. I proposed exactly that at 16:58 and it is wrong: between boundaries the manual has to live somewhere, and somewhere was Downloads. Release-boundary commits do not remove the gap, they rename it. The daily commit is load-bearing. The cost it carries is MANUALIMG’s 98%, and that is what to fix instead.

THE FRAMING — READ THIS FIRST C28-26321 · DRTRIAGE — what ships today is TRIAGE, not disaster recovery

Fred 11-17 16:44 "I just want to get these thoughts on paper so we do not loose what real dr looks like. what we have now is triage."

WHAT TRIAGE IS — today's job. Four GPX files (routes, tracks, waypoints, rider trails), gated on a change fingerprint or seven days, emailed and archived to Map Features. It restores GEOMETRY. Measured 11-17, a restore from it loses: the rider PROFILE (users: name, email, cell, callsign, default role, vehicle, team, organizer), the RIDE HISTORY (rides, enrollments — roles, teams, node numbers, TAK uids), the RADIO SETUP (network_configs: region, modem preset, hop limit, tx power, frequency slot), the TRACK STATISTICS (track_properties: distance, duration, max and average speed, elevation, per track), the ROUTE NARRATIVES (route_notes, 826 rows), the NAMES (artifact_aliases), and the IMPORT RECORD (source_ingestions). There is no restore sequence and no automation beyond a prompt.

So the honest summary: lose the tablet today and you keep what a GPX file can carry, and nothing else.

WHAT REAL DR LOOKS LIKE. It rests on one measured fact: the rider's own data is ~40 MB of a 3.1 GB corpus. Everything else is redistributable or disposable — 485 MB of trails, trail_properties, land ownership and reference points; 921 MB of state source; 1.4 GB of cert fixtures; and the map tiles. So real DR is cheap, and it has two halves:

  • SAVE — one automated job, run in housekeeping in the background, that zips the rider-owned set from BOTH databases plus my_tracks/, rides/, recipes/ and route_drafts/, writes it to the DR directory, and mirrors it off the tablet. Off-tablet is the part that makes it recovery rather than a copy: a backup that only exists on the device being recovered is not a backup.
  • RESTORE — in dependency order, because the data has dependencies: replay the trail imports from source_ingestions (automated, no file needed, 265 of 266 rows already carry the bbox) → DB-restore the rider-owned tables → maps last from the recipes, since tiles are keyed on geom_hash and must follow the geometry. Waypoints before routes, because a route's recipe anchors on a trailhead waypoint.

⭐ AND WHY A DB RESTORE, not files. Both of 11-17's defects lived in the export/import round trip: four duplicate routes admitted because a re-import recomputes geom_hash from re-parsed text, and the GtGpx.trail MULTILINESTRING bug silently dropping points on the way out. A DB restore re-parses nothing and recomputes no hash, so neither failure is reachable. The round trip is the right mechanism for sharing with another rider; it was never the right one for recovering your own data.

THE GAP, in one list no off-tablet mirror (on hold pending an iPad equivalent) · no profile, ride or radio-config capture · no DB restore · no trail replay · no automation · and version_ingested / filters_used never written, so a replay today would be approximate.

the detail DRDESIGN (the mechanism) · DRSCOPE (the measurement) · DRRESTORE (the sequence, and that tapping from the DR directory already covers five of six steps) · DRDECIDE (what is actually being changed now) · MAPMENU (the menu revisions). The only DR work in flight before the iOS release is DRDECIDE's email→DR-directory write.

DR TASK — POST-2.8b, FOR REAL DISCUSSION C28-26321 · DRDESIGN — DB restore, trail replay from the ingestion record, maps last

Fred 11-17 16:43 "move the research to the dr task post 2.8b so we an have real discussion we should be able to automate trail imports by using completed json, we should be able to restore everything else except for maps in a db restore and the restore maps as the last step"

⭐ WHY A DB RESTORE IS THE RIGHT MECHANISM, and this is the strongest argument in the whole DR thread: it sidesteps the export/import round trip entirely — which is exactly where BOTH of 11-17's defects lived. TRACKROUTESEP admitted four duplicate routes because a re-import recomputes geom_hash from re-parsed text; the GtGpx.trail MULTILINESTRING bug drops points on the way out. A DB restore re-parses nothing, recomputes no hash, and cannot lose a point. The round trip was never the safe way to move the rider's OWN data; it was only the way we had.

⭐ TRAIL REPLAY FROM THE INGESTION RECORD — measured 11-17 16:47, and it is feasible today

  • bounds_json is populated on 265 of 266 rows, with real bboxes ({"s":36.87,"w":-114.17,"n":42.09,"e":-108.85}). So source_id + bounds_json is enough to replay "re-import this source over this area" NOW.
  • ⚠ version_ingested and filters_used are populated on ZERO rows. Both are in the schema but absent from both writers — GtOsmImport.kt:356 and GtTrailImport.kt:747 insert only (ingestion_id, source_id, ingested_at, trail_count, dupes_skipped, bounds_json). Adding them is a one-line change in each and would make future replays EXACT rather than approximate.

the shape that falls out 1. replay trail imports from source_ingestions (automated, no file needed) → 2. DB restore of the rider-owned tables from both databases, per DRSCOPE → 3. maps last, from the recipes. Maps are excluded from the DB restore because tiles are keyed on geom_hash and must follow the geometry.

status Deferred past 2.8b for a real discussion, per Fred. DRSCOPE's measurement (rider-owned is ~40 MB of a 3.1 GB corpus) and DRRESTORE's tap-from-DR finding are the inputs to it. Nothing here is being built now; the only DR change in flight is DRDECIDE's email→DR-directory write.

⚠ WHAT TODAY'S BACKUP LOSES. GtBackup.TYPES is routes / tracks / waypoints / trails only, so a restore from the current DR position returns the GEOMETRY and loses:

⭐ THE RIGHT CUT IS NOT spatial-versus-extension. So MAPMENU's "one file that restores all components" means the rider-owned set from both DBs, not the four artifact types.
