SharedPreferences live in /data/data and are root-only"). The users row is in the extension DB and IS backupable; what else sits only in prefs has not been enumerated.

QUEUED POST-2.8 — FRED 11-17 16:33 C28-26321 · DRRESTORE — recovery is automated, and runs the jobs in dependency order

Fred, verbatim "the dr recovery and save should be automated. recovery should launch jobs in the proper sequence. start with an import of home state, than rider trails restored, thank tracks, waypoints, routes, then maps."

the sequence

  1. home state import
  2. rider trails
  3. tracks
  4. waypoints
  5. routes
  6. maps

⭐ WHY THIS ORDER IS RIGHT, not merely one choice It matches the real dependencies, each verifiable in code:

  • waypoints before routes — a route's recipe carries an anchorLat / anchorLon resolved against trailhead waypoints (GtRead.trailheadsNear, routeUsingTrailhead, setRouteAnchor). Restore routes first and their trailhead anchors resolve to nothing.
  • maps last — corridor tiles are keyed on geom_hash (enqueueCorridor(gh, slot, replace)), so the geometry has to exist before tiles can be requested for it. The existing side-by-side restore procedure already puts maps last for exactly this reason: step 6 is "on Droid 2: RESTORE MY MAPS from the recipe, let the maps download."
  • home state first — it is the base trail layer (229,363 rows on Droid 1) and the heaviest job; rider trails land in the same table.

⭐ SUPERSEDED 11-17 16:41 — NO SEQUENCER IS NEEDED. Fred: "we can launch every one of the revery jobs by tapping it from the dr directory." That is the rule he set the same morning — "click on an item launches the update" — applied to the DR directory. Verified in code 16:47: FIVE OF THE SIX STEPS ALREADY WORK BY TAPPING.

  • rider trails — GroupTrack_RIDER_TRAILS_<date>.gpx. Comes back as TRAILS, not tracks, because trailsGpx writes <type>grouptrack-trail</type> — the ONEIMPORT-2026-11-04 mark the importer reads.
  • tracks / waypoints / routes — the three GroupTrack_*_<date>.gpx files; familyOf sees XML/GPX, routes to MAP_FEATURES, and the importer takes each to its own table.
  • maps — a .gtmaps recipe; familyOf sees JSON with GtRecipes.kind, routes to MAPS, which is RESTORE MY MAPS.
  • ⚠ home state is the exception, and correctly so — state trails are REDISTRIBUTABLE and the 921 MB states/ source is deliberately outside the DR position. Step 1 is therefore "re-run the state import" (its own screen, map.trails_state), not "restore a file".

⭐ and the listing can BE the runbook. GtFilePickerBody sorts by filename (sortedBy { it.name.lowercase() }), so naming the DR files in dependency order — 1_state, 2_rider_trails, 3_tracks, 4_waypoints, 5_routes, 6_maps — makes top-to-bottom the correct order with nothing orchestrating it.

⚠ SO WHAT IS ACTUALLY MISSING is the "work with DR" launch point — the same gap Fred named at 15:19. The picker body already handles Family.ROOT; nothing opens it. Give the DR directory an entry point and recovery is: open it, tap down the list. That replaces the job chain below.

⚠ the job-chain note, kept for the record An automated sequenced restore is six jobs run in order with each waiting on the last. That is the same mechanism as C28-26321 · "ONE job-launcher seam serves BOTH the download queue and the state import" — so DRRESTORE should be built on that seam rather than beside it, or we end up with a second sequencer.

⚠ open What a restore does with data already present — merge or replace. Dedup makes merge safe for geometry (UNIQUE(geom_hash) refuses a repeat), but a restore onto a populated tablet is a different intent from a restore onto an empty one, and the rider should probably be told which is happening.

comment 11-17: the SAVE half is specced in MAPMENU above (one file, DR directory, no email, automatic on 7 days). This is the RECOVERY half. Both automated, per Fred.

CANDIDATE DIRECTION C28-26319 · shift LOCAL duplicate detection from hash to SHAPE

ties toPOST-2.8 — it touches every import path, so it waits until the refactor has shipped. ⚠ But it should be decided before 2.9's download rules are written, because they are the same mechanism.
problemFred 2026-11-15 11:04: "local duplicate creation should probably shift to shape rules once they are created. Would have solved our rename issue from yesterday."

And it would have. The 11-15 investigation found two rows for one ride — Cedar City to Newcastle_oldirontown and Cedar city to Newcastle /oldirontown — differing by a single character, with different hashes. So the hash check saw two tracks and the name check saw two names. Shape would have seen one piece of ground. Fred's own diagnosis: "I'll bet we made a change at some point to allow that character in the name, and the reimports and the rule change caused the duplication."

And the codebase already states why hash cannot do this job: "NOT the geometry hash. Two GPS traces over the same ground are never byte-identical — the same reason OSM and UGRC turned out to be independent surveys with 9 hash matches in 89,554."
anticipated solution⭐ It collapses THREE mechanisms into one. Local duplicate prevention, 2.9's download rule ("ground I do not have"), and the rider-trail network matching are all the same question — do I already hold this ground? GtRiderScan's candidatesFor() already answers it, and was built to decide which ground in a new track is genuinely new. Point it at an incoming artifact instead of a freshly recorded one.

Keep hash as the cheap first pass — it catches the same file re-imported, in O(1), and trails.geom_hash is already UNIQUE. Shape is the second pass, for same-ground-different-trace.
classIdentity resolution. The same class as the 08-11 alias duplicates and the 11-04 rename duplicates — both were "two records for one thing" because identity was computed from something that is not the thing.
✅ the governing-principle tension, and why it is not a conflictV3_TrackSharing_SpatialModel states: "The DB controls every write, in all cases … Code never decides 'should this go in?'; it presents the record and the DB decides." A shape rule cannot be a SQL constraint, so this looks like a violation. It is not. The same document says: "Code owns: identifying entities, computing hash / wkt / bbox / name, presenting records to the DB." Shape resolution IS identification — already code's job. Code resolves shape to an identity; the DB still enforces uniqueness on that identity. The principle holds exactly as written, and the write path does not change shape.
⚠ the real costA TOLERANCE. Hash has none — equal or not. Shape needs one: how close is "the same ground"? That introduces a tunable, and a tunable needs certifying across the real corpus (229,178 trails, 130 tracks) with the DIFF list examined, not a number picked by eye. There is precedent: the OSM/UGRC comparison established that independent surveys of one trail are genuinely different geometries, so the tolerance has measured ground truth to calibrate against. The certification is the work; the tolerance existing is not an argument against it.
wheregrouptrack-shared/…/rider/GtRiderTrails.kt (candidatesFor, GtRiderScan); every import path that calls insertTrack.
evidence11-15: the rename-duplicate pair measured on Droid 1; GtRiderTrails.kt's own "9 in 89,554"; the three mechanisms shown to be one question.
comment11-15: decide before 2.9's per-artifact download rules are written — if the download uses shape and local inserts use hash, the device ends up with two different notions of "already have it", which is worse than either alone.

ENTRY 1 C28-26318 · The download-area estimate — raster assumptions in the vector app

problemA ~60 km area shows 271,735 tiles / 3,980 MB when the real job is 1,287; a ~150 km trip is refused with "Area Too Large" against a limit the dialog calls 500 MB while the code enforces 5000. Over-count measured at 147×–232×. Confirmed again on device 11-14: 118,360 "called for download" against 547 actual = 216×, and identical on 2.7e (Droid 2), so it is not a port regression.
anticipated solutionDELETE, DO NOT REPAIR (Fred 08:39). Remove the N tiles — X MB estimated line, the whole withinCeiling branch, and the four UI quickEstimate callers. The honest number arrives with the queue. ⚠ Removing the ceiling removes no real protection — measured 11-14, there is no free-space guard anywhere in the tile download path; what tamed space is vector being ~200× smaller than raster.
whereCallers: MapScreens:51, GroupTrackScreen:913, :1295, :2459; the gate at GroupTrackScreen:667. The queue callers at :372/:427 are already correct.
class⭐ RASTER-ERA ASSUMPTION SURVIVING INTO THE VECTOR APP — three sub-kinds in this one defect: the zoom ceiling (z18 vs VEC z14 / DEM z12), the layer multiplier (satLayers=3), and the byte constant (AVG_TILE_KB = 15f). Expect this class repeatedly; check all three before closing a new one.
comment⚠ Check the ratio against 147×–232× before opening anything new in this class — 11-14's sighting closed against this entry in one comparison instead of becoming entry 101. GtTileMath.areaTiles exists for when a count is wanted again; it has no caller and Fred's 08:42 ruling moved its testing here. Don't build a Cert17* runner for it.

ENTRY 2 C28-26318 · The track list shows an alias as a second track

problemThe same ride appears twice in the track select panel. The list joins aliases and renders a row per alias, so one track with one alias yields two entries. Both open the same track, because they are the same row — UNIQUE(geom_hash) makes two rows with one hash impossible, and the pair shows the same hash.
anticipated solutionOne entry per track_id, primary name displayed; the alias participates only in resolving what the rider typed or tapped. A query fix, not a data fix — nothing in the table changes and the alias is correct and kept.
classAn alias is for MATCHING, never for LISTING. D22 (show the name the search matched) is a result-annotation rule, not a listing rule. Routes, rides and waypoints carry aliases too — expect this class there.
comment11-14: severity LOW — cosmetic, nothing lost or mis-selected, but it reads as a retrieval failure, which is how it was reported. Unverified: the extension DB was never opened, so the alias text and its write date are unconfirmed (one small pull and one query). If the alias turns out to be a path-safe form of the name (/ → _) written back as an alias, the fix moves upstream and D34 could not have caught it.

ENTRY 3 C28-26318 · Duplicate track names accepted on import — D26 is not enforced

problem26 ride names each appear exactly twice: two real rows, different hashes. The import path dedups on geom_hash only. D34 ("same hash AND same name") passed because the hashes differ, and nothing checked the name alone, which is what D26 requires. The rows were written 11-04, after D26 (10-29) and D34 (10-30) were both decided.
anticipated solution⚠ FRED'S DECISION, OPEN. (a) hard fail on name, D26 literally — but it blocks his own re-import testing; (b) fail only when name AND hash match, which is today's behaviour; (c) prompt — replace / keep both / rename, which costs an import-path dialog. This choice decides whether the fix is a one-line check or a screen.
class⭐ A RULE ENFORCED AT TWO LEVELS IN TWO PLACES. D34's hash half is a schema constraint; D26's name half lives in Kotlin, in whichever save path checks. Fred 18:22: "all these rules are supposed to be in the db." Expect this class for every business rule that is not a constraint.
evidenceEvery pair shares an identical bounding box; one row created 2026-08-11 20:14–20:23 (the recovery rebuild), one 2026-11-04 17:21–17:42, the second WKT 1.3–1.9× longer for the same ground ⇒ the 08-11 recovery imported at roughly half the point density. Every 08-11 row's canonical <hash>.gpx is one of the 69 single-trackpoint stubs. ✅ What was ruled out: all 136 stored geom_hash values recompute from their own geometry, so the hash function has NOT changed, and there are zero duplicate hashes in tracks (136/136), trails (229,363), waypoints (599) or routes (23).

DECISION C28-26318 · Retire the 26 inferior 08-11 track rows

problemThe 08-11 set is the inferior copy — 26 rows at roughly half the point density, with single-trackpoint stub GPX files. Deleting them resolves the display duplication and loses nothing.
anticipated solutionA CONSIDERED DELETION, NOT A SWEEP. Check lineId references first, per the 07-11 track-duplication rule, then delete the 26 rows and their stub files.
commentDepends on Entry 3's decision only in spirit; can be done independently.

OPEN C28-26318 · 69 of 136 canonical GPX files hold exactly one trackpoint

problemHalf the my_tracks folder. The 26 from Entry 3 are explained by the 08-11 half-density recovery; the rest are not. No two multi-point files share a point count, so they are distinct recordings.
anticipated solutionLikeliest stationary or aborted recordings, which the 08-01 note says fail silently. Measure first: group the 69 by created date and see whether they cluster on the recovery or spread across normal use. If they spread, the track writer accepts a one-point track and should refuse.

CERT GAP C28-26318 · Two holes the step 17 certification does not cover

problem(1) insertTile's codec dispatch was never exercised — both live slots are role "overlay" and TileCodec.encode returns overlay bytes unchanged, so the base branch is unreachable today. (2) Per-member error logging is gone from the data path: the shared ports swallow exceptions and return 2.7e's own fallbacks silently, so a failing call writes nothing to the log. Return values are byte-identical; diagnostics are thinner, and it cost real time during the 11-14 switch verification.
anticipated solution(1) Leave it — unreachable code is not worth a fixture; record it so a future raster-ish slot does not assume it was proven. (2) Add logging on the PLATFORM side of each delegation where a fallback return is indistinguishable from a legitimate empty result. Cheap, and it restores field diagnosability without touching the certified port.
classA certified port is quieter than the code it replaces. Expect this on every future switch.

TIED TO 2.8b — the iPad release

DO NOT TOUCH C28-26318 · iosMain/text/GtMathLog.kt:125 — the "Division by zero" warning is correct

problemThe compiler warns Division by zero on return -1.0 / 0.0. It looks like a bug and is not.
anticipated solutionNONE — leave it. Negative infinity is the correct IEEE result for log(0), mirroring __math_divzero. The file is a bit-exact port of ARM's optimized log() (the B[] polynomial, INVC/LOGC tables, fma, N=128) so iOS produces identical bits to Android's libm. Rewriting the line as Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY is harmless; touching anything else in the file breaks the bit-exactness that is its entire purpose.

OPENED 2026-11-18 EVENING — THE MARKER HAZARD AND THE DEVICE-ONLY TESTS (CL18C-2026-11-18) (C28-26331 … C28-26332)

C28-26331 — A DR RESTORE THAT REPLACES THE GROUPTRACK DIRECTORY SILENTLY DELETES EVERY TRAIL (MARKERDR-2026-11-18)

Release: 2.8b, and it affects shipping Android — not iOS-only.

PROBLEM DEFINITION. GtStartupHousekeeping.jobSchemaConverge decides whether to wipe the trails table by testing for a dotfile: const val SCHEMA_MARKER = ".schema_2026-09-03A" in the GroupTrack root. Marker absent → DELETE FROM trails + DELETE FROM trail_properties, ALTER the missing columns, write the marker, return needsReload = true. Marker present → return immediately.

MEASURED 11-18 ON THE SIMULATOR. Seeding from the Nov 6 Droid 1 capture gave a full database with no dotfiles at all — the capture contains none. Converge cleared 322 trails; tracks (130) and waypoints (595) survived. Re-seeded with the marker present: 229,178 trails intact, no clear, no banner. So the code is behaving as written; the input state is new.

WHY ANDROID HAS NEVER HIT IT IN NINE MONTHS. Fresh install → no marker but an empty trails table, so the clear is a no-op. Existing install → marker present. Only a restore or a seed creates the third state (full data, no marker), and that state is handled destructively.

THE RULE (Fred 11-18): “what that means on the real iPad is we have to install once before downloading and restoring db.” → install → launch once → restore. And the restore must write over the top of the existing directory; a restore that replaces the directory carries the marker away with it and the next launch clears the trails.

THE WORK. 1 · Audit the Android DR restore path for delete-then-write behaviour on the GroupTrack root. 2 · Either preserve the dotfiles across a restore or have the restore write the marker itself. 3 · Decide whether converge should refuse to clear a NON-EMPTY trails table without an explicit confirmation — a clear of 229,178 rows should not be a silent consequence of a missing zero-byte file. 4 · Document the install-then-restore order in the DR procedure and the iPad install notes.

ALSO AT RISK, same root, same reason: .db_schema_version, .trails_cleared_2026-09-02B, .nomedia.

TO VERIFY, NOT ASSERTED: gt_sidebyside_v7.sh:159 uses ls -A, so the newer capture tool should include dotfiles. Unrun, unconfirmed. Until confirmed, treat every capture as dotfile-free.

C28-26332 — THE DEVICE-ONLY TEST CASES (DEVTEST-2026-11-18)

Release: 2.8b. Why this card exists: these cannot be certified on the simulator, and until now they were scattered across sections rather than collected anywhere. Fred 11-18, on the reconnect behaviour: “the reconnect issues can be explored once we have a device connected. add it to the test cases.”

1 · BTREPAIR — reconnect after forget/re-pair. No BLE stack on the simulator at all.

2 · NOSIMRADIO — the radio and mesh menus are untestable on the simulator, not merely deferred. Every radio function, channel config and mesh exchange is device work.

3 · IMPORTS — email, download and share-in. Fred: “not a sim function.” The device builds its own data from the internal processes and certifies startup + imports there.

4 · GPS REALITY — the simulator gives a settable fix (xcrun simctl location, or a GPX route) and that is enough for I · CENTRE ON ME. Drift, accuracy values, loss of fix and reacquisition are device-only.

5 · MEMLIMIT — memory ceilings and ApplicationExitInfo-equivalent reporting under real pressure.

6 · CFBundleDocumentTypes — open-in / file-association handling from other apps.

7 · releaseFramework — produced today but never linked; a release-configuration build is a device-path concern.

8 · MARKERDR (C28-26331) — the install → launch once → restore order must be executed and verified on the real iPad, not reasoned about.

OPENED 2026-11-18 — THE EIGHT CARDS OWED FROM SDK 37 AND XCODESTEPS (CL18B-2026-11-18) (C28-26323 … C28-26330)

All eight were measured on 11-18, not guessed. Four belong to a POST-2.8 Android release and four to 2.8b; each card's tag says which. Full narrative is in the DAY18B-2026-11-18 section of docs/grouptrack_handoff_interim_2026-11-18.html.

QUEUED POST-2.8 — OWN RELEASE C28-26323 · TGTSDK37 — targetSdk 37 is nearly empty for GroupTrack, and should ship on its own

Where we are: COMPILE_SDK=37 and AGP 9.1.1 landed 11-18 in 698aa4494. TARGET_SDK is still 36, deliberately. compileSdk is what Compose demands; targetSdk is what opts into Android 17 behaviour changes.

Measured cost of moving targetSdk to 37 — close to nothing: RFCOMM N/A (0 BluetoothSocket refs) · native DCL N/A (0 System.load) · background audio N/A · widget bitmap N/A · keystore N/A.

Why it still gets its own release: one change, one build. A targetSdk bump changes runtime behaviour across the whole app, so it does not ride along with feature work — and the two ungated changes below (C28-26324, C28-26325) want instrumenting first, because they bite whether or not we bump.

ACTIVE POST-2.8 — UNGATED, BITES TODAY C28-26324 · MEMLIMIT — the Android 17 memory limiter kills silently and we have no way to see it

⛔ This is UNGATED — it applies regardless of compileSdk and targetSdk, so staying on targetSdk 36 is not a shield.

What it does: the per-app memory limiter throttles, then kills. There is no catchable exception. The only way to see it after the fact is ApplicationExitInfo with reason REASON_OTHER and "MemoryLimiter:AnonSwap" in getDescription().

What GroupTrack has today, measured: zero getHistoricalProcessExitReasons, zero onTrimMemory, and no largeHeap. So a limiter kill is today indistinguishable from a crash, and we would spend the day debugging the wrong thing.

The work: read getHistoricalProcessExitReasons at startup, and when the last exit was a limiter kill, say so in the log and in the startup notice. This matters most for the 20-minute state import and the big GPX writes — exactly the runs a rider would report as "it just closed".

ACTIVE POST-2.8 — UNGATED, CONTRADICTS A USER ACTION C28-26325 · BTREPAIR — Android may re-pair the radio the rider just told us to forget

⛔ Also UNGATED. Android 17 re-pairs autonomously on bond loss and prompts the user; ACTION_KEY_MISSING is only broadcast if that re-pair fails.

What is already safe, measured: GroupTrack has 0 ACTION_KEY_MISSING refs, and the bond receiver at MeshCommLink.kt:160-167 only logs and refreshes — idempotent, so a surprise re-pair does not corrupt our state.

The conflict: MeshCommLink.forget() (:121-127) calls the hidden removeBond() by reflection. Android may simply re-pair the radio afterwards, so FORGET may not stick — a user action we appear to honour and then silently undo.

The work: a device test on Android 17 — forget the radio, then watch for an autonomous re-pair prompt. Decide then whether forget needs to also mark the node as refused on our side, rather than relying on the bond being gone.

PROCESS — STALE STRING, FOUND 11-18 C28-26326 · DEPLOYHINT — deploy_v3.sh's usage text and closing hint still say deploy_v2.sh

How it surfaced: I read the Oct 6 deploy.sh instead of the Nov 9 deploy_v3.sh and caught it before issuing the command. Then found v3 itself still names v2 in two places it prints to the operator.

Why it is worth a card and not a shrug: this is the GOLDEN RULE's failure mode in reverse — the file name is right and the text is stale, so a correct script tells you to go run a superseded one. The commands folder has 40-odd _SUPERSEDED_DO_NOT_RUN files precisely because running the wrong version is the hazard we design against.

The work: two string edits in $GT_CMDS/deploy_v3.sh. While there, check the other _v3 scripts for the same stale self-reference.

ACTIVE 2.8b — NEVER ONCE EXERCISED C28-26327 · RELFRAME — releaseFramework has never been linked

Measured 11-18: every iOS build to date has linked debugFramework. The release variant has never been produced or linked, so nothing is known about it: not its size, not whether the generated header is identical, not whether it links at all.

Why this is not a detail: GroupTrack does not ship debug builds (standing rule, Android side). The iPad release will link the release framework, and the first time we try it will be under deadline. The GtOrgJson.NULL failure on 11-18 is the warning: a problem in the generated header stops all Swift compilation, and it surfaced only when something actually imported the framework.

The work: link releaseFramework once, now, and compare the generated header's declaration counts against debug's measured 405 @interface + 101 @protocol = 506.

QUEUED 2.8b — BLOCKS A NAMED FEATURE C28-26328 · DOCTYPES — CFBundleDocumentTypes is not set, so a .gpx in Downloads cannot open GroupTrack

Fred's requirement (11-16), one of the two net missing features: another user sends a ride or a box as an attachment, and tapping the .gpx should launch the app and its import.

Where we are: STEP 3 added six INFOPLIST_KEY_* entries; CFBundleDocumentTypes is not among them. Without it iOS does not offer GroupTrack as a handler, so the feature cannot work regardless of what the import code does.

IN FLIGHT 2.8b — HALF BUILT 11-18 C28-26329 · LOUDASSERT — the startup assertion reports; it does not refuse to start

What landed 11-18 (17698c717): StartupAssertion.swift checks all 19 bundle assets through the production reader and reported 19 of 19 on the iPad. DbProbe.swift reported 8 of 8. Both render their results in ContentView.

⛔ What is still missing is the loudness. The whole reason the assertion exists is that the asset reader's failures are SILENT: GtHelpLinks.load wraps iosBundleAsset in runCatching and falls back to an EMPTY table; GtMapSpec.spec() catches and returns null; and a missing .sql leaves a HALF-BUILT database with no error at all. A probe that merely prints its findings into a view nobody reads does not catch any of those in a real build.

The work: make a failed assertion refuse to proceed, with the missing name in the message. Then it is a gate, not a report.

ACTIVE C28-26321 · ONE job-launcher seam serves BOTH the download queue and the state import

problemBoth were treated as candidate SUBSYSTEMS. Measured 11-17, neither is. ConvoyDownloadQueue.kt is 1,354 lines of which the runtime is ~400 and only launchWorker (77 lines: WorkManager, Data.Builder, Constraints) is irreducibly Android; the three workers are 676 lines with ~60 Android-API lines between them. HomeStateImportController.kt is 1,799 lines with 41 Android-API matches, and those 41 resolve to: ~20 ctx: Context threaded only to reach importsDir/manifestFile (already closed — GtImportPaths); 4 withContext(Dispatchers.IO) which are not Android at all (multiplatform coroutines — they matched a "Context" grep); 3 raw SQLiteDatabase.openDatabase calls (androidx.sqlite is already the portable driver both platforms use); and WorkManager + WorkInfo + awaitWorker.
anticipated solutionDefine ONE seam, not two — "start a named unit of work and await its completion", with the constraint concept attached: (a) enqueue named work (Android WorkManager unique work; iOS an operation on a queue), (b) await completion (Android WorkInfo observation; iOS the operation's completion), (c) network required or not — ⚠ a DELETE job is deliberately NOT_REQUIRED, because "requiring CONNECTED for every job would make freeing storage impossible offline, which is exactly when a user needs to free storage". Everything else in both subsystems is portable logic whose sockets are already closed.
commentFred 11-17 08:44: the state download was "also a candidate for subsystem and runs a long time with screen present... we were waiting for download queues to define the model." That was the right call — it IS one model. ⚠ SEQUENCING: do this AFTER the first sim build, not before. SVCFIRST says do not stand up the environment while services are missing because a bug cannot be told from a gap; the mirror holds — porting ~1,000 lines of download engine before the sim exists means no way to run a single download. Today append and enqueueCorridor throw with dated markers, which costs nothing on the map path because downloads are not on it.

ACTIVE C28-26321 · SCOPED 11-17: a stub screen held on top, so another selection cannot terminate the job (JOBSCOPE-2026-11-17)

⭐ THE SCOPE, SET BY FRED 11-17 09:04 — read this before the rows below: “our scope is to solve the platform difference so jobs start and run without terminating on another selection (current Apple behavior). leaving a stub screen present and displaying on top will resolve that issue. any other issues will be addressed in certification testing.”

So the deliverable is ONE SURFACE WITH TWO STATES (JOBCHIP-2026-11-17, Fred 11-17 09:05: “any other selection minimizes the chip”):

statewhenwhat it does
FULL — on topthe job starts, and by default while it runsstays present and displaying over whatever else; another selection cannot terminate the job
MINIMIZED — the chipthe rider makes another selectionthe stub screen MINIMIZES to the chip rather than being dismissed — step x of xx, still present

⭐ THE CHIP IS THE MINIMIZED STATE OF THAT SCREEN, NOT A SEPARATE INDICATOR. That distinction is the whole design: one surface that is never absent while a job runs, so the job can never be invisible and can never be terminated by a selection. On iPadOS it is also what keeps the process alive, because a foreground app with a visible scene is not suspended.

⭐ ACCEPTANCE-TESTING DETAIL, NOT TO BE LOST (CHIPDRAG-2026-11-17, Fred 11-17 09:06): the chip is a DRAGGABLE / repositionable item — his words, “a drawable item so we can position anywhere of the screen” — so the rider can move it off whatever it is covering. It is NOT part of the scoped platform fix above; it is an acceptance-testing requirement for the chip once the chip exists. Recorded here because Fred asked that it not be lost.

⛔ NOT in scope (explored 11-17, deferred to certification testing): a separate global progress host above the nav host, independent of this screen; and reconciling the two recovery models (the tile queue auto-restarts at init, the state import stamps and waits for a rerun — both are correct for their own work). The rows below are the MEASURED BACKGROUND the scoping rests on, not the work.

problemFred 11-17 08:48: the state-download screen does not stay visible once another screen is over the top; "job must always stay up with new screen or process over the top... we can minimize screen with a step x of xx counter but something must stay present if anything else is allowed to be selected." Measured 11-17: NO persistent progress surface exists anywhere in the app — every "x of y" string is either cert code or screen-local dialog state (MapScreens:393, :495). So today a rider can leave the import screen and the job becomes invisible while it keeps running.
anticipated solutionThe data half is already built. HomeStateImportController.progress is a StateFlow<ImportProgress?> on an object (line 98), observable from ANY screen, and ImportProgress already carries currentSourceIndex + totalSources (so "step x of xx" is currentSourceIndex + 1 of totalSources), currentStep ("Downloading"/"Extracting"/"Importing"/"Cleanup"), stepDetail ("47,200 of 112,000") and estimatedMinutesRemaining. What is missing is an ALWAYS-PRESENT CONSUMER: one app-level host above the nav host that renders a minimized chip whenever phase == "running". Three screens collect that flow today (MapScreens:658, HomeStatePickerScreen:85, and TrailImporter's own) and all three are screen-scoped.
comment⚠ THIS IS AN ANDROID GAP TOO, not an iPad item. It is the application-screen model for every long runner: state import, the download queue, the OSM import stages, the Flight-guarded track sync / properties backfill, and delete-area. ⭐ It REFINES NONAV-2026-11-16 (see the handoff): "do not let them navigate from the long running app. period." becomes — navigation is permitted ONLY while a persistent indicator is present. On the iPad it does double duty, because the app must stay foreground for the job to run at all, so the chip is also what tells the rider not to leave. Pair it with UIApplication.isIdleTimerDisabled while any job runs — the mirror of GroupTrackScreen.kt:230's view.keepScreenOn = true, and Phase 5's power/wake socket (1 hit).

TIED TO 2.8b — THE JS BRIDGE, THE SPIKE, AND THE CERTIFICATION THAT WAS NEVER RUN (BRIDGEMEASURE-2026-11-15)

Fred, 2026-11-15 08:19–08:34: "it was a test of the map reuse and the java script tap interfaces porting over… a quick is there enough portability to warrant the approach. we need to certify those assumption the the kotlin port would function… so that was a proof of concept that the app could port. if it is built on wrong assumption we need to update and redo that stub… or do we stay the course on the steps top out the application and just start those steps. is there anything to gain form redoing the pic or do we just move on?"

⭐ THE PROCESS THAT ANSWERED IT (and the durable rule): MEASURE BOTH SIDES OF A SEAM BEFORE PORTING IT. The question "was the POC built on a wrong assumption" is unanswerable from the POC, because a POC's evidence is observation — trails appeared, clicks arrived — and observation cannot distinguish "the mechanism is right" from "the mechanism is right and the data feeding it is stale". It is answerable by enumerating the seam's members on both sides and diffing. Twenty minutes of grep produced: the full 14-method surface, the fact that none returns a value, the fact that no call site consumes one, the two divergent bridge objects, the GtSpatial ancestor, and the 36 unrun tests. None of those six is visible from running the app. This is the seam method turned on a seam we were about to port by eye, and it belongs in the master's durable rules once it has been used a second time.

✅ VERDICT: the POC's premise is sound, nothing is gained by redoing it, and the certification that matters is already written. See W6 in the handoff for the TRANSCRIBE-DO-NOT-REPAIR decision and its rationale.

FOUNDATION — BLOCKS EVERYTHING ABOVE IT C28-26319 · S3 — 36 of 37 iOS certification tests have never been executed

⭐⭐ FRED, 2026-11-15 08:38 — WHAT THIS TEST ACTUALLY IS: "this the foundation layer that all application objects are built upon. really big certification test."

This is not a routine test pass, and sizing it as one would be the mistake. The nine platform sockets plus the shared model are what every application object on iOS stands on — no screen, no map, no import, no ride, no housekeeping can be built above a layer that has not been proven on the platform. 37 tests is the right size for a foundation; the problem is that 36 of them have never run, which means the foundation under the entire 2.8b release is currently UNVERIFIED while the work above it is being planned.

Consequences for how we read the results: a failure here is blocking, not triage — it invalidates whatever sits on it rather than adding an item to a list. A failure in GtFiles, GtClock or GtImportPaths is worth more attention than a failure in any one feature, because every feature inherits it. And the inverse is the real prize: 37 green is the single largest de-risking event available on the iPad side, and it costs one command, because the tests are already written.
ties to2.8b, and it is the next command.
problemgrouptrack-shared/src/iosTest/ holds 37 *MacTest.kt files. Result XML exists for one: GtCertMBTiles17MacTest, 2026-11-14 21:52, tests=1 failures=0. The other 36 — including GtCertMapState17MacTest and GtCertMapRecipe17MacTest, the other two step-17 ports — have been compiled, never run. We have been describing three ports as certified while two of them have never executed on iOS.
anticipated solution./gradlew :grouptrack-shared:iosSimulatorArm64Test. One command. Expect failures: several will want fixture directories that only ever existed on the Droid side, and GtCertMBTiles17MacTest hard-codes …/GroupTrackSpike/_data/cert. Tie every failure to a key as it appears.
classCompiled is not run. Same class as G10's "compiling is not producing" — a build-stage success being read as a later-stage success. Third instance; worth promoting to a durable rule.
wheregrouptrack-shared/src/iosTest/kotlin/com/grouptrack/shared/cert/; results land in build/test-results/iosSimulatorArm64Test/.
evidence11-15 08:28: 37 source files counted, result XML enumerated — exactly one file, timestamped 11-14 21:52.
comment11-15: this supersedes W2's "stub app that calls the three ported objects" — these tests are that stub, asserted rather than eyeballed and needing no Xcode project. Verify by counting result files, not by reading the console.

TIED TO 2.9 — DATABASE SYNC RULES + PC / MAC PLANNING ACCESS TO THE MODEL

⭐⭐ 2.9 IN ONE PLACE — the 2026-11-15 10:33–11:05 design discussion, consolidated (R29SUMMARY-2026-11-15)

Written at Fred's direction (11:05): "just summarize this discussion into the 2.9 database sync rules creation with PC and Mac planning access to model." Everything below came out of one conversation; the cards further down this section hold the detail and the evidence.

1 · WHAT 2.9 IS FOR

Upload GroupTrack's data to AWS so that there is content worth participating for. The paywall is live immediately at $3.00/month; the first ~200 enrollees are couponed to a free year. The corpus accumulates during that year, and 3.0 sells access to it. So the export is the business gate, not a feature — if uploads fail quietly, 3.0 launches with nothing to sell.

2 · WHO HOSTS WHAT — the federation, and it is the most important structural decision

layersourceGroupTrack hosts?
rider trails, tracks, routes, waypoints, surveysAWS — the community layerYES — only this
base trailspublished trail sources (agency / Geofabrik)NO — publisher → device
mapsopen source maps (OpenFreeMap)NO — provider → device

Consequences: the ODbL prohibition becomes a shape rather than a rule — OSM-derived trails cannot reach our infrastructure because they never route through it. And hosting cost is bounded to what riders contribute, not to 50-state trail coverage or map tiles.

3 · UPLOAD RULES

  • Only rider trails travel: source_id = RIDER AND shared = 1. shared defaults to 0, so a filter bug omits rather than leaks — the right failure direction for a licensing constraint. The rider-trail category exists to be this firewall.
  • Each track carries its rider's survey.
  • The server collapses GEOMETRY (the alias rule) and NEVER the surveys — 20 riders on one trail → one trail, twenty ratings. That asymmetry is the product. contributions is keyed UNIQUE(artifact_type, artifact_id, user_id): a rider counts once and can update their own survey.
  • ⚠ The server must report WHICH RULE FIRED per submission — accepted-canonical / accepted-alias / omitted-hash-claimed / omitted-date-claimed / error. First-in deliberately drops data, so without per-submission accounting a defect is indistinguishable from correct behaviour, and after months of accumulation there is no way to learn what was lost.

4 · DOWNLOAD RULES — THE AREA IS THE UNIT

  • Trigger: the area you are PLANNING to ride. Not a sync of everything — an acquisition driven by intent. "No sense populating them unless you are going to ride there."
  • It UPDATES from current sources, so it runs more than once per area.
  • It is an ACQUISITION, not a sync — each source is authoritative for its own layer, so there is no merge and no conflict resolution in either direction.
  • THE RULES: trails update always · maps update always · the artifact list goes UP and only new artifacts come down. No timestamps, no versions, no cursors — the device's own list is the delta, which keeps the server stateless about the device.
  • Order: area create → area trails → rider created trails → tracks, routes and waypoints → maps. Base trails first because rider trails are matched against the real trails only (excludeRider = true).
  • ⭐ DUPLICATES ARE BY SHAPE, NOT BY HASH. Two riders over one ride have different points, so different hashes — GtRiderTrails.kt: "9 hash matches in 89,554". So the rule is "give me ground I do not have", not "give me hashes I do not have" — otherwise a rider is handed twenty traces of the trail they rode last week. Hash stays as the cheap first pass for exact re-imports. GtRiderScan.candidatesFor() already does shape matching.
  • ⭐ ALIASES ARE SERVER-SIDE ONLY. "What I call a track is my name for the track. I do not care what three other people called it. I always have mine, I don't need yours." Nothing to create, merge or reconcile on the device.
  • Each artifact type gets its own download rules — detailed at process time, not now.

5 · PC AND MAC PLANNING ACCESS TO THE MODEL

  • Online PC version — already has an identity: the React web client at grouptrack.org (specified V25_Note_…3.0Gateway_2026-06-04). SSL and API_BASE_URL are live.
  • Mac version — reduced: online planning functions only. ⚠ Which functions is undefined, and it decides whether the Mac is a trimmed web client or a third interface.
  • Three audiences, each with its own surface: individual rider (draw an area, pull trails with real ratings, contribute own tracks) · individual organizer (publish rides, invites, enrollment, followers, broadcast — a content multiplier, since participants' tracks feed the corpus) · group with organizers (member roster with owner/admin/member, shared ride and artifact library, one inherited radio config via config_mode = inherit_org).
  • Visibility: rides work today — is_public (default FALSE = invite only) plus org_id (NULL = personal, SET = org). A public individual ride is available to all groups; a group ride with is_public = FALSE is group-only. No schema change needed for rides.

6 · THE FRAME: WHAT IS MISSING IS NOT DESIGN

Fred 10:52: "the contact points and exchanges need to be implemented, but the methods and segmentation already exist." Measured true all morning — hash identity, the rider-trail firewall, shared opt-in, the alias rule, org_id/is_public, the three audiences in schema, bbox on both sides, per-area flags, track_surveys, and V3_FEATURES_ENABLED + API_BASE_URL already ported to grouptrack-shared AND certified on iOS 11-15. 2.9 and 3.0 are an implementation problem wearing a design problem's clothes. The job is the endpoints, the exchanges, and the places a user touches them — plus API expansion ("the RDS schema already supports 66+ fields across 5 tables; the PHP API uses ~20%").

7 · OPEN DECISIONS — all cheap now, expensive later

#decisionwhy it cannot wait
1Artifact visibility scope for group-private items. The *_properties tables have only a boolean shared — no owner scope — so "private items available to groups" is satisfiable for rides and not for tracks and trails. Recommend putting visibility on contributions, not the artifact, so geometry stays communal and collapses as designed while the contribution is group-scoped.Changes the upload path. Months of uploads under the wrong model cannot be re-scoped.
2A source_id category for community-downloaded rider trails.Reconcile step 3 ("stored rider trails this run no longer produces → REMOVED") would silently delete them — no local track produces them. One constant now, a data-loss bug later.
3Shape vs hash for LOCAL duplicate detection (see the POST-2.8 card).If the download uses shape and local inserts use hash, the device holds two different notions of "already have it" — worse than either alone.
4Survey coverage for routes and waypoints. Only track_surveys exists.If ratings are the product, a rated route — a planned ride someone enjoyed — is plausibly worth more than a rated track, and there is nowhere to put it.
5The exact "first 200" boundary and tie-break (count rule, same-second registrations).Small, and decided badly under pressure if left to launch.
6Which functions the Mac's "online planning only" includes.Decides trimmed client vs third interface — a scope difference, not a detail.
7Moderation of first-in canonical names.First-in claims the universal name. Nothing corrects a bad one or prevents a deliberately offensive one. A paid product needs an answer.
⚠ Deferred by Fred, deliberately: per-artifact-type download rules, and the detail of the sync rules generally — "we will work out the details when we address the item." Recorded here so the rules survive; the mechanics are process-time work.

NEW SECTION, created 2026-11-15 10:29. There was no 2.9 section anywhere, and no recertification item in the doc set at all — grepped both Drive volumes for recertif: zero hits. The only definition of 2.9 that existed was one line in GroupTrack_2.8_iPad_Plan_2026-11-10_v11.html.

⭐⭐ WHY 2.9 EXISTS — THE CONTENT ENGINE (Fred 2026-11-15 10:39)

"2.9 was always the upload of data from GroupTrack to AWS so there is data to create a benefit from participating in the app. Process was to run 3-6 months as a free app gathering data and content before a paywall is launched."

2.9 is not a technical release with a business side. It IS the business model's first stage. The sequence: ship 2.9 → the app runs free for 3–6 months while riders' tracks, waypoints and survey data populate the AWS model → only once a corpus exists that is worth having does 3.0's paywall launch. The product being sold in 3.0 is the content accumulated in 2.9.

Consequences that change engineering decisions — each one follows from the above, not from taste:

  • ⚠ The export is the BUSINESS GATE, not a feature. If uploads fail quietly for three months, there is no corpus and the paywall launches with nothing to sell. A silent upload failure is not a bug to fix later; it is the whole release failing invisibly.
  • ⭐⭐ THE FIRST-IN MODEL DELIBERATELY DROPS DATA, SO SILENT LOSS IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM CORRECT BEHAVIOUR. V3_TrackSharing_SpatialModel says a later submission of a claimed hash, or a claimed (hash,date), is "OMITTED — not stored, no duplicate". That is right by design. But it means "dropped because someone was first" and "dropped because of a defect" look identical from the device. Therefore the server must report WHICH RULE FIRED per submission — accepted-as-canonical / accepted-as-alias / omitted-hash-claimed / omitted-date-claimed / rejected-error. Per-submission accounting has to be in the design from the start; it cannot be added after three months of accumulation, because by then there is no way to know what was lost.
  • The 3–6 month clock starts when 2.9 SHIPS. So 2.9's ship date sets the paywall date, and any slip in 2.9 slips revenue by the same amount plus the gathering window.
  • This is why rider tracks DOWNLOAD. Participants get the benefit — other riders' trails — in return for contributing. The return path is not a convenience; it is the incentive that makes the free period produce content at all. Which settles CONFLICT 1 above: 2.9 cannot be one-way.
  • Upload completeness matters more than upload speed. A dropped track is lost content, and content is the asset. Favour idempotent retry and provable arrival over throughput.
  • ⚠ Survey data is the sharpest risk in the whole release. It is one of Fred's three named payloads, it has no design coverage in V3_TrackSharing_SpatialModel, and it has no certification harness (tracks and waypoints have 15c/15d and the whole-DB cert). Three months of uncertified survey uploads is three months of content that may not exist.
✅ CONFLICT 3 RESOLVED — Fred 2026-11-15 10:42: "I was thinking of a free year's access for the first 200 or so users." The two models are sequential, not competing: everyone free for 3–6 months while the corpus builds (no paywall exists yet) → then the paywall launches and the first ~200 users get a free year as the reward for having been the ones who built it.

✅ CORRECTED 10:43 — Fred: "they are the same thing if you limit the free year to the first 200 enrollees. The paywall is live immediately, it just coupons the first 200 enrollees."

⚠ Claude had this wrong and the error is recorded so the reasoning is not repeated. Claude assumed the paywall arrives AFTER the gathering period, and therefore warned that a year measured from created_at would expire early for the earliest contributors. That premise was false. The paywall is live from the start; there is no separate launch date to measure from. So created_at + 1 year is simply correct, and the "trap" does not exist.

⭐ THE DESIGN, STATED PLAINLY: the paywall is live immediately. The first ~200 enrollees are couponed to a free year. Everyone after them pays $3.00/month. The 3–6 month gathering period is therefore a CONSEQUENCE, not a mechanism — it is simply what happens while the first 200 riders ride out their free year and generate the corpus. Nothing has to be switched on later, and nothing has to be migrated at a launch boundary.

⭐ AND IT COLLAPSES TWO PROBLEMS INTO ONE CHANGE. The paywall spec's task 6 is "Remove year-2099 default from /users/register". That default becomes the coupon rule: at registration, if the enrollee count is under 200 then expires_at = NOW() + 1 year, otherwise the normal paid/trial state. One rule, in the one place that already needs changing. The free cohort needs no separate migration path at all.

Task 7 is a DIFFERENT population. "Migration plan for walkaway users to free/trial state" concerns the older V2.4 / V3-walkaway users who already exist with 2099 expiries — not the coupon cohort. The two must not be conflated: one is a historical cleanup, the other is a forward registration rule.

(b) "The first 200" must be CAPTURABLE from enrollment onward. They cannot be identified retroactively unless signup order is recorded during the free period. This is a 2.9 schema requirement driven by a 3.0 pricing decision — the kind of cross-release dependency that gets missed because each release reviews only its own scope.

✅ MEASURED: the schema already supports both. create_schema_v3_final.sql — users.created_at DATETIME DEFAULT NOW() gives signup order, and users.expires_at DATETIME NULL carries the comment -- NULL = free tier. No new fields needed; the free year is a dated expires_at.

⚠⚠ AND A LIVE TRAP, already on the paywall spec's own task list: 
6   Remove year-2099 default from /users/register              15 min
7   Migration plan for walkaway users to free/trial state      2 hr
/users/register currently defaults expires_at to year 2099. That is accidentally correct for the gathering period — everyone free, no expiry — and fatal at paywall launch, because every user who registered during those 3–6 months would carry a 2099 expiry and never be billed. Task 7 is exactly that migration, and Fred's free-year-for-the-first-200 is a natural fit for it: one UPDATE at launch setting expires_at = <launch> + 1 year for the earliest cohort and a trial state for everyone else.

Pricing confirmed from V3_Registration_Paywall_Spec_v2.docx: $3.00/month, with a Free vs Subscribed feature-comparison screen specified.

Nothing owed on the free-period model any more — it is settled. What remains is the count rule's exact boundary: "the first ~200" needs a definite number and a definite tie-break (enrollment order by created_at, and what happens if two register in the same second). Small, but it is the sort of thing that is decided badly under pressure if it is decided at launch.
superseded conflict-3 note GroupTrack_2.8_iPad_Plan_2026-11-10_v11.html describes 3.0's paygate as "e.g. the first 200 users free for a year". Fred's 10:39 statement describes everyone free for 3–6 months while data is gathered, then a paywall. These may be complementary (an open gathering period, then a paywall with the early cohort grandfathered) or they may be two different plans from different months. Not resolved here — it is a question for Fred, and it matters because it decides whether the free period is time-boxed, cohort-boxed, or both. See also GroupTrack_V3_Registration_Paywall_Spec_v2.docx, which has not yet been read.

⭐ 2.9 AS FRED DEFINED IT — 2026-11-15 10:33 (authoritative; supersedes the one-liner)

"2.9 is the recertification of the database — the export of data from current GroupTrack to populate the AWS model with tracks, waypoints, and survey data. Rider tracks can also be downloaded when connection with the AWS model is established. This version includes an online PC version as well as a Mac version, which is reduced functionality to only include online planning functions."

2.9 containsnote
Database recertificationNot an abstract model exercise — it is recertifying the data for export. The device model has to be provably exportable into the AWS model.
Export: tracks, waypoints, survey dataThree named payloads. Survey data is the one with no prior certification harness — tracks and waypoints already have 15c/15d and the whole-DB cert.
Download: rider tracksOnce a connection to the AWS model is established. This is a RETURN path.
An online PC versionNew platform in 2.9.
A Mac version, reducedOnline planning functions only. Not a port of the app — a deliberately smaller surface.
⚠⚠ TWO CONFLICTS WITH THE EXISTING DOC SET — recorded, not silently overwritten. Fred, 11-15 09:11, on exactly this hazard: "it would be nice to search all docs… and with the evolution be sure different decisions at different points do not conflict."

CONFLICT 1 — "one-way" is no longer true. iPad Plan v11 says "2.9 = ONE-WAY data sync Android → AWS". Fred's definition adds rider tracks downloading once a connection exists. That is bidirectional, and it changes the design: a one-way export needs no conflict resolution, no merge rules and no identity reconciliation on the device. A return path needs all three. This is the single largest consequence of the correction and it must be settled before 2.9 is scoped.

CONFLICT 2 — the PC/Mac planning station has MOVED from 3.0 to 2.9. iPad Plan v11 lists under 3.0: "AWS services: paygate, Mac/PC planning station against the AWS model, map-licensing review." Fred now places an online PC version and a reduced Mac version in 2.9. Either the plan is stale or the scope moved; Fred's statement is the authority and the iPad Plan needs the correction when it is next touched. Consequence: 2.9 grows from a sync release into a sync release plus two new client platforms, while the paygate stays in 3.0.

Owed: update GroupTrack_2.8_iPad_Plan_* (v11 is current) so the roadmap line and this section agree. Until then, this section is the one to trust.

2.9 DESIGN CONSTRAINT C28-26319 · do NOT single-thread the DB checks when 2.9 recertifies the data model

ties to2.9 — the data-model recertification that comes with the AWS alignment (ownership from V3, spatial and maps from the device).
problemMeasured 2026-11-15 on the first full execution of the iOS certification suite. The 37 classes run serially in one Kotlin/Native binary. One pass: 4 classes in 62 minutes, 90+ minutes projected for all nine heavy ones, and 1.8 GB of work directories left behind — because three classes each COPY a ~600 MB corpus (r15c_work 581 MB, t15e_work 632 MB, t15f_work 617 MB) and seven more read one end to end. 2.9 recertifies the DB model against a LARGER surface (device model vs AWS model, two sides to compare instead of one). At this shape the gate becomes unrunnable — and a gate too slow to run is a gate that does not get run, which is worse than a slow one because the absence is invisible.
anticipated solutionThree independent levers, cheapest first — do not design the 2.9 checks serially and fix it later:
1 · STOP COPYING. Three classes copy ~600 MB each for no reason but mutation safety. One shared read-only corpus plus a transaction that is rolled back gives the write tests their isolation without 1.8 GB of I/O. Biggest win, no parallelism needed.
2 · SHARD. The classes are independent — each opens its own connection and writes its own output file. Boot several simulators and run disjoint class sets at once. Nothing in the design prevents this today; it simply was not built that way.
3 · SPLIT THE SUITE. Measured 11-15: the 27 fast classes run in about 10 seconds each; the 9 heavy ones take 90+ minutes. The 27 are the routine check (every build); the 9 are a release gate (before a cut). Conflating them means neither happens at the right cadence.
classTest-harness scalability. Same class as the progress-signal defect found this morning: the harness was built to answer a question once, not to be run repeatedly, and the cost only appears at full scale.
wheregrouptrack-shared/src/iosTest/kotlin/com/grouptrack/shared/cert/ (37 classes); work dirs under …/GroupTrackSpike/_data/cert/.
evidence11-15 full run, uncapped: CERT 20,293 B at 14:26:52 · MAPCHECK 51,609 B at 14:50:32 · GT15B 662 B at 15:00:16 · GEOM 1,064 B at 15:09:10 — all four byte-identical in size to runs from 11-09, 11-10 and 11-14. Work dirs measured at 581/632/617 MB.
comment11-15, Fred 10:27–10:29: "good stress test. that's what causes confidence" → "add that bit about not single threading the db checks to the 2.9 recertification on db model." Found as a side effect of running the suite for the first time, not by design review — which is the argument for recording it now rather than rediscovering it when 2.9 starts and the deadline is live.
⭐ The determinism result is the other half of this. Four classes producing byte-identical output to runs spread across 11-09 to 11-14 — a week in which the Android side was refactored underneath them — is what makes the suite worth making fast. A flaky suite is not worth parallelising; a deterministic one is.

2.9 — DESIGN ALREADY EXISTS C28-26319 · V3_TrackSharing_SpatialModel_2026-06-28.md IS the 2.9 export design

what was foundA full design target for exactly what Fred described as 2.9 — how tracks are identified, stored and shared to the server. Do not redesign this; read it.
decisions it already fixesHash is identity — geom_hash (SHA-256 of geometry WKT), not name, not filename.
The DB controls every write, in all cases — code identifies and presents a record; the DB enforces dedup/uniqueness/aliasing via its own constraints. Makes every path idempotent.
Server canonical name = FIRST-IN FOR THE HASH. The first source ever to submit that geometry sets the universal name.
Server alias = FIRST-IN FOR (hash, date) — one alias per hash per date.
Everything else is OMITTED — a later submission of a claimed hash, or a claimed (hash,date), is dropped. Not stored, no duplicate.
The local preferred name does NOT override the universal. Two layers: local flexible, server deterministic.
Tracks are the only file-backed entity — waypoints and routes are DB records only, so the <hash>.gpx rules are tracks-only.
connection to today⭐ This is the model behind this morning's duplicate-alias investigation. The 08-11 rows with the same name and different hashes were the LOCAL layer doing what this design says it may do. The defect was in the local dedup, not in the model.
what 2.9 still owesFred's 10:33 definition names three export payloads: tracks, waypoints, survey data. This document covers tracks thoroughly and waypoints/routes in passing. SURVEY DATA is not in it at all — and it has no certification harness either (tracks and waypoints have 15c/15d and the whole-DB cert). Survey data is the thin spot in a release whose entire point is proving the data exports correctly.

⭐ SHAPE, NOT HASH — now proven on live data (SHAPEPROOF-2026-11-15)

Fred 2026-11-15: "duplicate tracks will be by shape and not by hash. points can be different for the exact same ride by two riders." And: "local duplicate creation should probably shift to shape rules once they are created. would have solved our rename issue from yesterday."

That was a design argument this morning. GtCertGeomMacTest turned it into a measurement this afternoon.

ResultValue
rows checked (real 11-08 spatial DB)230,109
SAME230,106 — incl. all 229,361 trails, 595 waypoints, 131 tracks
DIFF0 — geometry hashing is identical across engines
NONCANONICAL3 routes — c1c7ec1d, b5439e7d, be3da471

WHAT THE 3 ARE. Each is "stored with a space after the comma". The same ground, serialised differently, so it hashes differently. 0 DIFF establishes that the ground itself is not in question — only the text it is stored as.

⚠ SO HASH-BASED DEDUP IS ALREADY WRONG ON THIS DATABASE. Not theoretically, and not only for two riders recording the same trail — for three routes sitting in the live DB right now. A hash comparison reports them as distinct ground. A shape comparison does not. This is the same failure as the 11-14 rename issue, and it is why #r29's download rule is "give me ground I do not have" rather than "give me hashes I do not have".

OPEN — and now evidence-backed rather than speculative:

  • Canonicalise on write, so the format cannot vary. Cheap, and it stops new ones appearing.
  • Decide whether to canonicalise the 3 existing rows or leave them as the regression fixture. Leaving them has real value: they are the only known natural instance of the defect.
  • Shift local duplicate detection to shape, per Fred above. The 2.9 federation already assumes shape; the device does not yet.
  • The 9-in-89,554 OSM/UGRC hash-match figure now has a companion measurement from the other direction: 3 in 230,109 rows differ by format alone.

✅ CURRENCY: GOOD — better than feared

navigation_xref.txt header: Generated: 2026-11-14 21:10 branch 2.8 @ dfc26d797 files scanned: 273 composables: 243 Clickables: 418 state-opened panels: 57 routes: 0

  • Only 4 commits behind, and all four (2c38ffd81, 56580296f, c953e9c50, cba0c41ee) are docs or Gradle — zero Kotlin changes.
  • All four step-17 switches are INSIDE it — 374c9c5a4, 860be3a26, c11c1f524, 1fcad774f all landed before dfc26d797 (19:09). So it reflects the gutted MapStateStore, MapRecipe and MBTilesStore.

⛓ THE DEFECT: routes: 0 — and it is fatal for dead-code isolation

With no route anchors there are no entry points, so every panel lands under "Panels with clickables not reached from any route". A blast-radius reference with zero routes cannot distinguish live code from dead code — which is exactly what it was about to be relied on for.

ROOT CAUSE, measured: the generator greps for composable("route-string") — the OLD Compose navigation style. This app uses type-safe navigation. Routes are Kotlin types registered through an inline wrapper:

// ConvoyNavigation.kt:48  STD1D-2026-10-24
inline fun <reified T : Any> NavGraphBuilder.gtComposable( ... ) {
    composable<T> { entry -> ... GtTheme { scope.content(entry) } }
}
// ConvoyNavigation.kt:60   gtComposable<ConvoyRoutes.ConvoyAuthorityGate> { ... }

THE FIX — two changes:

  1. Scan core/navigation/. ConvoyRoutes is declared at core/navigation/src/main/kotlin/org/meshtastic/core/navigation/Routes.kt:179 — and gen_xrefs_v3.sh does not scan core/, despite its header claiming it "covers the WHOLE code base". That claim is wrong and is itself a checklist item.
  2. Parse gtComposable<ConvoyRoutes.X> (and composable<Namespace.X> for the upstream graphs), not composable(.

Expected result: routes: 0 → routes: 33, and the "not reached from any route" section becomes meaningful instead of listing everything.

⭐⭐ WHAT THIS LIST IS — the definition, the number and the order (PRUNETOTAL-2026-11-13)
Fred, 11-13 16:29: "items that have no impact on android functionality that need to be removed as we gen apple model."

⭐⭐ THE ENTRY TEST — “IT CANNOT BE IN USE”, not “it is unreferenced” (CDEF-V3-2026-11-13)
Fred, 11-13 16:36: “our definition it[’s] it can’t be in use. it may be there in code but if the structure that populate[s] and read[s] it no longer exist[s] it cannot be in use.”
⛔ This supersedes “proved unreferenced”. Unreferenced is SUFFICIENT but not NECESSARY, and the gap between them is the entire Class B category: the raster arms in enqueueCorridor, and all of G1g, are fully referenced and cannot execute. A reference-based test misses them; a usability-based test catches both.
So the evidence an item carries is not “nobody mentions it” — it is THE PRODUCER AND THE CONSUMER ARE BOTH GONE:
  • no producer — nothing can put data in, drive the branch, or reach the launch point. (G1g: begin() is the only writer of a migration record and sits inside unreachable code, so no record can exist.)
  • no consumer — nothing reads the result. (MBTilesStore:81’s format row: written on every store, read by nothing, anywhere.)
⚠ A leftover CONFIGURATION entry is not a producer. map_sources.json still lists google-hybrid and a default_slots entry for legacy key SAT — 14 of the original 15 sources were removed, per the file’s own _onesource note — but a config row nothing acts on is as inert as an unread metadata string.


≈ 2,344 LINES THAT NEVER EXECUTE. Removing them cannot change Android behaviour, because they are not reached — that is M2's theorem (#skip-policy): code not executed is by definition the same result under the new code. The claim rests entirely on the per-item reachability evidence below, nothing else.
WHY IT IS DONE AS THE APPLE MODEL IS GENERATED, and not sooner or later: the alternative is porting dead code, which then certifies VACUOUSLY — 0 DIFF because neither side runs — and is afterwards maintained on TWO platforms. A dead member ports perfectly and tells you nothing. Pruning before the gen is also why removal is deferred: a deletion is a change to the SHIPPING app, so it waits until the iPad build has shown nothing needed it.

MEASURED, AND DE-DUPLICATED. ⚠ The C-items below overlap — C1b's "~374 lines" is ConvoyCorridorDelete (234) plus corridorTiles (~140), which are also C7 and C5 — so adding the C column gives a wrong number. The split that does not double-count:
  WHOLE FILES, delete outright — 1,047 lines in 5 files: ConvoySourceClear 317 · ConvoyCorridorDelete 234 · ConvoySourceMigration 228 · ConvoyCorridorWorker 202 · TileCodec 66.
  PORTIONS OF LIVE FILES, surgery not deletion — 1,297 lines: ConvoyMapSourceScreen :175→end less the live SectionHeader = 877 · corridorTiles + stampPoint 147 · the two raster arms in ConvoyDownloadQueue (enqueueCorridor 102 + enqueueCorridorBatch 36) = 138 · GtRemoveTrackScreen 112 · estimateSizeMB/isWithinCeiling/AVG_TILE_KB 12 · ConvoyMarkerRenderer class ~10 · MBTilesStore:81's format = "webp" 1.
  ⭐ On that last line: it is written into the MBTiles metadata table and NEVER READ — not in MBTilesStore, not elsewhere in the app, and nothing in the map assets opens the file to read metadata; tiles are served by readTile(z,x,y). So it is an inert string in an unread row, not a correctness defect: the same Class B shape as everything else here, one level down — the statement runs, its OUTPUT is dead. ⚠ An earlier note called it a live correctness fix; that was Q4 applied to the code but not to its output.

⭐⭐ THE ORDER — and why biggest-first is right here. ConvoyMapSourceScreen's dead branch is the KEYSTONE: it is the only caller of three of the five deletable files.
  ConvoyMapSourceScreen dead branch (877) → ConvoyCorridorDelete (234, with GtRemoveTrackScreen 112) · ConvoySourceClear (317) → ConvoySourceMigration (228).
  So TWO surgical edits unlock 1,768 lines, 75% of the list, and ConvoySourceClear / ConvoySourceMigration need no direct handling at all — they fall in order. Independent and trivial: ConvoyCorridorWorker (202, zero callers) and TileCodec (66, one call site).
  ⛔ Biggest-first works because the biggest item is the LOWEST RISK PER LINE — one contiguous dead span, in one file, behind one condition. The delicate work is small: the 138 lines of raster arms sit INSIDE two live functions with many live callers (enqueueCorridor, enqueueCorridorBatch), where a slip breaks the live corridor download. Do that one LAST, alone, with its own build.

⚠ TWO THINGS THAT ARE NOT "no functional impact", and must not hide in the number:
  • GtRemoveTrackScreen's 112 lines — the CODE is unreachable, but removing it also takes the greyed menu row and its manual sections. Zero functional impact, NON-ZERO visible impact. Deliberate, and it travels with C9.
  • C2 is UNVERIFIED — noticed, never proved dead, and map_sources.json still offers google-hybrid as slot SAT. ⛔ It is NOT in the 2,344 and must pass Q4 before it joins the list. The total is only as good as the evidence, and C2 is the one item with none. Evidence for all four is in docs/GroupTrack_2.8_step17_G1_inventory_2026-11-13.html §5.

    The analysis as written 13:07: Fred's principle: "however we pull the maps is the method we need to use to remove them." The two derivations DO NOT MATCH:
      • DOWNLOAD (vector, live): GtVectorQueue.squaresFor — a z12 grid (SQ_Z=12), a 1-square ring around each point, interpolated every 0.02°, all tracks' squares unioned so shared ground is queued once.
      • DELETE (ConvoyCorridorDelete:135/191): ConvoyTileCalculator.corridorTiles(segments) — per-zoom tiles z10–z18, a 0.00724° cos(lat)-scaled buffer, no slot cap, funnel OFF.
    ⚠ ConvoyCorridorDelete has ZERO vector awareness — no isVector, no VectorTiles, no squaresFor anywhere in the file. It computes the old shape and calls MBTilesStore.deleteTiles(store, corridor).
    ⛔ CONSEQUENCE: a rider pressing delete-corridors removes only the tiles where the two sets happen to coincide and strands the rest — the same stranding the August comment warned about, arriving from the other direction. The store keeps growing.
    ⭐ NOT a port problem: G1a reproduces 2.7e faithfully, defect included, and the cert PASSES because both sides compute the same wrong set (a defect both sides reproduce identically is a PASS).
    Fix in principle: give the delete the same isVector branch the download has, so it derives squares via squaresFor and removes those. Small, but a behaviour change — Fred's call whether this is a 2.7f hot-fix or a 2.8 item.
    Reachable from: MapScreens:423/491, ConvoyMapSourceScreen:542/550, via ConvoyNavigation:156.
