  • ⛔⛔ V1 — BLAST ANALYSIS over every group already certified and switched. A GATE ON DECLARING STEP 17 DONE, not a cleanup. (BLASTCHECK-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 14:09: "before we complete we should use the function ref on the things we have processed already for blast ana[l]ysis. if we['ve] missed then here we could have missed things we have put to bed already and they will not bite us until we have a broken Mac implementa[t]ion."
    WHY — the method failed three times in one hour on 11-13, all the same way. Reachability was judged with ad-hoc greps that EXCLUDED the declaring file, which hides every INTERNAL caller. Three wrong calls inside G1a alone:
      • maxZoomForSource called "0 live refs" — it is called by calculateTiles at ConvoyTileCalculator:272, a member being kept.
      • buildTileUrl called "0 live refs" — its live declaration IS ConvoyTileDownloader:54 and its use at :177 is in that same file.
      • corridorTiles called live, then dead, then live again before the xref settled it.
    And one failure the Android cert cannot catch at all: GtTileCalc.tileRelPath was written into the port with no 2.7e counterpart (decl 0 in the xref; the Android ConvoyTileDownloader.tilePath has no callers either — the one apparent hit, GroupTrackScreen:1139, is a local variable of the same name). A ported member with no OLD original has nothing to compare against, so it passes certification by never being exercised.
    ⭐ THE RISK THIS GATE EXISTS FOR. Dropping a member that is actually live, or adding one that never existed, does NOT fail the Android certification — both sides agree, or the member is simply never called. It surfaces on the iPad as a missing symbol or a silent no-op, long after the group was signed off and we stopped looking. That is the "broken Mac implementation" Fred names.
    WHAT TO RUN — regenerate first (bash "$GT_CMDS/gen_xrefs_v3.sh"; the index is only as fresh as its last run), then for EVERY member of EVERY shared port already switched, three questions against docs/where_used_raw.txt read as declaration / internal / external, never with the declaring file filtered out:
      1. Fabrications — does each member of the shared port have a 2.7e declaration? decl 0 means it was invented and certifies nothing.
      2. Wrongly dropped — does each 2.7e member left OUT of a port have zero callers, counting uses inside its own declaring file?
      3. Orphaned callers — does each member KEPT have all its callers routed to the shared front door, with none still reaching the old Android implementation?
      4. ⛔ Can the branch it sits in ever execute? (BLASTQ4-2026-11-13.) Q1–Q3 are all CALLER-based and would have missed every straggler found on 11-13. Fred, 14:14: "removal followed[,] the trick is the function[s] were still embedded in functions that were still used with[out] any way to call or access them." The removal was done; what survived is dead code inside live functions. Two classes:
        • CLASS A — symbol-level dead, hidden behind a chain. corridorTiles has no live caller — but only once you know ConvoyCorridorDelete and ConvoyCorridorWorker are themselves dead. The xref finds this provided the chain is followed all the way down instead of stopping at "it has callers".
        • CLASS B — branch-level dead inside a LIVE symbol. ⛔ THE XREF CAN NEVER FIND THIS. ConvoyDownloadQueue.enqueueCorridor is live with many live callers, yet everything after if (GtMapEngine.isVector(context)) { … return … } is unreachable. The index’s unit is the SYMBOL; the dead thing is a BRANCH. No amount of caller analysis sees it.
        HOW TO RUN Q4 — cheap, because the gates are few and known. List the conditions that can never vary, then read what sits behind them: GtMapEngine.isVector() = cached ?: true, isVectorCached() = true (VECONE-2026-11-01 "the only map"), and any state declared mutableStateOf(false) that is never assigned — which is exactly how optTracks (ConvoyMapSourceScreen:233) and selectedSlot (:82) killed 779 lines of G1g without a single caller disappearing. Everything behind if (!isVector), or after an if (isVector) { … return }, is dead whatever its enclosing function’s caller count says.
      5. ⛔⛔ WRONGLY KEPT — does each member PUT INTO the port have a reachable caller? THE ONLY ONE OF THE FIVE THAT FIRES BEFORE THE PORT IS WRITTEN. (BLASTQ5-2026-11-13.) Fred, 14:19: "had we stuck to the original plan we would have converted all the ma[th], none of that code would have executed and we would have moved on leaving the mess in place."
        The hole it closes. "Port AS IS, warts and all" is the right rule for FAITHFULNESS and says nothing about NECESSITY. Followed alone on 11-13 it would have carried into grouptrack-shared: corridorTiles + stampPoint (~140 lines), estimateSizeMB + isWithinCeiling + AVG_TILE_KB, the fabricated tileRelPath, and all 779 lines of G1g. An earlier draft of GtTileCalc.kt said so in as many words — "Ported to keep the OLD/NEW comparison total".
        ⛔⛔ AND IT WOULD ALL HAVE PASSED CERTIFICATION. Dead code ports perfectly: OLD and NEW agree exactly, 0 DIFF, two clean runs, signed off. The cert compares OLD against NEW, so it is a faithfulness test and NOT a necessity test — it cannot tell you a member should not exist, because a member nothing calls behaves identically on both sides. ⚠ Q1–Q4 miss it too: the member HAS a 2.7e declaration (not Q1), was not dropped (not Q2), has no callers to orphan (not Q3), and need not sit behind a constant gate (not Q4).
        ⭐ Why it is worth a question of its own. The cost is not a bug — nothing breaks. It is dead code PROMOTED into the shared module, certified, carried to iOS and maintained on two platforms forever, which is strictly worse than leaving it in the Android app where it was only in one place. ⚠ So ask it while writing the port, not at the end: by then the member is certified and the evidence says it is correct.
        The rule, stated: port AS IS — but only what is REACHABLE. Faithfulness governs HOW a member is ported; reachability governs WHETHER it is.
        ⭐ WHY THE BIG MOVES MISSED THIS CLASS. The convoy-app move, one map and removing the tick all certified back to CALLERS, which was right and caught everything that stopped being called. Class B never stops being called. Neutralising a feature by making its gate constant leaves the code behind the gate intact, reachable on paper and unreachable in fact — so it survives a caller-based sweep untouched. These are the stragglers.
    SCOPE — FIXED, AND IT CANNOT GROW. Fred, 14:11: "everything completed ahead of us should use the ref function by field or function. this way we limit what we have to perform the blast analysis on." (BLASTSCOPE-V3-2026-11-13.) V1 therefore covers ONLY the backlog that predates the discipline:
      • G2a–G2g — step 17 group G2, closed 11-13 before the method changed.
      • 15a / 15e, 16b / 16c, 17e1 / 17e2 — the earlier shared work the later ports depend on (GtMath, GtTileMath, GtVectorQueue, the import seams).
      • Nothing from G1a onward — those carry their own check, M1 below. ⛔ Do NOT add later groups to this list; adding them is the failure this split exists to prevent.
    ⭐ Why fixing the scope matters. Left open, V1 would face all of step 17 at the end — the largest surface at the latest moment, with the least appetite for finding anything. That is how a final gate becomes a rubber stamp. A fixed backlog plus a per-group check means the end-of-step work is small and the verification is done while each group is still fresh.

    ⭐⭐ M1 — THE PER-GROUP CHECK. Standing method, from G1a on. The LAST action of closing any group, after the two clean cert runs and the switch, before the commit.
    Run the three questions above over that group’s members only — fabrications, wrongly dropped, orphaned callers — and record the answers in the group’s own findings doc. A group is not closed until they are recorded; "certified and switched" is not the same as "verified complete".
    BY FIELD AND BY FUNCTION — both indexes, because a port moves both:
      • docs/where_used_raw.txt — functions, derived from function_universe_raw.txt.
      • docs/field_crossref_raw.txt — the field-level cross-reference. ⚠ A field read by code the port left behind fails exactly like a dropped function, and the function index will not show it.
    ⛔ Regenerate before each group’s check (bash "$GT_CMDS/gen_xrefs_v3.sh"): the indexes are only as fresh as their last run, and a port that just switched its callers is precisely the change a stale index cannot see.
    ⛔ Read decl / internal / external separately, and never filter out the declaring file — that single habit produced all three wrong calls listed above.
    ⛔⛔ WHEN — THIS IS THE LAST STEP BEFORE THE 2.8a DROID BUILD. Fred, 14:10: "that has to be reviewed last[,] the last step for building a 2.8a droid app." (BLASTSEQ-V2-2026-11-13, replacing v1's looser "before step 18 starts".)
    ⭐ Why last and not sooner. 2.8a is the Android release cut FROM the finished shared code, so step 17 is complete at that moment and nothing further will move. Run the analysis then and it covers ALL of it — G1 through G7 in one pass, against one regenerated index. Run it earlier and it certifies a moving target: every group closed afterwards is unchecked, and the gap is invisible because the groups it did cover all passed.
    So the order is: finish step 17 → regenerate the indexes → V1 blast analysis over every port → then cut 2.8a → then step 18 (the iPad build).
    ⚠ 2.8a is the checkpoint that matters because it is the last build where a miss is cheap. Android has the tooling, the device harness and the cert runner; the iPad has none of that yet. A wrongly dropped or fabricated member that survives into 2.8a surfaces on the iPad as a missing symbol or a silent no-op, on the platform with the least tooling, after the group was signed off — which is the whole reason the certification method exists.
  • ⚠ T1 — TOOLING DEFECT, DIAGNOSED AND DEFERRED: the navigation xref mislabels every ListItem row. (NAVXREFLABEL-2026-11-13)
    Why it matters now: Fred, 11-13 14:21 — "the purpose of the application walk through would be more likely to surface these issues." The walkthrough is the designated instrument for REACHABILITY (the cert proves faithfulness, the xref proves completeness, only the walkthrough proves a user can get there), and docs/navigation_xref.txt — "the click tree, for the user manual" — is what produces it. It currently reports launch points pointing at the wrong screens.
    THE SYMPTOM, from the 11-13 01:08 index at 0c6774b48:
      • ConvoySettingsScreen.kt:267 | “Change Map Sources” | onNavigateToProfile() — line 267 is the PROFILE row; its real label is "Edit rider profile" (line 265).
      • ConvoySettingsScreen.kt:276 | “Send issue reports by email” | onNavigateToMapSources() — line 276 is the MAP SOURCES row; its real label is "Change Map Sources" (line 274).
      • "Edit rider profile" appears ZERO times in the whole file. Labels are shifted one row up against actions, and the first label of each run is lost.
    ROOT CAUSE — label_near() in $GT_CMDS/navxref_2026-11-09.py (called at :193 with the CLICKABLE’s position):
      win = body[pos : pos + 1600] searches FORWARD from the clickable, and returns the first match. In a Material3 ListItem the label is the FIRST argument and modifier = Modifier.clickable{} is the LAST, so the forward window runs past the closing paren into the NEXT row and takes its headline. The backward fallback (back = body[pos-400 : pos]) sits below and is never reached, because forward always matches something.
    THE FIX — in label_near, before the forward search: if the text immediately behind pos shows we are inside a ListItem( / headlineContent, take the LAST Text(…) BEFORE pos instead of the first one after it. Strictly conditional, so every other clickable is byte-identical. New filename per the golden rule (navxref_v2_….py), never a reissue.
    BLAST RADIUS — small but exactly the wrong 6: 6 of 418 clickables use the label-before-modifier shape, and they are Settings-style rows, i.e. launch points. ⚠ A walkthrough reading this file would conclude "Change Map Sources" opens the profile screen.
    VERIFICATION when it is done: regenerate, then diff — clickables must stay 418, EXACTLY 6 labels may change, nothing else. A larger diff means revert the one .py and think again. The previous output is recoverable: docs/navigation_xref_2026-11-09.txt is a dated copy beside the live file.
    ⭐ WHY IT IS SAFE TO DEFER, AND WHY IT WAS. The risk of the fix itself is low — it is a GENERATOR, not the app: it cannot break the build, the ports or a certification, and its output has a dated backup. The reason to wait is focus, not danger: the tool is needed at V1 (the last step before the 2.8a Droid build), so fixing it mid-G1 buys that work nothing. ⚠ There is also one real coupling — gen_xrefs_v3.sh regenerates ALL FOUR indexes, so running it mid-port swaps out the where_used_raw.txt the port work is querying. One variable at a time.
    DO IT: at a break point, or as the first step of V1 — whichever comes first. It must be fixed BEFORE V1 runs, since V1 leans on the walkthrough.
  • ⭐⭐ M2 — SKIP, DON’T REMOVE. Standing rule for every remaining step-17 piece. (SKIPPOLICY-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 15:13: "do we remove or just skip as not used. I prefer to skip if it is no[t] called an[d] place the clean after the initial iPad gen."
    Two decisions that were being run together, and must not be:
      • SKIP — the member or file is NOT PORTED into grouptrack-shared. Free: it changes no Android code, nothing that ships in 2.8a moves, and there is nothing to retest. Decided at port time by M1’s five questions.
      • REMOVE — the Android code is DELETED. That is a change to the shipping app, so it carries real risk and gets its own pass. ⛔ Deferred until AFTER THE INITIAL iPAD GENERATION, by which point the iPad build exists and has shown that nothing needed it.
    ⭐ Why the separation matters: skipping is a decision about the PORT; removing is a decision about the APP. A wrong skip costs a port later. A wrong removal costs a defect in a release. Keeping them apart keeps the expensive mistake out of reach.
    ⚠ This supersedes the earlier "DELETE rather than PORT" wording used for G1g in C8 above. G1g is SKIPPED — not ported, not yet deleted. Its removal is a C-list item like the rest, and the C-list is already timed post-2.8/Mac, which is the same boundary Fred names here.
    So the step-17 question is never "should this be deleted?" It is "is this reachable?" — and if not, it is skipped and written down. Deletion is a separate activity with its own schedule.

    ⭐⭐ WHY A SKIP IS PROVABLY SAFE — the basis of this rule. Fred, 11-13 15:14: "code not executed is b[y] definition same result under the new code." That is the whole argument, and it is not a judgment call: unexecuted code contributes nothing observable, so omitting it leaves behaviour identical BY CONSTRUCTION. Skipping unreachable code cannot change what the app does.
    Consequence 1 — A SKIP NEEDS NO CERTIFICATION RUN. Its evidence is REACHABILITY, not an OLD/NEW comparison. This is why G1b was settled in twenty minutes with the cross-reference instead of a build / deploy / cert cycle, and why a skipped piece still closes properly: the finding is written down with its call sites, and that IS the proof. ⚠ So a skip must never be recorded as "certified" — nothing was certified. It was shown to be unreachable, which is a different and stronger claim.
    Consequence 2 — CERTIFYING UNREACHABLE CODE IS VACUOUS, which is Q5 from the other direction. A dead member ported faithfully gives 0 DIFF because NEITHER SIDE RUNS. The pass carries no information. That is why "it certified clean" can never be the reason a member belongs in the port.
    ⛔ THE ONE THING THIS DOES NOT LICENSE. The theorem holds only for code that is genuinely never executed, so the ENTIRE weight rests on the reachability evidence being right — and on 11-13 three reachability calls were wrong in one hour, all from greps that excluded the declaring file. The argument is sound; the input is what needs the care. Hence M1’s five questions, hence by FIELD and by FUNCTION, hence never filtering out the declaring file.
  • C10 — TileCodec.kt (66 lines): SKIPPED in G1b, remove post-iPad. The WebP re-encoder is pre-vector legacy.
    G1b was inventoried as "the first declared SOCKET in step 17 — a seam contract, not a port". It turns out to be neither: there is no platform codec work left to declare a seam for, because nothing re-encodes any more.
    WHY IT IS DEAD. TileCodec is V2.6a, built to hit a "35-40% aggregate storage-reduction goal" on raster satellite/topo imagery. Under vector maps there is no such imagery, and encode returns at its first line for everything now downloaded:
      • VectorTiles.downloadSources → TileLayer("overlay", …, "pbf", …) — role overlay, so if (isOverlay) return bytes. Correct: vector tiles are protobuf and re-encoding them as an image would corrupt them.
      • ElevationTiles.downloadSources → TileLayer("overlay", …, "png", …) — role overlay. Also correct, and ElevationTiles:24 says why: DEM encodes elevation IN THE PIXEL VALUES, so "re-encoding (as satellite base images are, to WebP) would change them -- spikes and pits in 3D".
      • the only role: 'base' entries left in map_sources.json are google-hybrid and a custom-source template — the pre-vector raster sources, whose store has been empty since the conversion.
    WHERE USED (fresh index): TileCodec has exactly ONE call site, MBTilesStore:156, plus one doc comment in VectorTiles:37. compressWebp, BASE_QUALITY and OVERLAY_QUALITY have ZERO external references.
    ⚠ AND A DEAD BRANCH INSIDE THE DEAD FILE (a Q4 catch): because encode returns early for overlays, val quality = if (isOverlay) OVERLAY_QUALITY else BASE_QUALITY can only ever be BASE_QUALITY, so OVERLAY_QUALITY = 100 is unused and the entire WEBP_LOSSLESS arm of compressWebp is unreachable. The file’s own header still documents "OVERLAY → LOSSLESS WebP", which the code stopped doing at [V2.6a-Q95-PASS].
    ⭐ WEBP IS STILL LIVE ON THE READ SIDE — do not conclude otherwise (Fred 15:11, "we do use we[bp] on html ... screens"). It is the MAP’s HTML, not the manual’s: GroupTrackScreen:1148/1162/1175 serve WebResourceResponse("image/webp", …) under // [V2.6a-WEBP] read intercepts serve image/webp. Those are the SAT tile reads already listed as C2, and they are WebView code, so they do not port either way. ⚠ The manual’s own images are JPEG — 182 data:image/jpeg embeds, and ZERO .webp files exist anywhere in the repo or the Drive folder.
    THIRD ITEM, newly found: MBTilesStore:81 records format = "webp" in the MBTiles metadata for every tile, which no longer describes what is stored (VEC is pbf, DEM is png). A one-line correctness issue — recorded, not fixed.
  • ⭐⭐⭐ THE RASTER HEAD — why the prune pile exists, and the pre-port gate it produced (M3). (RASTERHEAD-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 16:44: "probably the old downloads and writes not attached to a source that would actually deliver tiles." · 16:41: "I am wondering if this is the misconception pile that we did not know what to do with."

    ⭐ THE MODEL — one subsystem with its head cut off. The prune pile is not random leftovers. 9 of its 10 items are stations on the RASTER TILE PIPELINE, which still exists end to end in code while its source no longer delivers anything:
      MapSourceManager (google-hybrid, slot SAT — configured, never displayed) → ConvoyDownloadWorker:180's else-branch → ConvoyTileDownloader / corridorTiles → TileCodec.encode (role "base") → MBTilesStore (SAT, SAT_LABELS_*) → the ESRI read intercepts. Plus the operations on those stores: ConvoyCorridorWorker, ConvoyCorridorDelete, GtRemoveTrackScreen, ConvoySourceClear, ConvoySourceMigration, ConvoyMapSourceScreen. (The 10th, ConvoyMarkerRenderer, is a marker JS bridge — unrelated.)
    The source stopped delivering and everything downstream stopped running while remaining fully referenced. That is why no reference-based search found it.

    ⭐ WHY NO CERTIFICATION EVER TOUCHED IT — tested, 10 for 10. Searched every Cert*.kt / GtCert*.kt in app and shared: ConvoyCorridorDelete, ConvoySourceClear, ConvoySourceMigration, ConvoyCorridorWorker, TileCodec, corridorTiles, SAT_LABELS, GtRemoveTrackScreen, ConvoyMapSourceScreen, ConvoyMarkerRenderer — ZERO certification files each. For contrast: SpatialDbManager 16, VectorTiles 4, MapSourceManager / GtVectorQueue 3, ConvoyTileCalculator 2.
    The certification work to date followed THE DATA — reads, writes, lookups, imports, queues, state. Anything participating in I/O got certified; what never matched an I/O path was set aside and never classified. It accumulated as the pile nobody knew what to do with, and today's finding is WHY it never matched: it does not run.
    ⭐ So "zero certifications" is a cheap PREDICTOR of unreachability and a good first filter. ⚠ It is only a predictor: MBTilesStore also shows zero and is thoroughly live — it is exercised INDIRECTLY (the writes/reads baselines go through SpatialDbManager and the queue, never naming it). Use it to decide where to look, never as the verdict.

    ⛔ THE DANGER THAT REMAINS: raster-only branches INSIDE the shared trunk. The vector pipeline shares ConvoyDownloadWorker, ConvoyTileDownloader, MBTilesStore and ConvoyDownloadQueue with the raster one, so those files are LIVE. The dead parts are branches within them — enqueueCorridor's arm, the role == "base" path, ConvoyDownloadWorker:180's else. Class B: referenced and unreachable, invisible to every tool except the constant-gate check (Q4).

    ⭐ THE ENTRY TEST'S THIRD VARIANT — EXECUTED BUT UNCONSUMED. Three shapes all pass "no consumer" while failing "unreferenced":
      • MBTilesStore:81 writes a format metadata row that nothing anywhere reads.
      • GroupTrackScreen:1154/1167 intercept ESRI URLs (/Reference/World_Transportation/, /Reference/World_Boundaries_and_Places/) that nothing sends, reading SAT_LABELS_* stores that no source can create — those cache_dirs appear NOWHERE in map_sources.json. This closes C2.
      • ConvoyMapSourceScreen:79/81 call getAllSources() / getSlotAssignments() on every open and the credits branch consumes neither — of its ten bindings only selectedSlot is used, and only as the if that picks the branch. Fred, 16:50: "reachable from the display panel that sho[w]s open map source licensing. somebody just repurposed the store." The credits are HARDCODED strings; the source-registry state it still initialises is vestigial.
    ⚠ A reference-based test catches none of these. "It cannot be in use" catches all three.

    ⭐⭐ M3 — THE PRE-PORT ELIGIBILITY GATE. Run BEFORE porting a piece, not at its close.
    Fred, 16:41: "I do not know how that was even identified as needing certification." — and the honest answer is that nothing identified it. The G1 inventory was built by FUNCTIONAL GROUPING ("map store and map sources"), measured by line count, with everything assumed live. No reachability test was applied to any of the 13 files. That is why it contained two pieces that were never port work and called one of them "the first declared SOCKET".
    So the port list must be DERIVED from reachability, not from a file listing. M1 stays as the closing check (Q3, after the switch); M3 is the opening one, and it is cheap — index queries and reading, no builds, no deploys, no cert cycles. The biggest file in G1 took about ten minutes.
    THE QUESTION, per member — mechanical, not a judgement call: is its producer/consumer on the VECTOR path, the RASTER path, or BOTH? Raster only → cannot execute → C. Vector or shared trunk → port and certify.
    THE ROW, per member: what · referenced (decl / internal / external, never filtering the declaring file) · producer (what changes it) · consumer (what uses it) · verdict. For a SKIP that row IS the justification — it is what C2 lacked for a month.
    ⚠ Give it a fixed output and a stopping rule. It is open-ended reading, and 11-13 showed how fast that becomes an all-day diversion.

    ⭐ THE FOUR REMAINING G1 VERDICTS — run 11-13 16:50-17:00
    PieceImpacts executing code?Verdict
    G1d MapStateStore + ConvoyMapRecipeYes, heavily — checkedIdsFor 23 refs, rideKey 12, readMap 8, all from GroupTrackScreen; the recipe feeds MapConversion / EmergencyRestorePORT. Dead and trivial: ViewState, writeDaily, KEEP_DAYS
    G1e MapSourceManagerPartly. Live core: TileLayer (the type VectorTiles/ElevationTiles return), init, setActive, getOverlayJson, getSlotSources, getDownloadSources, activeSourceKeyPORT THE CORE ONLY. Dead head: 8 public functions with ZERO external refs (getOnlineUrl, getLocalUrl, getOverlayLayers, getAllOverlayLayers, getActiveOnlineUrl, getActiveLocalUrl, resolveUrl, terminatedColumns) + updateSlotSource, getApiKey/saveApiKey, isSourceAvailable, getAllSources, getSlotAssignments — all reached only from unreachable screen code. ⭐ And the column-file WRITE chain behind updateSlotSource can never run; the READ path at init still does
    G1f MBTilesStoreYes — insertTile, readTile, tilesInBounds, deleteSource (16), closeAll (13), the count/zoom helpersPORT. ⚠ Two members' only callers are DEAD: countTiles ← ConvoyCorridorDelete:213, deleteTiles ← :140.     G1h GroupTrackConversionYes — the authority gate calls isComplete, isFreshInstall, recordFreshInstall, inspectMaps, moveOutMaps, copyMaps, renameSources; ConvoySettingsScreen:106 calls readRecordPORT — but see the CODE RULE 3 question below

    ⚠ THE HONEST HEADLINE: the remaining PORT work does not collapse. 2,257 → roughly 1,900. The raster-head insight cut the PRUNE pile, not the port pile, because the port pile is the shared trunk — which is exactly why it survived.

    ⛔ THE OPEN QUESTION FOR FRED — G1h and CODE RULE 3 (664 lines). GroupTrackConversion is the ONE-TIME SAF storage conversion. It is reachable, so M3 says port it — but CODE RULE 3 says one-time launch code carries no marker and WE remove it when the next release is cut. If every install has converted, 664 lines may be removable rather than portable. That is a release decision, not a tooling answer, and it is the single biggest open item on the remaining list.

    ⚠ AND THE WARNING THAT MATTERS MOST FOR G3–G7. The group list was produced by the same functional-grouping criterion that put two dead pieces inside G1, and it has never been reachability-tested. G3 (trail rules / land 2,559) · G4 (1,649) · G5 (pure ride logic 1,665) · G6 (exports / sharing 900) · G7 (server and catalogs 994) = 7,767 lines, none of it examined. ⭐ Run M3 over each BEFORE porting it. Some fraction will not need porting either, and finding that out costs index queries, while not finding it out costs a port, a vacuous certification, and maintenance on two platforms.
  • ⭐⭐⭐ THE RESTRUCTURED TASK LIST — BY SEAM, NOT BY FILE. The gate before 2.8a can be built as a testable lab. (SEAMTASKS-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 17:01: "organize like items for certification so we [ad]dress in the same pass the io items etc." · 17:04: "detailing the function[] calls to be certified in that seam … we will end up with a day or two of seam work certification that must be completed before the android refactored version can be built and deployed as a testable lab."
    WHY BY SEAM. The seam is the expensive part; the members around it are cheap once it exists. One seam design serves many members — and the alternative is what happened on 11-13, where G1c built GtMapSpecEnv.readAsset and G1e will need the same thing again. Cross-device risk also CLUSTERS by seam: SQLite differs between the platforms, file paths differ, clocks differ. Grouping by seam puts that risk in one pass with one harness instead of spreading it thin.

    ⭐⭐⭐ THE THREE LEVELS. We have been conflating L2 with L3 and paying L2 prices for L3 work. (SEAMLEVELS-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 17:08: "if we group the seam we then have io reads and writes form 30 processes, the semantics read and write function we are certifying that we can read or write doesn't matter where or how many the method changes and the method is certified for all activities for that entity on the seam."
    Fred, 11-13 17:13: "we keep conflating functional certification for a specific task or activity (combining many seam certified activities) with seam certfications. can I read or write, does this method to access or write dat exist or do I need to add a new method."
    levelthe question it answersunitcosthow often
    L1 — SEAM EXISTENCEDoes a method to read or write this entity exist in the shared model, or must one be added?the entityminutes — an index queryonce per entity, at port time
    L2 — SEAM CERTIFICATIONDoes that method read and write identically on both platforms?the methodsubstantial — this is the real workONCE. Every caller inherits it.
    L3 — FUNCTIONAL CERTIFICATIONDoes an activity that combines already-certified seam operations reach the same result?the activity / the membercheap — the seam is scriptedone per activity

    L2 IS CERTIFIED ONCE FOR ALL CALLERS — and we have already done it twice, by accident:
    • GtTileMath: lon2tile / lat2tile certified once at 1,938,346 values. G1a's own findings doc then states that "lon2tile/lat2tile are NOT under test" — the caller inherited the seam and nobody re-certified it.
    • GtHkDb (G2f), in its own header: "the statement a side DECIDED to run is a DECISION and is compared. Whether SQLite would honour it is a different certification." That sentence is the L2/L3 split, written down three days before we named it.
    What the conflation costs. MBTilesStore's 18 functions read as 18 certifications. They are one L2 (can the shared model read and write a tile store at all) plus 18 cheap L3s (does each call pick the same rows and build the same statement). The same correction applies to G3–G7's 7,767 lines: those are mostly callers of a handful of seams. The step-17 estimate scales with SEAM COUNT, not line count.

    ⭐ L1 ANSWERED FOR EVERY REMAINING G1 ITEM (measured 11-13 17:15 against the 2.7e sources).
    itemlinesplatform calls measuredseams it needsdo they exist?verdict
    G1d MapStateStore + ConvoyMapRecipe390JSON 29 · File 7 · Context 7 · clock 1 · SQLite 0 · SAF 0file read/write · JSON · clockGtStorage+okio · GtOrgJson · GtTime — ALL EXISTNO NEW SEAM. L3 only.
    G1e MapSourceManager599JSON 25 · File 5 · Context 11 · SQLite 0 · SAF 0 · SharedPreferences 0file read/write · JSON · httpGtStorage+okio · GtOrgJson · GtNetworks — ALL EXISTNO NEW SEAM.     G1f MBTilesStore605SQLite 32 · File 3 · Context 0 · JSON 0 · clock 0SQLite open + statementgtOpenDatabase (db/GtDb.kt) over androidx.sqlite SQLiteConnection — EXISTSSEAM EXISTS. One L2 pass on the tile-store schema, then 18 L3s.
    G1h GroupTrackConversion663SAF/DocumentFile 19 · Context 15 · clock 11 · JSON 8 · File 6SAF document tree · file · clock · JSONthe last three exist; SAF HAS NO SHARED SEAM — the only new socket in all of G1✅ RETIRED — NOT PORTED. (G1HRETIRE-2026-11-13) Fred, 11-13 21:50: "yes that can be retired. nothing was published on google that used that functionality." CODE RULE 3 applies, and on the strongest possible basis. (G1HBASIS-2026-11-13) Fred, 11-13 21:51: "yhta was teh first publihsed public group track offering. it is the decline to publish from google is what caused the conversion" — i.e. the 2.6g build of 09-13 WAS GroupTrack's first public publication, and the conversion existed in order to GET published. Google declined the All Files Access declaration, so the SAF migration was built as the price of admission — not as a migration for an installed base, because there was no public installed base yet. ⭐ So the retirement does not rest on "probably everyone has converted by now". It rests on the stronger fact that there was never anyone to convert — no public device ever held shared-storage GroupTrack data that this engine was needed to move. The only devices that ever exercised it were Fred's own (Droid 1 proven 09-12, Droid 2) and the internal testers. No rider holds a half-converted state or a stray -EXT folder because no rider ever started one. The SAF socket is never built.
    ⭐ AFTER THE 21:50 RETIREMENT OF G1h: G1 NEEDS ZERO NEW SOCKETS. G1d and G1e need no new seam; G1f's seam already exists (gtOpenDatabase) and needs one L2 on the tile-store schema; G1h is gone. The only genuinely new L2 in the group has been removed rather than built — 663 lines not ported and a socket not designed. This also makes Fred's two-day estimate the right number for Pass 2: G1d ≈ half a day, G1e ≈ one day, G1f ≈ one day, and G1h's 1–1.5 days is now zero. Fred's 17:09 read ("half the entities already exist in GtHkDb") was conservative.

    ⭐ THE EXISTING SEAM SURFACE — CHECK THIS BEFORE ADDING A SOCKET. commonMain public fun/expect counts, 11-13:
    GtSettings 42 (socket + GtSession) · GtWrite 32 · GtRead 28 · GtHkEnv 26 · GtOrgJson 20 · GtTime 19 · GtSpatial 18 · GtImportDb 16 · GtNetworks 16 · GtStorage 11 · GtDbAuditPort 4 · GtSchema 3 · gtOpenDatabase 1.
    236 seam functions are already in service. An L1 question is answered from this list, not from a new design.

    ⚠ WHAT THIS DOES NOT CHANGE. An L3 pass is cheap only once its L2 holds. A member whose seam is uncertified is not cheap, and a member with no reachable caller is neither: it goes to the C list, because an unused method cannot be functionally certified at all (11-13 16:58). The lists below are L3 sets; each seam's own L2 is named under its Contract.

    ⭐⭐⭐ L4 — THE PROCESS WALKTHROUGH, DRIVEN BY THE QUICK START. The last gate before 2.8a is a testable lab. (WALKTHROUGH-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 17:22: "every process will have to be rectified as a process on the droid version near and start over. using the quickstart manual for the sequence so we exercise that manuals correctness"
    WHAT IT IS. Once the refactored Android build exists, every process is re-certified as a process on the Droid, end to end, in the order the Quick Start gives. One pass produces two certifications:
    • the refactored app does the job — which L1/L2/L3 cannot prove. L3 certifies a member; a process combines many members across many seams, and nothing below L4 exercises the combination.
    • the Quick Start is correct — if a step does not match the app, one of the two is wrong, and the walkthrough tells you which. The manual is tested by being used as the script.
    WHY IT IS NOT REDUNDANT WITH L1–L3. Fred, 11-13: "the purpose of the application walk through would be more likely to surface these issues" — the issues being code still in line that never executes. An L3 cert compares OLD against NEW for a member; it is blind to a process that no longer reaches that member at all. Only the walkthrough sees that. L4 is therefore where M1 Q3 (orphaned callers) gets answered at the process level, not just at the switch.

    ⭐ THE SCRIPT (measured 11-13 17:20). GroupTrack_QuickStart_2.7f_2026-11-11_v99.html — 10 tasks, 38 numbered steps, 45 links into the User Manual. Those manual links are the map from task → screen → the seams that step exercises, so the walkthrough is traceable back to the L2/L3 work without inventing a new index.
    #taskstepsat the 2.8a gate
    1First start5bench — and it must be a FRESH INSTALL ("start over", 17:22)
    2Set up the map4bench
    3Plan a route4bench
    4Get the maps before you lose signal4bench
    5Create a ride and send it3bench
    6Get a ride someone sent you3bench
    7Ride on your own4bench
    8Ride as a group4⚠ CANNOT BE WALKED IN NEW HAMPSHIRE — DEFERS TO JANUARY
    9After the ride3bench
    10Something wrong?4bench
    ⚠ THE TASK 8 CONSTRAINT, STATED NOW SO IT IS NOT DISCOVERED IN JANUARY. Group riding needs real distance between carts. There are no ATVs in New Hampshire and no way to space carts apart until Utah in January. Therefore:
    • the 2.8a lab gate is tasks 1–7, 9, 10 — 34 of the 38 steps, walked on the bench;
    • task 8 (4 steps) defers to the January field test — the same constraint that already defers G8. One trip closes both, so plan them as one trip, not two;
    • task 8 stays OPEN with its reason recorded. It is never marked complete by reasoning about it — that is exactly the "nominal pass" this project has already rejected once today (11-13 16:58).
    ORDER RULE. The Quick Start's order is the walkthrough order — do not reorder it for convenience, because the order is itself part of what is being certified. Task 1 being a fresh install matters beyond tidiness: a fresh install and a converted install take different startup paths, and what a fresh install actually needs is the evidence that answers CODE RULE 3 on G1h (GroupTrackConversion).
    IF THE MANUAL IS WRONG, it gets a NEW file (…_v100) under the GOLDEN RULE — never a re-issue of v99 — and the defect is recorded with the step number that found it.

    ⭐⭐⭐ L4 RUNS SIDE BY SIDE AGAINST 2.7e ON THE SAME DATA — Fred, 11-14 12:51/12:52 (L4SIDEBYSIDE-2026-11-14)
    "I think when we get to the testing of the 2.8 droid version we will leave droid 2 on the old version. copy the full data from droid 1 to droid 2 and test functions a-z side by side."

    ⚠ THIS CLOSES THE ONE REAL HOLE IN THE CERTIFICATION ARGUMENT. Everything below L4 has an oracle: the OLD side. L4 did not — the method was "a person reads the screen and judges whether it looks right," and judgement is exactly what fails on a screen you have seen a thousand times. Droid 2 running 2.7e on Droid 1's data IS the oracle. It is the same OLD-vs-NEW shape as the harness, one level up: same inputs (the same corpus), two implementations, compare the outputs — except the output is a screen and the comparison is visual. A step does not pass because it looks right; it passes because the two devices agree.
    Droid 1 — 8624SBCEDF00001789Droid 2 — 24039703201775
    build2.8a (the ported app)2.7e update f — ⛔ DO NOT DEPLOY 2.8 TO IT. Its value is entirely that it is the old code.
    dataits own real corpus (the protected one)a copy of Droid 1's corpus, taken before 2.8 ever launches
    roleunder testthe oracle

    ⭐ THE ORDER IS THE WHOLE TRICK, AND IT IS EASY TO GET WRONG:

    1. SNAPSHOT DROID 1 FIRST, WHILE IT IS STILL ON 2.7e. 2.8 writes state files on first launch — MapStateStore alone writes *_panel.json and *_view.json on the first panel interaction. Once 2.8 has run, the "old" corpus no longer exists and Droid 2 would be seeded with a 2.8-written one, which makes the comparison circular and silently useless.
    2. Push the snapshot to Droid 2 (still 2.7e), and confirm 2.7e reads it — a corpus 2.7e cannot open is not an oracle.
    3. Then install 2.8a on Droid 1 and walk the Quick Start in order on both, step by step, screens next to each other.

    ⭐ SECOND BENEFIT, UNASKED FOR BUT REAL: step 1 is the first backup Droid 1's protected data has ever had. Up to now "protect its data" has been a rule enforced by not touching it. A snapshot turns it into a restore point — which also means 2.8a can be tested on Droid 1 at all, which the no-touch rule arguably forbade.

    ⚠ ONE THING TO CONFIRM BEFORE TEST DAY, NOT ON TEST DAY: which data is reachable over adb. The GroupTrack folder on external storage pulls fine. Anything in app-private storage (/data/data/com.grouptrack.android/ — SharedPreferences, any private SQLite) does not, without root. If part of the corpus lives app-private, that part will not copy and the two devices would be compared on partially different data without anyone knowing — which is worse than no oracle, because it manufactures DIFFs that look like port regressions. Resolve GtImportPaths.groupTrackRoot() to a real path and enumerate what sits outside it. Cheap now; expensive to discover mid-walkthrough.

    Also confirm the applicationId matches on both sides. If 2.7e update f and 2.8a share com.grouptrack.android, the data paths are identical and the copy is a straight transfer. If they differ, every path in the snapshot needs rewriting. Both devices are sideload-only, no Play Store, so there is no signature-collision hazard in any of this.

    WHAT A DIFF MEANS HERE. By the governing frame, a port regression is excluded by construction — every service was certified identical. So a side-by-side DIFF is one of exactly three things, and saying which is the finding: (a) a seam that was never certified and got missed — the only outcome that reopens a certification; (b) a wiring defect, where the switch resolved but the caller now passes different arguments — the download-estimate shape; (c) the two corpora are not actually identical — which is why the paragraph above matters. Check (c) before believing (a).


    WHERE L4 SITS: after the blast analysis (V1) and after the 2.8a build. It is the last gate before the build may be called a testable lab.

    ⭐⭐⭐ THE GATE ORDERING — NO iPAD BUILD UNTIL ANDROID IS CERTIFIED AS A WORKING APP. (IPADGATE-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 17:24: "we cannot proceed with a iPad build until we certify that all functions correctly still on the android. that all our methods continue to support a fully functioning app."
    THE ORDER IS NOW FIXED:
      L1/L2/L3 per item  →  the switches  →  V1 blast analysis  →  2.8a Droid build  →  L4 process walkthrough  →  ⛔ THE GATE  →  the first iPad generation.
    WHY THE ORDER IS THIS WAY, AND NOT NEGOTIABLE. The shared module is the same code on both platforms. An uncertified Android app does not merely risk Android — it means we do not know what the shared methods do, and an iPad built on top of that inherits a defect we cannot localise, with a second platform's own differences layered over it. Certifying Android first is what makes an iPad failure attributable: anything that then breaks on iPad is the iPad's platform half, because the shared half is already known good.
    WHAT THE GATE BLOCKS: the first iPad generation, and anything built on it.
    WHAT IT DOES NOT BLOCK: the C-list removals. Those still land after the first iPad generation, exactly as decided at 15:13 ("I prefer to skip if it is no called an place the clean after the initial iPad gen"). The two decisions do not collide, and the reason is Fred's own theorem: code that does not execute returns the same result under the new code, so ~2,344 lines of unreachable Android code sitting in the 2.8a build cannot affect what the L4 walkthrough observes. Dead code does not fail a walkthrough. Missing code does.

    ⛔ THE DECISION THIS GATE FORCES — OPEN, FOR FRED, AND IT MOVES THE iPAD DATE.
    "All functions correctly" and task 8 (Ride as a group) cannot both be satisfied before January: group riding needs real distance between carts, and there are no ATVs in New Hampshire. So the gate resolves one of two ways, and they are weeks apart:
    optionwhat it meanscost
    A — strictTask 8 is part of "all functions correctly". The gate does not open until the January Utah field test passes.The first iPad generation waits until January. Nothing is taken on faith. G8 and task 8 close on the same trip.
    B — carved outTasks 1–7, 9, 10 (34 of 38 steps) open the gate. Task 8 is recorded OPEN with its reason and closes in January as a field item against both the Droid and the iPad.iPad generation can start weeks earlier. The risk accepted is explicit and bounded: group tracking is uncertified on either platform until January, and that is the feature the app is named for.
    Not recorded as decided. Claude's read, offered and not acted on: B, because the shared-module risk task 8 would catch is the mesh/transport path, which is G8 — already deferred to January for the identical reason, so A pays a month to re-learn what G8's deferral already tells us. But the choice is Fred's, and until he states it the gate is treated as A (strict), because that is the reading that cannot produce a false pass.

    ⭐⭐⭐ RESEQUENCED — THE PRUNE MOVES BEFORE THE iPAD BUILD. This SUPERSEDES the 15:13 decision. (RESEQ-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 17:27: "in terms of sequencing I think our discovery and task list lends itself [t]o another change we build and decertify the droid. then we work the c list down and prune and clean the droid code base continually rectifying. then we progre[ss] to the iPad build."
    ⚠ WHAT THIS REVERSES. At 15:13 Fred decided: "I prefer to skip if it is no called an place the clean after the initial iPad gen." The 17:27 sequence moves the cleanup before the iPad generation instead. 17:27 governs. The 15:13 decision stands only in its other half, which is unchanged and still correct: SKIP at port time is free; REMOVE is a separate, later act (see M2).
    ⚠ CLAUDE'S FIRST RATIONALE FOR THIS ORDER WAS WRONG, AND IS STRUCK. (RESEQFIX2-2026-11-13)
    Claude argued the order was driven by asymmetric certification cost — that pruning after the iPad generation would make each removal a "two-platform change re-certified twice". Fred, 11-13 17:35: "I disagree with your assessment and soon as we gen a droid version and certify 2.8 version for release we are back to one codebase for android and Mac. removing code remove from both implementations. I just do not like ginning and correcting code that we do not know if it is even used."
    Fred is right, and the measurement makes it worse for the original argument:
    • One codebase. Once 2.8 is certified for release, Android and Mac share grouptrack-shared. A removal there is ONE edit that removes from both implementations — not a change applied twice. That is a property of the architecture working, not a cost.
    • And this pile is not even shared code. Measured 11-13 17:36: every prune-pile file lives under app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/, and they are two different kinds — do not read the list as "delete these files" (PRUNEPRECISION-2026-11-13):
        WHOLE-FILE deletions (1,047 lines): ConvoySourceClear 317 · ConvoyCorridorDelete 234 · ConvoySourceMigration 228 · ConvoyCorridorWorker 202 · TileCodec 66.
        IN-FILE removals only — the file STAYS (1,297 lines): ConvoyMapSourceScreen's dead branch 877 (the file is the live map-source credits screen) · ConvoyTileDownloader's corridorTiles+stampPoint 147 · the raster arms 138 · GtRemoveTrackScreen 112 · the estimateSizeMB chain 12 · ConvoyMarkerRenderer ~10 · MBTilesStore:81's metadata write — ONE line. MBTilesStore itself is G1f, a PORT target, not a deletion. Zero prune candidates are in grouptrack-shared (the only matches there are header prose reading "NOT PORTED"). Because they are SKIPPED, they are never ported, so the iPad never receives them at any point — removal timing has no bearing on the iPad, not "half the cost".
    ⭐ THE ACTUAL REASON FOR THE ORDER — Fred's, and it is a better one. Do not gin and correct code we do not know is even used. The ordering is not about what a removal costs; removal is cheap whenever it happens. It is about not spending porting, fixing and certifying effort on code of unknown utility. That is the same principle as M3, the pre-port eligibility gate and "port AS IS — but only what is REACHABLE": the expensive mistake is working on dead code, not deleting it late.
    WHAT THIS CHANGES IN THE PLAN: nothing in the sequence — P1→P4 stands as Fred set it at 17:27. What it changes is what must be front-loaded. Since removal is cheap at any time, the thing that genuinely has to come early is the knowledge of whether code is used — which is the instrumented walkthrough in P2 below, not the deletions in P3. Front-load the determination; the removals can follow at any convenient point.

    ⭐⭐ THE MIRRORED-TYPE ADAPTER PATTERN — THREE INSTANCES, AND IT IS NOT A DEFECT.
    Pass 1 keeps meeting the same situation: a shared data class that mirrors an Android one field for field, because the port deliberately avoided blocking on a type that had not moved yet.
    shared typeAndroid typefieldsresolution
    GtMapSpec.GtTileLayerSpecMapSourceManager.TileLayeridentical, same order: role, urlTemplate, tileFormat, cacheDir, renderOrder = 0one-line .map { } adapter now, deleted in G1e when TileLayer itself moves. CODE RULE 3 shaped: temporary, removed when its successor lands. Alternative: defer both downloadSources to G1e.
    GtTileMath.GtDownloadEstimateDownloadEstimateidentical: tileCount, estimatedMB, withinCeiling, zoomMin, zoomMax✅ no adapter needed — see the C-list find below. The used surfaces are the same.
    GtMapSpecEnv(none yet)—write GtMapSpecAndroid, as above
    The general rule this establishes: a shared type carries data; the Android type may add platform formatting that cannot exist in commonMain ("%,d" grouping and String.format are JVM, not Kotlin common). Where the Android type adds only formatting, keep the Android type as a thin wrapper and map into it. Where it adds nothing, delete it and let callers take the shared type.

    ⭐ NEW C-LIST LINES FOUND BY THE MAPPING — Pass 1 doing its job.
    A THIRD DEAD CHAIN, same shape as 11-13's estimateSizeMB → isWithinCeiling → AVG_TILE_KB: on DownloadEstimate (ConvoyTileCalculator:361–363), displaySize, displayCount and summary have zero callers anywhere in live app code — and summary's only inputs are the other two, so the three fall together. 3 lines → C list. Their absence is also what makes GtDownloadEstimate a drop-in: the two types have identical used surfaces.
    Also to the C list from this mapping: bearingDeg, angleDiff (RiderTrailScanner, confirmed dead above) and the four ConvoyTileCalculator non-ports already listed.

    ⭐⭐⭐ THE DOWNLOAD-ESTIMATE DEFECT — and the first live proof that CERTIFICATION PROVES SAMENESS, NOT CORRECTNESS. (ESTBUG-2026-11-14)
    Fred, 11-14 08:27: "and that calc is wrong it clcs in sat tiles if it has a toast message."
    He is right, and the path holds three defects. Found while mapping the G1a switch — which is Pass 1 earning its place.
    #defectwhereeffect
    1AVG_TILE_KB = 15f is a SATELLITE-RASTER constant, used to turn a tile count into MB.ConvoyTileCalculator:28 → :321; mirrored in GtTileMath:27 → :48The app now downloads only VEC (pbf vector) and DEM (terrarium png). Neither is 15 KB. Every MB figure shown to a rider, and the withinCeiling verdict that gates the "Area Too Large" dialog, are computed for maps the app no longer has.
    2The dialog text is off by 10×.GroupTrackScreen:673It reads "exceeds the 500 MB limit" while CEILING_MB = 5000f. Independent of defect 1 and visible to a rider.
    3This port put a WRONG COMMENT in the code — now corrected.GtTileCalc.kt headerIt claimed "AVG_TILE_KB's only use was estimateSizeMB. A dead chain ports as nothing." AVG_TILE_KB has TWO uses: estimateSizeMB (dead) and quickEstimate:321 (live, 8 callers). The analysis followed estimateSizeMB → isWithinCeiling, found it dead, and never asked whether the CONSTANT had another consumer. estimateSizeMB and isWithinCeiling are genuinely dead; AVG_TILE_KB and CEILING_MB are live.

    ⛔⛔ WE CERTIFIED THE BUG, AND THE CERTIFICATION WAS NOT AT FAULT. GtTileMath.quickEstimate was ported AS IS under step 16b and certified at 0 DIFF on 1,938,346 values. It carries the identical AVG_TILE_KB = 15f and the identical sizeMB line. OLD == NEW, and both are wrong by the same 15 KB.
    • The cert did exactly its job — it proved the port faithful. Porting AS IS is still correct, and this is not an argument against the method.
    • But nothing in the L1–L4 ladder can catch a wrong constant, because a wrong constant is identical on both sides. L2 compares platforms; L3 compares OLD to NEW; neither compares either side to reality.
    • What caught it was Fred looking at a toast. That is his 11-13 17:22 point landing in practice — "the purpose of the application walk through would be more likely to surface these issues" — and this dialog sits in L4 walkthrough Task 4, "Get the maps before you lose signal."
    • ⭐ THE DURABLE RULE: certification answers "does it behave the same?"; it never answers "is it right?" A faithful port of wrong code is still wrong code. The only gates that test correctness are L4 and a human reading the screen. Budget for that explicitly — do not let a wall of 0-DIFF results imply the app is correct.

    ⭐ HOW THE FIX IS SHAPED — deliberately NOT part of Pass 1.
    • ⛔ Do NOT change the constant inside the port. It would invalidate the step-16b certification and break port-AS-IS. Pass 1 switches behaviour-for-behaviour; a correctness change must not ride along inside it.
    • It needs a MEASUREMENT, not another guess. No code anywhere in the repo knows a real tile size — AVG_TILE_KB = 15f is the only source, and the tiles+duration yardstick designed 07-25 was never built. The stores live on device, so the number comes from a device query on VEC.mbtiles and DEM.mbtiles: SELECT COUNT(*), AVG(LENGTH(tile_data)), MAX(LENGTH(tile_data)) FROM tiles; — and VEC and DEM need separate figures, since pbf and terrarium png are nothing alike.
    • Then one deliberate change, after the switch: per-slot sizes replacing the single constant, the 500/5000 string corrected (a display string, not under certification, so it can ride along), and the cert re-run with the new expected values — a behaviour change, so it needs a new golden run, not a waiver.
    • ⛔⛔ A FOURTH AND DOMINANT DEFECT, AND IT RESHAPES THE FIX. (ESTZOOM-2026-11-14)
      Fred, 11-14 08:32: "also the multiplier for the number of tiles [is] based on raster z3-z18 tile counts."
      The TILE COUNT is wrong, not just the MB multiplier — and the count error dwarfs the KB error. quickEstimate's defaults are zMin = ConvoyConfig.DOWNLOAD_ZOOM_MIN (4) and zMax = ConvoyConfig.DOWNLOAD_ZOOM (18) — the raster download range. But the app downloads only VEC (Z_MAX = 14, OpenFreeMap ends there) and DEM (Z_MAX = 12). Tile count quadruples per zoom level, so summing to z18 adds four phantom levels for VEC and six for DEM.
      areaSHOWN to the rider (z4–z18)ACTUALLY downloaded (VEC z0–14 + DEM z0–12)over-count
      small ride, ~15 km27,378 tiles186147×
      medium, ~60 km271,735 tiles / 3,980 MB1,287211×
      large trip, ~150 km2,276,778 tiles9,820232×
      ⛔ THIS IS USER-FACING AND IT BLOCKS THE APP'S CORE JOB. The "Area Too Large" gate trips at 341,333 tiles (5000 MB ÷ 15 KB), so a 150 km trip is REFUSED OUTRIGHT while the real job is under 10,000 tiles, and a rider is told a 60 km area costs 4 GB. ⚠ SEVERITY CORRECTED — Claude called this a "2.8a release blocker". IT IS NOT. (ESTSEV-2026-11-14)
      Fred, 11-14 08:38: "the key info in the d[ow]nl[oa]d is th[e] estimate of how long to download. that is performed in the queue manager using avg tiles per sec for the runni[ng] job. ev[e]ryth[i]n[g] else is just informational. tiles coun[t]s are an estimation. neighborhood number. it is firmed as the queue is loaded."
      Verified 11-14 08:39, and he is right:
      • The ETA is already correct and this defect cannot touch it. tilesPerSec (ConvoyDownloadQueue:1187) is measured — downloaded / elapsed seconds on completed entries, real throughput, no estimate involved. Its tile total comes from queue entries, which are built through GtVectorQueue.enqueueArea and therefore already use the correct per-slot zMin = 0, zMax = Z_MAX. The preview error never reaches the number the rider acts on.
      • The tile count and MB in the confirm dialog are informational — a neighbourhood number, firmed as the queue is loaded. An inaccurate preview there is untidy, not harmful.
      • ⛔ ONE functional consequence survives: the withinCeiling branch is a HARD REFUSAL. GroupTrackScreen:667 renders "Area Too Large" with a single OK button that calls clearPendingDownload() — no way through — and it fires before a queue exists, so the rider never reaches the point where the count would firm up. On the inflated figure, areas past roughly 150 km are refused outright, against a limit the dialog states as 500 MB while the code enforces 5000. That is a gate, not a display.
      ⭐ THE CORRECTED PRIORITY: a defect worth scheduling, not one that stops 2.8a. The failure mode is bad — a hard refusal computed from a wrong number — but it is bounded to large-area downloads and it has an obvious workaround (select less). Fix it with the areaTiles call-site work, not ahead of Pass 1.
      ⭐ AND THE LESSON ON CLAUDE'S SIDE: the magnitude (~200×) was measured correctly; the severity was asserted without checking which number the rider actually acts on. A big multiplier on an informational figure is not a big defect. Before calling anything a blocker, trace it to the number the user makes a decision with — here that was the ETA, and tracing it took two greps.

      ⭐⭐ AND IT IS CALLER-SIDE — WHICH SUPERSEDES WHAT THIS DOC SAID MINUTES EARLIER. The ESTBUG entry above said the fix must wait until after the switch because changing it would invalidate the step-16b certification. That was wrong, and is struck. The bug is not in the ported function:
      • GtTileMath.quickEstimate is faithful AND correct — it computes exactly the range it is asked for. It stays untouched, and its certification is not in question.
      • The defect is in four Android call sites that pass NO zoom arguments and therefore inherit the raster defaults: MapScreens:51, GroupTrackScreen:913, :1295, :2459. The queue callers are already right — ConvoyDownloadQueue:372 and :427 pass zMin = 0, zMax = vecZ. So the preview and the actual job have been disagreeing by ~200×, with the correct number on the queue side all along.
      • ⚠ Two more callers take the defaults and need checking: ConvoyDownloadQueue:373 (the non-vec arm — likely dead in a vector-only app, so confirm against Q4) and :581, which passes no zooms at all.
      • Android call sites are NOT under certification, so this can be fixed now, independently of Pass 1, with every cert intact.
      THE FIX SHAPE: each UI site asks for the two slots it actually downloads — VEC z0..14 and DEM z0..12 — and sums them. The MB figure is then computed in the Android layer from per-slot measured sizes, not from the single AVG_TILE_KB inside the shared function. That is the same division this morning's adapter finding established: the shared type carries DATA, the Android type carries DISPLAY. It keeps the port clean and means AVG_TILE_KB never has to change at all — so the KB constant stops being a blocker, and the tile COUNTS become correct from the caller fix alone. Only the honest MB figure still waits on a device measurement.
      ⭐ THE SECOND DURABLE LESSON, ON TOP OF "SAMENESS IS NOT CORRECTNESS": a faithful port can be correct while its CALLERS are wrong, and a certification says nothing about what arguments the app passes. L3 compares OLD against NEW for the same inputs. Nothing in the ladder asks whether the inputs the app supplies are the right ones. Check the call sites' ARGUMENTS, not just their existence — add it to the M1 questions as part of what a switch must verify.

      Blast radius of the fix, measured: 4 display points (GroupTrackScreen:673, :698, MapScreens:63, :2520), 2 logs, 3 ViewModel plumbing sites. Small.
    ⭐ WHY "VERY FAST" IS THE RIGHT CALL — the pattern is proven, not novel. Measured 11-13 18:06: 71 of 357 live app .kt files already import com.grouptrack.shared, and the Android sockets are constructed in real app code, not only in harnesses — ConvoyDownloadQueue, ConvoyMasterConfig, ConvoySessionManager, ConvoySettingsRepository, ConvoyUser, ConvoyEventConfig. There is no central socket registry that must be built first; each call site constructs its own, and that has already been done dozens of times. A switch is therefore: point the call at the shared class and hand it its socket the way the other 71 files do.
    ⚠ THE ONE PART THAT IS NOT FAST — certified is not wired. The cert proves the method; the switch must prove the wiring. Each switch can surface M1 Q3 (orphaned callers): Android code that becomes unreachable the moment the call moves. That is not a certification cost, it is discovery, and its output feeds straight into the C list. Highest orphan risk = the pieces whose references sit in a single file: RouteExplorer (31 refs, 1 file), RiderTrailScanner (12, 1), OsmExtractProgress (2, 1), DbAudit (2, 1), OsmPbfTagReader (1, 1). Switch one of those early to measure what a Q3 cascade actually costs.
    VERIFICATION FOR PASS 1 (not certification): the build compiles · the existing cert suites still pass · the affected L4 walkthrough tasks still walk. A switch that breaks a walkthrough step has found a wiring defect, which is exactly what Pass 1 is for.

    ⭐⭐ THE G1h RETIREMENT — EXACT SCOPE, AND IT IS NOT A FREE DELETION. (G1HRETIRE-2026-11-13)
    Measured 11-13 21:50. Three things must be right or this breaks the app's startup:
    actionfiledetail
    DELETEGroupTrackConversion.kt663 lines. The SAF migration engine: run, moveOutMaps, copyMaps, renameSources, inspectMaps, the record.
    EDITConvoyAuthorityGateScreenV2.kt11 call sites in a 1,047-line file that runs before anything else. Remove the conversion screen branch and the convDone / convBusy / convStep state.
    ⛔ KEEP — DO NOT TOUCHGroupTrackStorage.kt224 lines, 51 live callers. This is the PATH ACCESSOR, a different thing from the conversion engine. "Retiring the storage conversion" could take it out by association and break 51 call sites. It stays.
    ⚠ CLASSIFICATION: VARIANT (c) — IT EXECUTES TODAY. isComplete() runs on every launch at ConvoyAuthorityGateScreenV2:312, inside the remember { mutableStateOf(...) } that decides whether the conversion screen appears. So by the 11-13 17:32 rule this goes to the BACK of the removal queue, not the front — removing it changes what executes, in the highest-blast-radius code in the app. If the gate breaks, the app does not start. Related trap from the 09-12 record: hasRealStorageAccess() must keep returning true, or the gate shows a screen nothing can satisfy.
    WHEN: CODE RULE 3 says we remove one-time launch code when the next release is cut — so this lands with the 2.8a cut, not before it and not long after.
    ITS VERIFICATION IS ALREADY IN THE PLAN: L4 walkthrough Task 1 — First start, 5 steps, which is exactly the authority-gate path. Walk it on a fresh install and on an over-the-top update, because those take different paths through the gate.
    ✅ ALREADY CLEAN: GroupTrackMigration.kt and GroupTrackMigrationScreen.kt, which the 09-12 record still listed as orphaned and awaiting deletion, are already gone. That note was stale.

    ⭐ THE PHASES, AS RESEQUENCED.
    phasewhat happenscertification that closes it
    P1Finish the remaining L1/L2/L3 work — G1d, G1e, G1f, and G1h only if CODE RULE 3 keeps it. Then the switches (G1a and G1c are still owed), then V1 blast analysis.per item: 0 DIFF both devices, twice
    P2Build 2.8a Droid and treat the whole app as uncertified — the refactored app is a new app; nothing 2.7e earned carries over to it as a process.L4 walkthrough, Quick Start order, 34 of 38 steps on the bench
    P3Work the C list down in keystone order, pruning the Droid codebase, re-certifying after each removal.the affected L4 tasks re-run per removal — not the full L1–L3 set (see the rule below)
    P4The first iPad generation, onto a pruned and certified shared model.its own L2/L3 per seam on the iPad half
    THE P3 RE-CERTIFICATION RULE. Removing unreachable code cannot change an L2 or an L3 result — by Fred's own theorem, code that does not execute returns the same result. So P3 does not re-run the cert suites. What P3 re-runs is the L4 walkthrough, because the walkthrough is the only thing that can catch the one failure mode that matters here: something we judged unreachable turning out to be reachable. A prune that breaks a walkthrough step has proved the reachability analysis wrong, and that is exactly what we want it to be able to prove. Prune in keystone order — ConvoyMapSourceScreen's dead branch is the only caller of three deletable files, so two surgical edits unlock 1,768 of the 2,344 lines (75%) — and walk the affected tasks after each keystone, not after all of them.

    ⭐⭐⭐ P3's INTERNAL ORDER — SORT THE PRUNE QUEUE BY EXECUTION, NOT BY SIZE. (P3EXEC-2026-11-13)
    Fred, 11-13 17:32: "before we remove we will identify the if the process is even executed now. something not executing is easy t remove. leaving th[e] execut[i]ng items to the back of the process."
    This is already the right sort, because the C list is not one kind of thing. The entry test found three variants, and they carry three different removal risks. Sorting by line count or by keystone alone mixes them; sorting by execution separates them:
    ordervariantdoes it execute?what removal costs
    1st(a) no caller — whole files, orphaned membersNo. Nothing reaches it.Nearly free. Deleting code that cannot run cannot change behaviour. These are the "easy to remove" items.
    2nd(b) branch-dead inside a live symbol — Class BThe symbol does; the branch does not.Medium — and the risk is the EDIT, not the code. The branch cannot run, but the scalpel is going into live code, so a mis-aimed cut breaks something that does run.
    3rd / LAST(c) executed but unconsumedYES. It runs on every pass.Highest — removal changes what executes. Fewer writes, less work, different timing. Known members: MBTilesStore.initSchema's metadata writes; ConvoyMapSourceScreen:79/:81's getAllSources / getSlotAssignments. Back of the queue, exactly as Fred says.
    HOW KEYSTONE ORDER AND EXECUTION ORDER RECONCILE — they barely conflict. The keystone edit (ConvoyMapSourceScreen's dead branch) is a variant (b), so strict execution order would put it second. But what it unlocks — three whole files — is variant (a), the free kind. So the real sequence is: prove the keystone branch dead → two surgical edits → delete the three now-orphaned files (free) → the rest of (a) → the rest of (b) → (c) last. One deliberate variant-(b) cut up front buys 75% of the queue as variant (a).
    ⚠ AND ONE ITEM IS NOT YET SORTABLE. MapSourceManager.getSourceByKey has 6 callers, three of them in dead files. Its live callers must be separated from its dead ones before it is classified into any variant. It is not in the queue until that is done.

    ⭐ HOW TO ESTABLISH "IS IT EVEN EXECUTED NOW" — MEASURE IT, DO NOT INFER IT.
    Static analysis is what we have been using, and on 11-13 it was wrong three times in one day (maxZoomForSource, buildTileUrl, corridorTiles — all from greps that excluded the declaring file and hid internal callers). It is necessary but it is not evidence of execution.
    The measured version is available for free, because P2 already runs the walkthrough:
    • Instrument every prune candidate in the 2.8a build — one log line on entry, per candidate. Under CODE RULE 3 this needs no marker and no version check; it comes out when the next release is cut.
    • Run the L4 walkthrough (34 of 38 steps, Quick Start order) with the log capturing.
    • Anything never logged is confirmed not-executed BY OBSERVATION, across every process the app actually performs — not by an index query. Anything that does log is reclassified on the spot, and if it was in the C list the entry test was wrong about it.
    What this buys: P3 starts with measured execution data instead of inferred, the three-wrong-calls failure mode of 11-13 cannot repeat inside the prune, and the log is a durable artifact — the evidence for each removal is a line of captured output, not a paragraph of reasoning. It also catches the opposite error: a candidate that logs during a step we assumed it had nothing to do with.
    ⚠ What it does NOT prove: that a never-logged item is unreachable forever — only that none of the 34 walked steps reached it. Task 8 is not walked until January, so any candidate touched only by group riding stays unproven and must not be removed on walkthrough silence alone. Static evidence plus the January field test closes those; the log closes the rest.

    ⭐ WHAT THIS DOES TO THE TASK-8 / JANUARY QUESTION — it largely dissolves it.
    P3 is real calendar work: 2,344 lines, keystone-ordered, each removal followed by a walkthrough pass. That phase plausibly runs into January on its own. If it does, option A (strict) costs little or nothing, because the January field test lands inside a window the prune was going to occupy anyway. The resequencing is therefore also the cheapest way to buy option A. Do not decide A vs B until P2 is done and P3's real pace is known — the decision gets cheaper by waiting, which is rare enough to be worth saying.

    ⚠ ONE READING TO CONFIRM. "build and decertify the droid" is read here as build 2.8a and treat it as uncertified — the P2 row above. The other possible reading, "build and re-certify", produces the same work in the same order, so nothing downstream depends on which was meant. Flagged rather than silently resolved.

    ⭐ SEAM D — NO SEAM AT ALL. Do this first (it proves the pipe, as G1a did).
    Decision certs (L3): MapSourceManager.TileLayer · setActive · getSlotSources · getDownloadSources · activeSourceKey · MapStateStore's data classes (TypeState, PanelBoxes, BBox, FitArtifact, RouteState, MapSnapshot) · rideKey · checkedIdsFor
    Contract: none. Pure data and in-memory lookups. Cheapest certs in the remaining set.

    ⭐ SEAM A — THE TILE STORE (SQLite). MBTilesStore, 605 lines, 32 SQLite calls, zero Context, zero JSON, zero clock — a pure database layer.
    Decision certs (L3) — these calls, with the seam scripted: insertTile · readTile · hasTile · countInTileRange · tileExtentAtZoom · sourceInfo · tilesInBounds · scanKeys · zoomLevelsPresent · countAtZoom · xyAtZoom · storeExists · countByParent · countTileRange · deleteTileRange · reclaimableBytes · deleteSource · closeAll
    Contract: a GtTileStore socket. ⭐ The pattern already exists — G2f's GtHkDb, where the statement a side DECIDED to run is the compared decision and whether SQLite would honour it is a different certification. So the SQL text and the key arithmetic port and get certified; execution stays platform. iOS brings its own SQLite binding.
    ⚠ Certify on COPIES, never the live stores (the inventory's own warning for this piece).

    ⭐ SEAM B — FILE-BACKED JSON STATE + CLOCK. Three files, one seam design.
    Decision certs (L3) — these calls, with the seam scripted: MapStateStore.saveMap · readMap · deleteMap · saveView · readView — and ConvoyMapRecipe.writeDailyAsync · writeFresh · CONVERSION — and MapSourceManager.init · getOverlayJson
    Contract: file read/write + JSON + a clock. ⭐ Mostly exists: readAsset (G1c's GtMapSpecEnv, and GtImportEnv:81), okio FileSystem, GtOrgJson (G2g). ⛔ THE CLOCK IS THE ONE TO DESIGN — ConvoyMapRecipe has a date in its filename and today() in its logic, and G2f's RULE 1 is that now() must be SCRIPTED on both sides or a run that straddles midnight flips a decision.

    ⭐ SEAM C — SAF / STORAGE CONVERSION. GroupTrackConversion, 663 lines, 26 DocumentFile/Uri/ContentResolver hits, 15 Context, 11 clock — the heaviest Android tie in G1, and iOS has no equivalent file model.
    Decision certs (L3) — these calls, with the seam scripted: isComplete · isFreshInstall · recordFreshInstall · readRecord · inspectMaps · moveOutMaps · copyMaps · renameSources
    Contract: a new storage socket. Only the record FORMAT, the completion logic and the exclusion rules port; the file movement is platform.
    ⛔ DO NOT START SEAM C UNTIL THE CODE RULE 3 QUESTION IS ANSWERED — this is the one-time SAF conversion. If every install has converted, 664 lines come out rather than go over, and the whole seam is unnecessary. That is a release decision for Fred and it is the cheapest possible win on the remaining list.

    ⛔⛔ THE GATE — what must be complete before 2.8a is built and deployed as a testable lab
    Fred's estimate, 17:04: "a day or two of seam work certification." On the evidence that is the right order of magnitude for G1, and it is contingent on three things:
      1. Seams D → B → A certified (D proves the pipe, B shares one design across three files, A is the socket). Seam C only if CODE RULE 3 says the conversion stays.
      2. The two outstanding SWITCHES — G1a and G1c are certified but NOT switched; the Android callers still use ConvoyTileCalculator, VectorTiles and ElevationTiles. The switch is what answers M1 Q3.
      3. V1, the blast analysis — the LAST step before the 2.8a build, over the pre-11-13 backlog (see #blast-check).
    ⚠ And it is G1 only. G3–G7 are 7,767 lines that have never been reachability-tested by the same criterion that put two dead pieces inside G1. Run M3 over each before porting. The "day or two" is the gate for G1's seams, not for step 17.
